**What 22.3 is (your Tue Nov 3 19:49 message; CUMULATIVE on 15 to 22.2; untested in game).**
- **Your message, recorded (Tue Nov 3 19:49 local):** "ok so, im still getting set on fire, while in the seat, dont set players in chairs on fire"
- **WHAT I FOUND (by reading every place our code can light a person; I cannot see which one burned you).** 22.2 made a chair never burn and spared a seated person ONLY when `Heat.SeatOf` found his chair: a player in a vehicle seat that ACF can damage (`ACF.Check` true) and that no safezone protects. In every other case he was an ordinary person and the flame (`TouchPerson`) or the lazer (`DamagePerson`) lit him: a chair ACF does not treat as damageable, a chair a safezone or a permission refuses, a "vehicle" that is not a plain seat entity. Nothing at all stopped fire that reaches him by itself (a prop we lit that burns beside him, or a fire addon reacting to it), and the hot-lazer sweep could light him if `HeatIgniteSeated` was true.
- **THE RULE (hard, covers every path of ours).** `Heat.CanIgnite(ent)` is now false for a chair AND for a PLAYER who is in a vehicle (`Player:InVehicle()`), while `SeatNoIgnite` is on (it is, unless the config says `false`). It does not look at what the chair is, whether ACF can hurt it, a safezone or a permission. It is asked by the lazer's `IgniteThrottled` (the person path and the prop path) and by the flame's `IgniteThrottled` (`TouchPerson`, `TouchPart`). The hot-lazer sweep: a seated person is lit only if `HeatIgniteSeated` is true AND `SeatNoIgnite` is false (before: `HeatIgniteSeated` alone; your config has it false, so nothing changes for you). He still takes the damage where 22.2 let him take it (a chair ACF cannot damage or a safezone one: the flame and the beam hit the person), but he does not burn. **`SeatSparePlayer` off does not light him either (that switch is about damage).** Out of the vehicle he burns as always.
- **THE SAFETY NET (for fire that reaches him some other way).** Every `Entity:Ignite` of ours now calls `Heat.NoteFire(source, entity, seconds)` (the lazer, the flamethrower, the hot-lazer sweep): it keeps the time until which a fire of ours is alive (that fire's seconds plus `SeatFireGrace`, 10 s) and what lit last. A timer every 0.2 s (`ACFLazers_SeatFire`, `Heat.SeatFireSweep`) does nothing at all (one number compared) when no fire of ours is alive. While one is, every player who is in a vehicle AND on fire is put out (`Extinguish`) and the server console says it, at most one line per person per five seconds:
  `[acf_lazers_heat] put out <name> (burning in a vehicle); the last fire of ours: <lazer / flamethrower / hot lazer> on <entity>, <n> s ago`
  **This line is how we find out what burned you if it still happens.** It also puts out a person who climbs into a vehicle while he burns from our fire. **Honest cost:** while a fire of ours is alive, ANY fire on a seated person is put out, also one from another addon; with no fire of ours alive nothing is touched (so a fire that is not ours and starts when none of ours burns anywhere is left alone). The fire can show for up to 0.2 s before it is put out.
- **THE OPTIONAL SWITCHES (a config that does not name them means ON / the default; only an explicit `false` turns a switch off).** `SeatNoIgnite` (true: the rule above and the net), `SeatSparePlayer` (true: the damage rules of 22.2), `SeatPutOut` (true: the net; `false` keeps the rule and drops the net), `SeatFireGrace` (10: seconds the net keeps working after the last fire of ours has burned out; 0 .. 3600). None is in your config yet; the next config I send has them.
- **WHAT 22.3 DOES NOT DO.** An NPC in a vehicle is not covered (players only). A person who is on fire when NO fire of ours is alive anywhere is left alone. Fire from another addon that starts while a fire of ours burns is put out too (see the cost). The PPC ammo type is not changed (it is the 22.2 file). If you are still burned with 22.3 and the console shows NO "put out" line, the fire is not ours and not from a fire of ours alive (V99).
- **THE FILES (THREE code files and this document; NO config).** `acf_lazers_heat.new.lua` (the rule in `Heat.CanIgnite`, `Heat.NoteFire`, `Heat.SeatFireSweep` and its timer, the hot-lazer sweep respects `SeatNoIgnite` and notes its fires), `acf_lazer_init.new.lua` (version 22.3: notes the fires it lights), `lazers_flame.new.lua` (notes the fires it lights). The PPC ammo type, the config and every other file are as before.
- **STATUS: UNTESTED IN GAME.** Offline (my harness): 11,532 checks in 18 suites, all pass (22.2: 11,458). New or changed: `t_heat` 766 (was 739; H23: `CanIgnite` for a person in a vehicle in every combination of the switches, an NPC, nil and an entity with no player methods; `NoteFire` with junk, negative, NaN and huge seconds and grace), `t_ignite` 140 (was 113; new section I8: the net with no fire alive, with one, who is touched and who is not, the end of the time to the millisecond, the console line and its rate limit, the switches, the timer, a person lit by a hot lazer who then climbs in; I7: `HeatIgniteSeated` true no longer lights a seated person unless `SeatNoIgnite` is off), `t_pen` 122 (was 112; the lazer beam on a person in a vehicle in every case 22.2 left open, plus the fire it notes), `t_flame` 845 (was 835; the same for the flame: a chair ACF does not know, a safezone chair, a permission-refused chair, a plain 'vehicle', the switches, the fire it notes). The new checks FAIL on the 22.2 files. **Mutation test:** 64 hand-made breaks of the 22.3 changes (`mapbuild/mut223.py`: every term of the new `CanIgnite` line, every line of `NoteFire` and `SeatFireSweep`, the timer, the hot-lazer sweep, the three call sites and the old guards behind them): 57 were caught by the first tests and 7 survived (the `IsValid` and `IsPlayer` guards in `CanIgnite`, the `IsValid` in the net, the 'seconds ago' number in the console line, and the seconds passed by the three call sites); I added a check for each and all 7 are caught now (64 of 64, no equivalent left). The older mutant sets were not re-run; their suites all pass.
- **WHAT THE HARNESS CANNOT KNOW (V99):** which of the paths burned you (hence the console line); whether `Player:InVehicle()` is true for YOUR seat the moment the flame arrives; that `Entity:Extinguish()` takes the fire effect off a player at once; what a fire addon does when its fire is put out (it may light him again every 0.2 s; the line would then repeat every five seconds).

  The four flame numbers (lifetime, the two speeds, the drag) are what "seems to work best for flame throwers" in your 21:21 message; I answered with the ranges they give and asked whether to make them the shipped defaults (V95, not answered yet). Nothing is changed in the tree for them.
- **THE CAUSE OF "I AM KILLED FROM INSIDE THE VEHICLE" (read in ACF's own code, `lua/acf/damage/damage_sv.lua` of the ACF I have in `acf_src`).** Every hit on a vehicle seat goes to `Damage.doVehicleDamage` (lines 204 - 215): it FIRST deals the same damage to the DRIVER (`Damage.dealDamage(Driver, ...)`, a person, "squishy", with ACF's damage to people, which is many times a prop's) and only THEN damages the seat "like a regular prop" (`Damage.doPropDamage`, line 222). `Damage.dealDamage` (lines 263 - 293) looks at the hook `ACF_OnDamageEntity` and then at the entity's own `ACF_OnDamage` method before it picks `doVehicleDamage`. So EVERY heat tick, lazer slice or blast on a chair that ACF treats as a vehicle also hits the person in it as if he were hit directly, and he dies long before the chair does. The flamethrower also hit the sitting person directly (he is a person to its sweep), and the chair could be set on fire.
- **WHAT 22.2 DOES (no ACF file is changed or replaced).**
  1. **A chair (anything with `Entity:IsVehicle()`, not a player) is NEVER set on fire by us.** `Heat.CanIgnite(ent)` is false for a chair: the lazer beam's fire step, the lazer's `IgniteThrottled`, the flamethrower projectile's `IgniteThrottled` and its `Heat.SetFire` for a part all ask it. Everything else burns as before. (Every `Ignite` in our code is one of these or the heat file's fire on PEOPLE, so nothing of ours lights a chair.)
  2. **The damage our weapons do to a chair is the chair's alone.** Our `DamageInfo`s carry a mark (`ACFLazersSpareDriver = true`; made by `Heat.MarkSpare`): the damage `Heat.Hurt` deals (the flame's damage to a part and our parts' overheating damage), the lazer beam on a prop (`DamageProp`), the PPC blast's info. When ACF is about to damage a chair with a marked hit, our hook `ACF_OnDamageEntity` ("ACFLazers_SpareSeatDriver") puts ONE method `ACF_OnDamage` on that chair (only if it has none of its own, so another addon's method is never replaced); the method sends a MARKED hit to ACF's `Damage.doPropDamage` (the chair only) and an UNMARKED hit (an ordinary ACF shell, another addon) to `Damage.doVehicleDamage` exactly as ACF does. Nothing of ACF is patched.
  3. **A person SITTING in a chair that ACF can hurt is spared by the lazer beam and by the flamethrower; the chair takes his place** (`Heat.SeatOf(player)`: he is in a seat, `ACF.Check(seat)` is true, and the chair is not protected by a safezone). The beam: the chair is damaged ONCE per beam, at the strength the beam has at that point (a copy of the beam's state, so a plate it burned through before him still counts), and he takes no damage, no fire and no disintegration; the beam goes on through him as it always does. The flamethrower: the chair is touched in his place (once per projectile) and, like any part, it ENDS the projectile after the 0.05 s fuze (so the flame stops at the chair instead of burning through it into him). **When the chair is gone (destroyed, or he gets out), he is a normal person again** and a flame or beam hits him and sets him on fire: that is what you asked for ("the vehicle melts, then the player pops out and gets set on fire").
  | Source | Empty chair | Person in the chair |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Lazer beam | damaged like a prop (heat and ACF health), never lit | the chair takes the beam in his place (once per beam); he takes nothing |
  | Flamethrower | a part like any other: heat + ACF health, ends the projectile, never lit | the chair is touched in his place and ends the projectile; he is not hit, not lit |
  | ACF health taken through `Heat.Hurt` (the flame's damage to a part, the overheating damage of our lazers, PPCs and capacitors) | the chair loses ACF health | the chair only, the driver takes nothing from it |
  | PPC blast | the blast's hit on the chair is marked: the chair alone | the chair's hit is not handed on to him |
  | Anything not ours (an ACF shell, another addon, vFire) | unchanged | unchanged: ACF's own rule (the driver is hit too) |
- **THE TWO SWITCHES (OPTIONAL; a config that does not name them means ON; only an explicit `false` turns one off).** `SeatNoIgnite` (true): rule 1. `SeatSparePlayer` (true): rules 2 and 3. They are NOT in your config yet; the next config I send has both lines.
- **WHAT IS NOT COVERED (read before you test).**
  - A person standing INSIDE the PPC blast sphere is a target of the blast himself (ACF's explosion goes through every valid entity in the sphere and checks what is in the way); 22.2 only stops the CHAIR's hit from being handed on to him (V97).
  - The capacitor's and the fusion engine's explosions, and any other addon's damage, are not marked (V97).
  - A person in a chair ACF does not treat as damageable (`ACF.Check` false), or in a chair a safezone protects, is a person (nothing to substitute). LVS / simfphys / WAC / Glide cars: ACF types the CAR entity as a "squishy" and their seat entities are separate; I did not look at those addons (V96).
  - It works through ACF's own function names `Damage.doVehicleDamage` and `Damage.doPropDamage` (present in the ACF I have). If your ACF does not have them the hook prints one line ("[acf_lazers_heat] this ACF has no Damage.doVehicleDamage / doPropDamage ...") and does nothing: then a person in a chair is hurt by hits on the chair as before.
  - The flame projectile now ENDS on a seated person's chair (it counts as a part and starts the 0.05 s fuze), where before it passed through the person.
- **THE FILES (FOUR code files and this document; NO config).** `acf_lazers_heat.new.lua` (the SEATS section: `Heat.IsSeat`, `Heat.CanIgnite`, `Heat.SeatOf`, `Heat.MarkSpare`, the hook; `Heat.Hurt` marks its info), `acf_lazer_init.new.lua` (version 22.2: the ignite guards, the mark in `DamageProp`, the person-to-chair redirect in `ProcessLaserImpact`, once per beam), `lazers_flame.new.lua` (the projectile: no fire on a chair, the chair touched in the seated person's place), `lazers_ppc.new.lua` (the blast's `DamageInfo` is marked). The other files of 22.1 and 22 are as they were.

**What 22.1 is (your Mon Nov 2 20:35 message; CUMULATIVE on 15 to 22; untested in game).**
- **Your message, recorded (Mon Nov 2 20:35 local):** "the flamer projectile needs 10x more drag, it is going waaaay too far, also make the fire effect become wider at the same scale the hit sphere does."
- **THE DRAG IS 10 TIMES.** `FlameDragMult` is 40 (22: 4), in the knob line and in the in-code defaults line. Nothing else about the flight changed (same muzzle speeds 80 .. 150 m/s, same gravity, same 3 s life).
- **THE FIRE WIDENS AS THE SPHERE DOES.** A fire sprite is made at the projectile's age a and used to be 0.6 x the sphere's width at that age, growing to 1.2 x the width the sphere had when the sprite was MADE (so the fire did not follow the sphere's growth while it lived, and its start was narrower than the sphere). Now: the sprite starts exactly as wide as the sphere was at age a and ends exactly as wide as the sphere is at age a + the sprite's life (about 0.45 s), `FlameTrailSizeStart` and `FlameTrailSizeEnd` are both 1 (were 0.6 and 1.2), so the fire is 16 units across at the gun and 48 at the end of the flight, a straight line like the sphere, and each sprite keeps growing with the sphere while it lives. The +-20% jitter of each sprite and the fading are as before. The burst where a flight ends uses the same rule.
  | Projectile age | 0 s | 1 s | 2 s | 3 s |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Sphere diameter = width of a sprite made then, units | 16.0 | 26.7 | 37.3 | 48.0 |
  | ...and the width that sprite reaches when it dies (0.45 s later) | 20.8 | 31.5 | 42.1 | 48.0 |
- **A CORRECTION OF MY 22 DOCUMENT AND MESSAGE (read this).** I wrote that "drag alone takes only about 5% of the speed in 3 s (115 to 109 m/s)". That number was the LENGTH of the velocity after 3 s, which also counts the fall (gravity adds speed downward), so it hid the drag. What the drag really takes is the SIDEWAYS speed: with the 22 value (x4) a 150 mm flame kept 100.2 of its 115 m/s sideways (87%); with the new x40 it keeps 45.5 m/s (40%). The tool's readout now says "N m/s sideways after 3 s" (new field `speedSideways`, pure re-run field `sideways`); the old `speedAfter` (length of the velocity) is still there.
- **WHAT 10 x DRAG DOES TO THE RANGE (150 mm, fired level, from 100 / 500 / 2000 units = 2.5 / 12.7 / 50.8 m up; time of flight and distance to the ground; from `sim/calc221_table.lua`):**
  | Drag x | from 2.5 m | from 12.7 m | from 50.8 m | sideways speed after 3 s |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 1 (an ordinary steel shell 4 calibers long) | 0.59 s, 66.2 m | 1.30 s, 147.7 m | 2.61 s, 293.9 m | 110.9 m/s |
  | 4 (22) | 0.59 s, 65.8 m | 1.32 s, 145.4 m | 2.64 s, 285.1 m | 100.2 m/s |
  | **40 (22.1)** | **0.61 s, 60.7 m** | **1.41 s, 123.0 m** | **2.97 s, 210.4 m** | **45.5 m/s** |
  | 100 | 0.64 s, 53.7 m | 1.52 s, 98.2 m | 3.02 s (still in the air), 144.0 m | 22.6 m/s |
  | 200 | 0.67 s, 45.2 m | 1.61 s, 74.5 m | 3.02 s (air), 97.2 m | 11.0 m/s |
  | 400 | 0.71 s, 34.6 m | 1.70 s, 51.3 m | 3.02 s (air), 60.7 m | 4.2 m/s |
  | 1000 | 0.76 s, 20.8 m | 1.85 s, 27.4 m | 3.02 s (air), 29.2 m | 0.7 m/s |
  **Read this before you test (V93):** a level shot from a normal height (2.5 m) lands in about 0.6 s whatever the drag, because GRAVITY brings it down (600 units/s^2), so 10 x drag shortens it only from 65.8 m to 60.7 m. The drag matters for shots from higher up and for the whole 3 s flight (the farthest reach over open ground or upwards falls from about 330 m to about 250 m). If it still goes too far on level ground, the knobs that shorten it are `FlameDragMult` (x400 = 35 m from 2.5 m) and the muzzle speeds `FlameSpeedSmall` / `FlameSpeedBig` (80 / 150 m/s).
  | Caliber | 75 mm | 100 mm | 150 mm | 200 mm | 225 mm |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Muzzle velocity, m/s | 80.0 | 91.7 | 115.0 | 138.3 | 150.0 |
  | Sideways speed after 3 s at x40, m/s (percent of muzzle) | 24.2 (30%) | 31.6 (34%) | 45.5 (40%) | 58.9 (43%) | 65.4 (44%) |
  | Landing from 2.5 m up at x40 | 0.62 s, 40.8 m | 0.61 s, 47.6 m | 0.61 s, 60.7 m | 0.61 s, 73.6 m | 0.61 s, 80.0 m |

- **THE FUSION ENGINE'S STATIC HEAT (your 02:55 message).** The engine heats itself every pass by `GetFusionStaticHeat(hp, charge, health)` x dt, where kJ per second = `FusionStaticHeat` (0.15) x the STANDARD HEAT (`FusionHeatScalar` x the engine's FULL OUTPUT in kJ/s = hp x GenKJPerHP, the "max theoretical charging output") x the CHARGE SHARE (the engine's own charge / its store, 0..1) x the HEALTH MULTIPLIER (1 at full health, a straight line to `FusionDamagedHeatMult` 5 at no health). It does not matter whether the capacitors have room. The old heat (a share of the kJ it puts into the capacitors) is OFF (`FusionEnergyHeatMult` 0); 1 puts it back on top.

  | ...static heat at full charge, full health | 487.5 | 1,950 | 12,675 |
  | ...at no health, full charge | 2,437.5 | 9,750 | 63,375 |

The same engine with the shipped 0.25: 81 / 136 / 161 F healthy, 288 F at no health. A 250 hp engine with your 2.5: 118 / 248 / 293 F healthy, 547 F at no health. (From `sim/calc22_fusion.lua`.)

- **PITCH SCALING WITH SIZE: WHAT WAS ALREADY THERE.** The fusion hum has had it since 17 (`FusionHumPitchSmall` 125 down to `FusionHumPitchBig` 70: bigger = deeper). The PPC fire sound has had it since 16.2 (`PPCFirePitchSmall` 125 down to `PPCFirePitchBig` 75). The lazer had it on paper (`MuzzlePitchSmall` 315 down to `MuzzlePitchBig` 180) and it did not work (below). The generator had no sound at all. All four scale their pitch with size now.
- **THE LAZER: WHY IT DID NOT SCALE, AND THE FIX (diagnosis from the code, not heard: [GUESS], V70).** Two causes, both in how the hum was started. (1) `MuzzlePitchSmall` was 315 and the engine's pitch ceiling is 255, so the pitch was 255 for every lazer from 20 mm up to about 144 mm (44% of the size range) and only fell for the biggest ones. (2) The engine's volume was `min(loudness, 1)` where loudness runs 1.4 (small) to 2.4 (big), so the volume was 1 at EVERY size and only the sound level (range, 78 to 83 dB) differed. On top of that the hum started with `Play()` and was changed a moment later with `ChangeVolume/ChangePitch(..., 0)`. **Now:** `ACFLazers.GetMuzzleSound(caliber)` gives the volume, the pitch and the level, and the lazer starts the hum WITH them (`SetSoundLevel` first, then `PlayEx(volume, pitch)`). `MuzzlePitchSmall` is 255 (a straight line 255 down to 180 over the whole range) and the volume is the loudness divided by the BIGGER of the two ends (`MuzzleVolumeBig`), so the biggest lazer is at 1 and the smallest at 1.4 / 2.4 = 0.58:
  | Lazer caliber | 20 mm | 50 mm | 100 mm | 144 mm | 200 mm | 250 mm | 300 mm |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Volume (20: 1 at every size) | 0.58 | 0.63 | 0.70 | 0.77 | 0.85 | 0.93 | 1.00 |
  | Pitch (20: 255 up to 144 mm, then 228 at 200 mm, 180 at 300 mm) | 255 | 247 | 234 | 222 | 207 | 193 | 180 |
  | Range, dB (as in 20 with the same knobs) | 77.9 | 78.6 | 79.5 | 80.3 | 81.2 | 81.9 | 82.6 |
  Making the small lazers quieter than before is the price of making the volume scale (it can only go DOWN from 1): V70.
- **THE GENERATOR HUM (new).** `ambient/grinder/grinderloop_01.wav` (`GenHumSound`) plays on the hub while it is taking power from its engines. Its SIZE is the model's own size curve (`GetGenScale` between `GenHPMin` 50 and `GenHPMax` 3500; 350 hp, the default, is a third of the way, V73). Small to big: volume `GenHumVolumeSmall` 0.35 to `GenHumVolumeBig` 1, pitch `GenHumPitchSmall` 140 to `GenHumPitchBig` 70 (bigger = deeper), range `GenHumLevelSmall` 55 to `GenHumLevelBig` 85 dB. The LOAD (hp taken / rated hp) scales it: the volume from `GenHumLoadFloor` 0.4 (a trickle) to 1 (full load), the pitch from `GenHumPitchIdle` 0.85 to 1; under `GenHumMinLoad` 0.01 there is no hum (capacitors full, `AllowCharge` off, engines off, Draw 0), V74. It is made when the hub first takes power, glides to the new numbers over one output interval (0.25 s) as the load moves, restarts if the engine stopped it, is made again when the sound file or the size changes, and stops when the hub is removed.
  | Rated hp | 50 | 100 | 200 | 350 | 700 | 1500 | 2500 | 3500 |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Size (0..1) | 0.00 | 0.09 | 0.21 | 0.33 | 0.49 | 0.70 | 0.87 | 1.00 |
  | Volume at full load | 0.35 | 0.41 | 0.49 | 0.57 | 0.67 | 0.81 | 0.92 | 1.00 |
  | Pitch at full load | 140 | 134 | 125 | 117 | 106 | 91 | 79 | 70 |
  | Range, dB | 55.0 | 57.8 | 61.4 | 65.0 | 69.6 | 76.0 | 81.1 | 85.0 |
  | Volume / pitch at 25% load | 0.19 / 124 | 0.23 / 119 | 0.27 / 111 | 0.31 / 104 | 0.37 / 94 | 0.44 / 81 | 0.50 / 70 | 0.55 / 62 |

The pitch follows the caliber as since 16.2 (`PPCFirePitchSmall` 125, `PPCFirePitchBig` 75, over the PPC's own size curve: 119.6 at 50 mm, 110.7 at 100 mm, 75 at 300 mm) and NOW THE VOLUME DOES TOO: both layers are multiplied by `ACFLazers.GetPPCFireVolumeMult(caliber)`, a straight line from `PPCFireVolumeSmallMult` 0.6 at `PPCCurveStart` (20 mm, which no PPC reaches) to 1 at 300 mm. **What 20 is (your four Sun Nov 1 23:41 to 23:50 messages; CUMULATIVE on 15 to 19; untested in game).**
- **Your messages, recorded:** (23:41) "i need to use a different damage calculation than ACF HE damage, because i need it to worry less about how much armor a prop has, also can we do a different distance damage falloff curve, inverseley exponential?" (23:41) "also i want ppcs to only damage very fast when you fire while they are over heat, they explode too fast as is when just over heat, they need to damage way slower by default, about 4x slower." (23:45) "HOLY shit we need to scale weight to the output of the engine, not the volume of the model lol" (23:50) "also reduce the amount fusion engines conform to the inverse exponential for their model size, make it about 3/4 as exponential as it is."
- **THE BLAST'S OWN DAMAGE MODEL.** ACF still decides WHO the blast reaches (its radius, the line of sight, what blocks what, permissions, safezones, your own contraption). What a PROP then takes is no longer ACF's `area x (penetration / armour)^2 x feathering`. It is **damage = (area / 264.7) x S x F(x) x A x the shot's age factor**, in MULTIPLES OF THE PART'S OWN BASE HEALTH (area / `ACF.Threshold`, 264.7):
  - **S** = `PPCBlastHealthSmall` 2.2 at 50 mm to `PPCBlastHealthBig` 4.4 at 300 mm, a straight line over the caliber (100 mm: 2.64; 150 mm: 3.08; 200 mm: 3.52; 250 mm: 3.96).
  - **x** = distance of the hit from the blast's centre / the blast radius (the hit position ACF puts on the damage, against the explosion position; radius 175 units at 100 mm, about 399 at 300 mm).
  - **F(x)** = the falloff, below. **A** = the armour factor, below.
  - The damage goes into ACF's "custom damage" field of the damage result (`DamageResult:SetDamage`), which `Compute()` hands back as the damage. ACF's penetration "loss" (how much of the blast a plate swallows on the way to the next part) is still worked out from ACF's own numbers.
  - **People** (ACF "Squishy"), a part whose area or hit positions cannot be measured, and everything when the flag `PPCOwnDamage` is off take the 19 path (ACF's damage x `PPCPropDamageScalar` 640 x the age factor). `PPCOwnDamage false` gives you the 19 blast back whole.
  - The heat a part gets is still its damage x `PPCHeatPerDamage` 3750, held to `PPCHeatMaxPct` (300% of the part's HOT point). The heat now follows the NEW damage (so a part the new model hurts more is hotter: V66).
- **THE FALLOFF: AN INVERSE EXPONENTIAL.** F(x) = (e^(-rate x) - e^(-rate)) / (1 - e^(-rate)), `PPCFalloffRate` 3, blended with the straight line 1 - x by `PPCFalloffBlend` (1 = fully curved, 0 = the straight line). 1 at the centre, 0 at the edge, steepest next to the blast and a long flat tail towards the edge (ACF's own was 1 - sqrt(x), steepest at the edge side):
  | x (share of the radius) | 5% | 10% | 25% | 50% | 75% | 90% |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | F(x), rate 3 | 0.85 | 0.73 | 0.44 | 0.18 | 0.06 | 0.02 |
  | ACF's 1 - sqrt(x) | 0.78 | 0.68 | 0.50 | 0.29 | 0.13 | 0.05 |
  | 100 mm shot (S 2.64): health lost, share of the part's full health, A = 1 | 2.25 | 1.92 | 1.17 | 0.48 | 0.15 | 0.05 |
  | 300 mm shot (S 4.4): the same | 3.76 | 3.20 | 1.96 | 0.80 | 0.26 | 0.08 |
  (Distances: 100 mm, radius 174.8 units: 8.7 / 17.5 / 43.7 / 87.4 / 131.1 / 157.3 units; 300 mm, radius 398.5: 19.9 / 39.9 / 99.6 / 199.3 / 298.9 / 358.7 units. A part whose armour is above the blast's penetration takes 0.75 of these.)
- **THE ARMOUR: it counts, but for a quarter at most.** A = (1 - `PPCArmourInfluence`) + `PPCArmourInfluence` x (min(1, penetration / armour))^2, `PPCArmourInfluence` 0.25. A part the blast can pierce takes the full number (A = 1); a part with far more armour than the blast's penetration takes 0.75 of it, never less. 0 = armour is ignored, 1 = ACF's own armour law. **HOW I READ "worry less about how much armor a prop has"**: armour can change the damage by 25% at most, where in 19 a thick plate took almost nothing (V64). A missing penetration or armour counts as A = 1.
- **THE LEVEL: where I put S (V66).** S 2.2 at 50 mm and 4.4 at 300 mm are picked so a 20 mm part of 3000 cm2 at 10% of the radius takes about what 19 gave it (about 16 points from a 100 mm shot and about 27 from a 300 mm shot; the toy default part). Away from that point they differ: a thin part far out takes less than in 19, a thick one takes much more, and a part is destroyed outright out to about a third of the radius (100 mm: 1.9 times its health at 10%, 1.2 times at 25%, 0.48 at half the radius).
- **THE PPC'S SELF-DAMAGE: 4 TIMES SLOWER, FAST ONLY AFTER FIRING OVER THE LIMIT.** The whole self-damage law (the over^2 part AND the turbo part past `HeatTurboStartPct` 120%, which is what really kills a PPC just over its limit) is multiplied by `PPCHeatDamageMult` 0.25. It goes back to the old speed (`PPCHeatOverfireMult` 1) for `PPCHeatOverfireSeconds` 5 after a shot that was RELEASED while the PPC's heat was ALREADY over 100% (read before that shot's own heat is added); every such shot restarts the 5 seconds. A PPC that merely sits over its limit, or whose shot took it over from below, loses health at the slow rate. The hover text says "Overheated: taking damage", and "Overheated: taking damage FAST (it fired while over its limit)" while the fast rate is on. The window is not saved by the duplicator.
  | PPC heat | 105% | 110% | 120% | 130% | 150% | 200% |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | 19 and the FAST rate: % of max health per second | 0.010 | 0.040 | 0.160 | 32.7 | 77.3 | 432.7 |
  | 20 slow rate: % per second | 0.003 | 0.010 | 0.040 | 8.2 | 19.3 | 108.2 |
  | 20 slow rate: seconds from full health to dead | 40,000 | 10,000 | 2,500 | 12.2 | 5.2 | 0.9 |
  | 19 and FAST: seconds to dead | 10,000 | 2,500 | 625 | 3.1 | 1.3 | 0.2 |
  (Past 120% the turbo term takes over, so a PPC at 130% dies in about 3 seconds in 19 and about 12 seconds now; a PPC at 110% to 120% takes minutes to hours either way.)
- **THE FUSION ENGINE'S MASS IS ON ITS OUTPUT.** `GetFusionMass(hp)` now runs on the straight slider (`GetFusionSlider`: `FusionMassSmall` at `FusionHPMin` to `FusionMassBig` at `FusionHPMax`), no longer on the curved size fraction of 17, so the mass follows the rated power and not the volume of the model. Shipped numbers: 250 hp 1,500 kg; 1000 hp 2,520 kg; 2000 hp 3,880 kg; 3375 hp 5,750 kg; 6000 hp 9,320 kg; 6500 hp 10,000 kg. With YOUR config (`FusionHPMin` 100, `FusionMassSmall` 600): 100 hp 600 kg; 300 hp 894 kg (17 to 19: 2,680 at 300 hp); 1000 hp 1,922 kg; 3300 hp 5,300 kg; 6500 hp 10,000 kg.
- **THE FUSION MODEL SIZE: 3/4 AS EXPONENTIAL.** `FusionSizeCurve` 1 -> 0.75 (the blend of the inverse exponential with the straight line; the rate stays 8). Scale with your config (`FusionScaleSmall` 0.3): 100 / 300 / 1000 / 3300 / 6500 hp = 0.300 / 0.422 / 0.679 / 0.903 / 1.000 (17 to 19: 0.300 / 0.455 / 0.773 / 0.987 / 1.000); shipped numbers (0.25 to 1): 250 / 300 / 1000 / 2000 / 3300 / 6500 hp = 0.250 / 0.286 / 0.620 / 0.805 / 0.893 / 1.000. The armour, the health (surface area) and the heat capacity follow the scale as before. **HOW I READ "3/4 as exponential"**: the blend 0.75, not the rate 6 (the rate would keep the shape's name and change where it bends; the blend is the knob that is literally "how much of the curve"). If you meant the rate, put `FusionSizeCurveRate` 6 and `FusionSizeCurve` 1 in the config (V69).
- **WHAT I DID NOT CHANGE.** The blast radius and the PPC's energy and cost, `PPCPropDamageScalar` (still used for people and when the flag is off), `PPCHeatPerDamage`, the PPC's heat per shot and limit, the ball, the fusion engine's code (its `init.lua`, `shared.lua`, `cl_init.lua` and tool are the 17 and 16.9 files byte for byte: they read the mass and the scale from the config; their comments still describe the old mass curve, see the code map), the lazer, the capacitor, the generator, the radiator. PPC penetration is still not built: it waits for your decision after the test.
- **BANDAID LEVEL (your rule).** None: the damage goes through the field ACF already gives a damage result for a custom number (nothing of ACF's explosion is copied or replaced), the whole new model is four helpers on the knobs in the config (`GetPPCBlastHealth`, `GetPPCBlastFalloff`, `GetPPCArmourFactor`, `GetPPCBlastDamage`) that the blast hook, the toy and the tests all read, the self-damage factor is one helper read at the one place the loss is worked out, and the mass is a one-word change in the one helper that gives it. A flag (`PPCOwnDamage`) and the old knobs keep the 19 behaviour reachable.
- **THE FILES (FIVE).** `acf_lazers_config.new.lua` (your FULL config with your eleven values, the new knobs and helpers, header item 35), `acf_ppc_init.new.lua` (the PPC: version 20, the overfire window, the slower self-damage, the FAST hover text), `lazers_ppc.new.lua` (the ammo type: the blast hook's own damage), `acf_lazers_toys.new.lua` (the blast toy `lz_toy blast` uses the new model and says which one it uses) and this document. **Drop the `.new` from every name; copy to the game folder, the git folder AND the SERVER; restart the game AND the server.** The four code files belong together (the PPC refuses to load with an older config and says so). The console says `[acf_ppc] init.lua version 20 loaded`.

A PPC keeps its colour (three whole numbers `BallR`, `BallG`, `BallB`, 0 to 255, clamped by the PPC whatever the tool or a dupe sends), tells the clients the colour with its other ball values (the networked vector `FlightColor`), and the client hook in `lazers_ppc.lua` puts it on the flying ball with **alpha 255, always** (the colour is built as `Color(r, g, b, 255)`; nothing in the addon can lower it). **Cyan (0, 255, 255) is the starting colour**: the colour picker starts there, and so does any PPC that was never given a colour (every PPC you built before 19, a dupe from before 19): the knobs `PPCBallColorR / G / B` (0, 255, 255) are that colour. Left-click with the tool on a new spot spawns a PPC with the picked colour; left-click on a PPC **recolours it** (the same click also gives it the slider's size, as the tool always did: set the slider to the PPC's size first if you only want a new colour, V62). A copy (the duplicator) keeps the colour: the three names are in the duplicator list.

**What 18 is (your Sun Nov 1 21:35 and 21:39 messages; CUMULATIVE on 15 to 17; untested in game).**
- **Your messages, recorded:** (21:35) "make ppcs do 8x the acf damage they do now". (21:39) "reduce explosive yield, and try to keep the damage at the same level as with that 8x i talked about, i want half the radius we were getting on the explosion."
- **HOW I READ IT.** The PPC's blast is an ACF explosion whose HE filler is the shot's energy x `PPCBlastScalar` / 8000. ACF's blast radius is `filler ^ 0.33 x 8 m` (`explosion_sh.lua`), so **half the radius is a filler of 0.5 ^ (1 / 0.33) = 0.1224 of what it was: `PPCBlastScalar` 1 -> 0.1224** (a shot's energy cost does not change; 100 mm: 1.37 kg of HE -> 0.168 kg, radius 350 -> 175 units (8.9 m -> 4.4 m); 300 mm: 16.7 kg -> 2.04 kg, 797 -> 399 units). "The damage at the same level as the 8x" I read as: **a part at a given distance from the blast takes about what the 8 times blast of the old radius gave it at that distance**, inside the smaller sphere. That is not one number, see the next point.
- **WHY ONE KNOB CANNOT MATCH IT EXACTLY.** ACF's blast damage to a part is `area / 264.7 x falloff x (blast penetration / armour)^2` (the ratio never above 1). The blast's penetration is `(filler x 8000 x share)^0.3 x 0.25` (`share` = how much of the blast's sphere the part covers), so a smaller filler means less penetration: a part behind armour that the old blast only just scratched takes about a quarter of the damage at the same distance (the falloff also hits sooner, because it is a share of the radius). Parts with armour THINNER than the penetration are at the cap (ratio 1) and do not care about the yield. So I put **`PPCPropDamageScalar` 20 -> 640 = 8 (your 8x) x 20 (what 17 had) x 4 (to make up for the weaker blast)**. The x4 matches the old blast exactly for a part behind armour at 10% of the new radius, within 5% from 5% to 10%, 12% less at a quarter of the new radius, a third less at half, and falling to a third at 80%; a very thin plate (about 2 mm) right at the centre takes MORE than before (it was already at the cap: x2.8 at 100 mm, x3.7 at 300 mm at 5% of the radius), and nothing is hurt beyond the new radius. - **THE HEAT IS UNCHANGED FROM 17 (the third number): `PPCHeatPerDamage` 30000 -> 3750.** A blast gives a part its damage x `PPCHeatPerDamage` kJ of heat (capped by `PPCHeatMaxPct`). The part takes about 8 times 17's damage next to the blast, so an eighth of the heat per point gives it 17's heat there (a test checks a 50 mm part at the same distance: the same damage within 5% and the same heat). **If you want the heat to follow the damage (8 times as hot), put it back to 30000 (V56).**
- **WHAT ELSE FOLLOWS FROM A SMALLER BLAST.** (a) The explosion picture and the screen shake are sized from the filler: the fireball is half the size (and the shake is weaker: it follows the filler's power). (b) A part the damage kills stops shielding what is behind it (ACF's own loss maths does not see the scalar), so the blast can still reach deeper into a craft than before but not farther out. (c) People in the blast take 8 times as much within it. (d) The PPC's tool screen and hover (blast radius, HE kg) show the new numbers by themselves. (e) The 300 unit radius in which a PPC hit cuts a fusion engine's operating percent is its own knob and did not change, so a hit can now cut an engine the blast itself does not reach. (f) The PPC's death explosion and its heat damage to itself are separate knobs and did not change.

Byte-identical to what 17 and earlier delivered (a script checks it).

**What 17 is (your Sun Nov 1 20:23, 20:24 and 20:26 messages; CUMULATIVE on 15 to 16.9; untested in game).**
- **Your messages, recorded:** (20:23) "changed [FusionHPMin = 100, FusionMaxPerPlayer = 8, FusionMassSmall = 600, FusionHeatScalar = 2.5, FusionScaleSmall = 0.3] in config, also we need a noise to represent how much that percentage is at, some low energetic humming, scaling with size of engine. also, we need an inversley exponential size scaling, so from 100 to 300, there is more size difference than 2000 to 6000 for example. need an adjustment knob for that scale ratio, between straight line scaling and inverse exponential." (20:24) "also i need to know if more heat is generated when its less efficient, so that way when it gets near full damaged, the heat output gets twice as bad," (20:26) "also let the active value, from 0-1, determine the intended operating percentage. lets players tell the engine how to act incase heat is a problem, less intended operating percentage, less heat, also less firing capability in that way."
- **YOUR FIVE VALUES.** `FusionHPMin` 100 (shipped 250), `FusionMaxPerPlayer` 8 (shipped 40), `FusionMassSmall` 600 (shipped 1500), `FusionHeatScalar` 2.5 (shipped 0.25), `FusionScaleSmall` 0.3 (shipped 0.25). They are in the delivered config as `[YOUR VALUE, shipped N]` lines (with your six Heat values from before: eleven in all). The tree keeps the shipped numbers (the tests pin them). Note: `FusionMaxPerPlayer` only sets the DEFAULT of the server variable `sbox_maxacf_fusions`; if the server already saved that variable (it is archived), set it there too.
- **THE THROTTLE (your 20:26 message).** The `Active` wire input is now a number from 0 to 1 = the operating percent the engine is asked to hold: 0 = off, 1 = 100% (as in 16.9), 0.4 = 40%; over 1 counts as 1, under 0 and NaN count as 0. The engine's TARGET is the LOWER of the throttle and its ceiling (health efficiency x heat efficiency). It climbs to the target at the spin-up rate (8 s to 15 s for 0 to 100%) and falls to it at `FusionSpinDownMult` x that rate (also when you lower the throttle). Energy is hp x 5.2 x operating, and heat is made per kJ stored, so a lower throttle is less energy AND less heat, and a hot engine can be held down by wire. Nothing wired = `FusionActiveDefault` (now a fraction too; 1 = full, as before). A PPC cut still takes points off whatever the operating percent is and it then climbs back toward the target. The hover shows "Active input = N% asked for"; the status line says "Running at 40% (the Active input asks for 40%)", "Spinning up to the Active input's 60%: operating 30%" or "Spinning down to the Active input's 20%: operating 50%" when the throttle (not the ceiling) is what it steers by. A new helper pair: `GetFusionThrottle(value)` and `GetFusionTarget(throttle, ceiling)`. At throttle 1 everything is exactly the 16.9 behaviour.
- **THE SIZE CURVE (your 20:23 message).** Model scale and mass now run on a SIZE FRACTION instead of the straight slider: `curved = (1 - e^(-rate x slider)) / (1 - e^(-rate))` (an inverse exponential: it climbs fast at the small end and flattens out; 0 -> 0 and 1 -> 1 for any rate) and `size = slider + FusionSizeCurve x (curved - slider)`. **`FusionSizeCurve` is your adjustment knob: 0 = the straight line, 1 = the full inverse exponential, 0.5 = half way.** `FusionSizeCurveRate` (default 8) is how sharp the exponential is (0.001 and under = the straight line again, 100 is the top). Defaults are `FusionSizeCurve` 1 and `FusionSizeCurveRate` 8 [GUESS, V50]: with your 100..6500 hp the step 100 -> 300 hp is 22.1% of the whole size range and the step 2000 -> 6000 hp only 9.2% (2.39 times less), as you asked. | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 2 | 7.0% | 45.6% | 0.15 | 0.349 / 1,259 |

If that is too sharp, rate 4 to 6 is milder (rate 6 makes the two steps equal); if you want the exact opposite of a line, rate 12. The blend knob scales it all: blend 0.5 at 300 hp is scale 0.388 / 1,787 kg (blend 0: 0.322 / 894; blend 1: 0.455 / 2,680). Heavier mid-range engines also hold heat better (capacity is mass x 0.49 kJ/K): 1000 hp is 6,950 kg on the curve, 1,922 kg on the line.
- **THE HUM (your 20:23 message).** A looping sound played from the SERVER (everybody near hears it) that follows the operating percent: silent at 0 (and under `FusionHumMinOperating` 1%), louder and higher in pitch as it climbs, so you can hear how hard the engine works; volume = (`FusionHumVolumeSmall` 0.3 .. `FusionHumVolumeBig` 1 along the size fraction) x operating; pitch = (`FusionHumPitchSmall` 125 .. `FusionHumPitchBig` 70) x (`FusionHumPitchIdle` 0.5 .. 1 along the operating percent): a bigger engine is louder, deeper and carries farther (sound level `FusionHumLevelSmall` 60 dB .. `FusionHumLevelBig` 80 dB, set once when the patch is made). The sound file is `FusionHumSound` = `ambient/machines/power_transformer_loop_1.wav` [GUESS that it exists and loops; V51]: set it to another loop (`ambient/machines/combine_shield_loop3.wav`, `ambient/energy/force_field_loop1.wav`) or `""` for no hum. Volume and pitch glide over one pass (0.1 s); nothing is sent while the operating percent is steady; the patch is stopped at 0%, when the engine is removed or explodes, and made again if the file or the size (a resize) changes. A sound error can never stop the engine (it is caught and printed once). Your config:
| Rated power | Operating 1% | 25% | 50% | 75% | 100% |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 100 hp: volume / pitch | 0.00 / 63 | 0.07 / 78 | 0.15 / 94 | 0.22 / 109 | 0.30 / 125 |
| 6500 hp: volume / pitch | 0.01 / 35 | 0.25 / 44 | 0.50 / 52 | 0.75 / 61 | 1.00 / 70 |
- **22: THE NEXT PARAGRAPH AND ITS TABLE DESCRIBE THE 17 TO 21 HEAT LAW (heat per kJ put into the capacitors, damage x1 to x2). 22 REPLACES IT with the static heat (see the 22 block at the top and the `Heat (22, ...)` line of the fusion section); the old law is still there with `FusionEnergyHeatMult` 1, and `FusionDamagedHeatMult` is 5 now.**
- **YOUR HEAT QUESTION (20:24): "is more heat generated when it is less efficient?" Until 17: NO, the opposite.** Heat was `FusionHeatScalar` x the kJ the engine put into the capacitors, and a damaged engine puts in LESS (its ceiling falls to 50% at 0% health), so it made LESS heat per second, not more. **17 adds `FusionDamagedHeatMult` (default 2): the heat per kJ rises on a straight line from x1 at full health to x2 at 0% health** (helper `GetFusionHeatMult(health)`; the hover shows "Heat made = 0.50 kJ per kJ made (x2.00 from damage), 400 kJ/s now"). **But read this: it is "twice as bad PER kJ". Because a wrecked engine also only reaches 50% operating, its heat per SECOND at its ceiling is x1.00 of a healthy engine's with the default 2 (it makes half the energy with twice the heat per kJ). If you meant "twice the heat per second", set `FusionDamagedHeatMult` to 4 (x2.00 per second at 0% health; V52).** Table (per kJ, per second at the engine's own ceiling, both against a healthy engine):
| FusionDamagedHeatMult | Health 100% | 75% | 50% | 25% | 0% |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Ceiling (energy) | 100% | 88% | 75% | 62% | 50% |
| 1 (damage does not change the heat per kJ; 16.9) | x1.00 per kJ, x1.00 per second | x1.00, x0.88 | x1.00, x0.75 | x1.00, x0.62 | x1.00, x0.50 |
| **2 (default)** | x1.00, x1.00 | x1.25, x1.09 | x1.50, x1.12 | x1.75, x1.09 | **x2.00, x1.00** |
| 4 | x1.00, x1.00 | x1.75, x1.53 | x2.50, x1.88 | x3.25, x2.03 | **x4.00, x2.00** |
  Simulated (your config, 6500 hp, no cooling, full throttle): healthy it explodes after 116 s, at 0% health with the multiplier 2 after 112 s (the same heat per second, half the energy), with the multiplier 4 after 37 s. 3300 hp at 0% health: survives with 2 (1,258 F), explodes after 102 s with 4.
- **THE HEAT SIMULATION OF YOUR CONFIG (the whole engine run for an hour, `sim/calc170.lua`).** Your values (scalar 2.5, mass 600..10,000 on the size curve, scale 0.3..1) with the new defaults, capacitors that always have room (an empty one every pass), NO radiator, full health. Where it settles after 3,600 s (temperature, share of the critical 1,500 F, the operating percent it keeps), or when it explodes:
| Rated power (hp), mass | Throttle 1 | Throttle 0.75 | Throttle 0.5 | Throttle 0.25 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 100 (600 kg) | 959 F (62%), operating 87.7% | 867 F (56%), 75% | 664 F (42%), 50% | 415 F (25%), 25% |
| 300 (2,680 kg) | 800 F (51%), operating 98.7% | 667 F (42%), 75% | 508 F (31%), 50% | 316 F (18%), 25% |
| 1000 (6,950 kg) | 899 F (58%), operating 91.8% | 787 F (51%), 75% | 602 F (38%), 50% | 375 F (22%), 25% |
| 3300 (9,831 kg) | 1,258 F (83%), operating 67.0% | 1,258 F (83%), 67.0% | 1,047 F (69%), 50% | 667 F (42%), 25% |
| 6500 (10,000 kg) | **EXPLODES after 116 s** | **EXPLODES after 128 s** | **EXPLODES after 214 s** | 1,026 F (67%), 25% |
  The heat efficiency does the regulating: it is 1 up to 50% of the critical rise and falls to 0.5 at the critical heat, so a small engine at full throttle backs itself off (100 hp: 87.7% operating, 959 F) and settles. The 6500 hp engine cannot: it makes 33,800 kJ/s at 100%, 2.5 x that is heat, and its 10,000 kg (4,900 kJ/K) cannot shed it, so it reaches 1,500 F in about two minutes. **The highest throttle that survives an hour with no radiator: 6500 hp = 0.459 (46%, 15,514 kJ/s); every smaller size survives full throttle.** At 0% health (multiplier 2) the 6500 hp engine survives at most 0.229 (7,724 kJ/s). With multiplier 4: 3300 hp at 0% health 0.223, 6500 hp 0.113. With the 16.9 shipped values (scalar 0.25, a straight size line, 250 hp smallest; `sim/calc170c.lua`) the engines settled at 224 F (250 hp, 11% of critical), 402 F (1000 hp), 523 F (3300 hp) and 562 F (6500 hp, 35%) at full throttle: your 2.5 is the change that makes cooling matter (V53). A radiator, a bigger capacitor bank that is never full, or a lower throttle all work; the numbers above are the no-help case.

PPC penetration is NOT built.

Every number is a knob.

- **THE ERROR WAS THE FILE NAME, NOT THE CODE (your Nov 1 message).** ACF only registers an entity whose `init.lua` is actually loaded; a file called `init.lua.new` is never loaded, so `acf_fusion` was "not a registered ACF entity class". **Every delivered file ends in `.new.lua` (or `.new`): drop the `.new` when you copy it** (`acf_fusion_init.new.lua` becomes `init.lua` in `lua\entities\acf_fusion\`). I still made 16.9 harder to get wrong: the class is ALWAYS registered once `init.lua` runs (whatever else is missing), a spawn with something missing says exactly what in chat and in the server console, the tool names the cause when ACF does not know the class (the entity folder is not on the server, or `init.lua` never ran: "look for a `[acf_fusion] init.lua version 16.9 loaded` line"), and the server prints `[acf_fusion] init.lua version 16.9 loaded` and `[acf_fusion] registered with ACF: acf_fusion can be spawned`.
- **HOW I READ IT (the readings; tell me which is wrong, V36 - V48).** (a) **The slider** = the rated power in hp, 250 to 6500, the hp of a generator hub that would make the same energy (the generator tool's meaning). The mass (1,500 to 10,000 kg), the model scale (0.25 to 1), the spin-up (8 s to 15 s), the PPC scalar (1 to 0.5) and the longest PPC wait (3 s to 5 s) all run on straight lines over the slider (V36). (b) **Active** is a wire input; nothing wired counts as ON (`FusionActiveDefault` 1; V37); the 16.8 Draw input is gone. (c) **Operating percent** climbs at (100% / the spin-up seconds) toward its CEILING; switched off, or over its ceiling, it falls at the same speed (`FusionSpinDownMult` 1; V38). (d) **Parented** = it has a GMod parent (`GetParent()` is valid; V39). Not parented: operating percent 0 at once, no energy, no heat; the charge still trickles down. A dupe pastes unparented. (e) **Heat** = `FusionHeatScalar` 0.25 kJ per kJ it puts into the capacitors (nothing stored = no heat); **critical** = 1500 F (`FusionCriticalF`). (f) **Efficiency** (= the ceiling of the operating percent) = health efficiency x heat efficiency: health 1 down to 0.5 at 0% health; heat 1 up to 50% of the critical rise, then a straight line down to 0.5 at the critical heat (V40). (g) **Charge** = the energy it holds: it follows (operating percent x a full store) UP at once and only drains at (full store / 60 s). A full store is 10 s of the engine's full output (V41). (h) **The bang** = filler kg = charge kJ x `FusionBlastMult` (1) / `ACF.HEPower` (8,000), never under 0.1 kJ worth. (i) **Safezone**: an engine in a safezone cannot explode (critical there only raises a red hover line; destroyed there it is removed with no bang) and a PPC does not disturb it (V42). (j) **The PPC cut** = the PPC's own percent-of-its-limit per shot (71.25% at 50 mm to 100% at 300 mm: the only number "the ppc logic" gives out) x the engine's scalar (1 at 250 hp, 0.5 at 6500 hp), taken off the operating percent as percentage points, at once; then it may not grow for a random 1 s to 3 s (250 hp) / 5 s (6500 hp), then it climbs again at the spin-up rate (V43). (k) **The ball**: the flying ball keeps growing in proportion to the caliber, times a multiplier of 1 at 50 mm and `PPCBallBigMult` 1.2 at 300 mm on a straight line (V45). (l) **PPC energy**: both knobs times 2/3 (V46). (m) **PPC penetration** is NOT built; you will say how after you have tested this version (V47).
- **WHAT A FUSION ENGINE IS NOW.** `acf_fusion`, one entity, one tool (Fusion Engine Spawner, one slider "Rated power (hp)"). Every 0.1 s: (1) the operating percent steps toward its ceiling (or 0); (2) the charge follows it; (3) it makes `hp x GenKJPerHP (5.2) x operating percent` kJ per second (a pass is capped at 0.25 s of time), water-filled into the free room of the WHOLE linked capacitor group (the same fill the generator hub and the lazer use), and a quarter of what it stored becomes heat in the engine; (4) at or over 1500 F it explodes. Examples on the slider (all worked out from the knobs by this document's builder):

- **WHAT A PPC DOES TO AN ENGINE (within 300 units of the engine's centre when the PPC goes off).** The cut, as points taken off the operating percent (an engine at 100% is shown; an engine at 40% loses the same points, down to 0):
| PPC caliber (mm) | The PPC's percent of its own limit per shot (the number the cut is made from) | Cut of a 250 hp engine at 100% | Cut of a 6500 hp engine at 100% |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 50 | 71.25% | 71.2 points (100% -> 28.8%) | 35.6 points (100% -> 64.4%) |
| 100 | 77.00% | 77.0 points (100% -> 23.0%) | 38.5 points (100% -> 61.5%) |
| 150 | 82.75% | 82.8 points (100% -> 17.2%) | 41.4 points (100% -> 58.6%) |

| 300 | 100.00% | 100.0 points (100% -> 0.0%) | 50.0 points (100% -> 50.0%) |
  Then it waits a random time from 1 s to 3 s (250 hp) / 5 s (6500 hp) (in between on the slider: a straight line) and climbs again at its spin-up rate (about 12.5%/s for 250 hp, 6.7%/s for 6500 hp). A second hit during the wait cuts again and rolls a new wait. An engine at 0% that is not waiting (idle), one in a safezone and one that is exploding are not touched.
- **THE PPC ENERGY, 2/3 (your Nov 1 message: "reduce the energy consumption of ppcs by a third, just change the knob").** `PPCEnergySmall` 500 -> `1000 / 3` and `PPCEnergyBig` 200000 -> `400000 / 3` (the knob lines and the in-code defaults). The energy curve mixes a line and an exponential between those two numbers, so scaling BOTH by 2/3 scales EVERY caliber by exactly 2/3 (a test checks 9 sizes). **What follows from it (not asked for; read this):** the blast is made from the shot's energy (`PPCBlastScalar` x kJ / 8,000), so the blast's HE and its RADIUS shrink too (the radius by about 12%); the PPC's heat per shot is a percent of its own limit and does not move. If you want the old blast with the cheaper shot, `PPCBlastScalar` 1.5 gives the old filler and radius back (V46):

- **THE FINDING YOU SHOULD KNOW BEFORE YOU TEST (V40).** With the shipped numbers an engine running flat out into capacitors that always have room settles by itself at **224 F (250 hp) / 401 F (1000 hp) / 524 F (3375 hp) / 562 F (6500 hp)**: 11% to 35% of the critical heat. That is the heat system's own cooling holding it (no radiator in my run); it never gets near 1500 F, and not even to the 50% (about 780 F) where the heat efficiency starts to fall. So **an engine only goes critical from OUTSIDE heat** (a PPC blast, fire, a lazer beam, another engine next to it) unless you change a number. (d) "the equivalent of 6500 hp energy gen" = **the energy a generator hub rated 6500 hp makes: 6500 x `GenKJPerHP` (5.2) = 33,800 kJ per second at full health**, no engines and no fuel; the small one 250 hp = 1,300 kJ/s. (e) "10000kg at 1x scale" = the entity's mass 10,000 kg and the model scale 1; "1500kg at 1/4x scale" = mass 1,500 kg and model scale 0.25.
- **WHAT A FUSION ENGINE IS.** A self-powered energy source. Every 0.1 s it makes `hp x GenKJPerHP x health efficiency x Draw` kJ per second (times the time since its last pass), and shares the energy out into the free room of the WHOLE capacitor group it is linked to (the same water-fill the generator hub and the lazer use: an almost full capacitor takes only its room, the rest goes to the others; capacitor-to-capacitor links are followed). A capacitor with `AllowCharge` off or one that is exploding takes nothing; when no capacitor has room it makes nothing. **Large: 33,800 kJ/s, Small: 1,300 kJ/s.** A 60-unit capacitor (203,575 kJ) fills in 6.0 s from the large one and in 2 min 36 s from the small one. **Health:** like the hub, the energy falls in a straight line from x1 at full health to `GenDamagedEfficiency` (0.5) at 0% (the same helper, `GetGenEfficiency`). **Heat:** none as shipped (`FusionHeatScalar` 0, you did not ask for any); 1 would give it a generator hub's heat per kJ made, and it holds heat like any part. **Wire:** input `Draw` (0 to 100, unwired = 100); outputs `Energy`, `Rated`, `MaxEnergy`, `Load`, `Full`, `HeatKJ`, `HeatF`, `HeatPct`. **Hover text:** the status (Making N kJ/s / No capacitor linked / Capacitors full / AllowCharge is off / Draw is 0), Type (name, mass, model scale), Rated power (hp, kJ/s at full health), Making, Health efficiency, Draw input, Temperature (only when `FusionHeatScalar` is above 0), Linked, Capacitors (stored / capacity kJ of the group). **Armour and health:** a guess, see V32: 40 mm at scale 1 (10 mm for the small one) times `ACF.ArmorMod`, health from the surface area like ACF's guns, both knobs.
- **THE TOOL.** `acf_fusion_tool.lua`, "Fusion Engine Spawner" in the "ACF - LaZers" tab (based on the generator tool). Menu: a drop-down (Large / Small), a dark panel with the big amber energy number ("33,800 kJ/s"), "6500 hp x 5.2 kJ per hp", "at 0% health: 16,900 kJ/s (x0.5)", "60 unit cap full in 6.0 s", the mass and model scale, and the same on the toolgun's own screen; all of it comes from `ACFLazers.GetFusionReadout` in the config, so the tool cannot disagree with the engine. **Left-click** spawns the picked size standing on the surface you aim at with its front turned toward you, frozen; left-click on a fusion engine changes it to the picked size in place (links stay). **Right-click / Shift + right-click / Reload** are the link kit exactly as on the generator tool (the engine first, then the capacitor). The server clamps a junk size from the console variable to the default.
- **LINKING.** The engine links to capacitors only (`acf_fusion` > `acf_capacitor`), both directions through ACF's link registry, so the existing link tools do it with no change to them and the capacitor file is unchanged (it already treats everything in its `Generators` set alike). Links survive a size change and a dupe.
- **LIMIT.** The engine creates `sbox_maxacf_fusions` (default `FusionMaxPerPlayer` 40, archived) like the other four; your server sets the number. Dupes: `Entities.Register("acf_fusion", ..., "FusionType")` and the modifier `ACFFusionCaps` (the linked capacitors).

- **HOW I READ IT (five readings; tell me which is wrong, V23 - V27).** (a) "the weight of the low side of the scalar var" = **`PPCMassMin`** (the mass at the small end of the PPC mass curve) 60 -> **120** kg. (b) "20x" damage = **`PPCPropDamageScalar`** 1 -> **20**. (c) "the heat given reduced to 0.6x" = the TOTAL blast heat is 0.6 times what 16.6 gave although the damage is 20 times: **`PPCHeatPerDamage`** 1,000,000 -> **30,000** (20 x 30,000 = 0.6 x 1,000,000). (d) "all entity limits at 40" = the five server variables are SET to their knobs (all 40) every time the server starts. (e) "the same radius" = the sphere ACF searches for targets does not change at all.
- **YES, THE DAMAGE CAN CHANGE WITHOUT THE RADIUS ([SEEN] in ACF's `explosion_sv.lua` and run through ACF's REAL explosion code in my test N10).** ACF takes the blast RADIUS from the explosive filler only (`Damage.getBlastRadius(FillerMass)`), and the filler is the shot's energy times `PPCBlastScalar` / 8000. It then finds the targets in that sphere (`ents.FindInSphere`). Our `PPCPropDamageScalar` is applied LATER, in the hook `ACF_OnDamageEntity`, to the area of a part ACF has already chosen. So the sphere, the parts inside it, the line-of-sight and armour checks are exactly what they were; only the number each part is damaged by is multiplied. N10 fires the same blast at the 16.6 numbers and at the shipped ones: the same sphere radius (399.57 units for a 100 mm shot), the same parts hit the same number of times, **exactly 20 times the damage** for every part, exactly 0.6 times the heat; a scalar of 1000 still gives the same sphere; and as a CONTROL `PPCBlastScalar` 4 does make the sphere bigger (that is the radius knob; I did not touch it).
- **WHAT 20x DOES AND DOES NOT DO (read this: "more concentrated" is not quite what a scalar gives).** The SHAPE of the damage is unchanged: it still falls with the distance from the blast and with the square of the armour (the ratio between two parts is exactly what it was; N10 checks it). Multiplying it by 20 makes every part in the sphere take 20 times more, so parts that used to be scratched near the middle now die and the zone where a part is destroyed gets bigger, but the curve is the same. The damage of a 3000 cm2 part to a fresh 100 mm shot (radius 400 units), at 5% / 25% / 50% of the radius, in ACF damage points, 20 x the 16.6 numbers: **5 mm: 35.8 / 3.34 / 0.86; 20 mm: 2.24 / 0.20 / 0.06; 50 mm: 0.36 / 0.04 / 0.008** (16.6: 1.79 / 0.167 / 0.043; 0.112 / 0.010 / 0.003; 0.018 / 0.002 / 0.0004). If by "concentrated" you meant MORE of it in the middle and relatively LESS at the edge (a plasma ball), that needs a steeper falloff than ACF's, which is a different change (a falloff exponent applied in the same hook, the radius still untouched): V24.
- **SIDE EFFECTS OF 20x, ALL OF THEM TRUE OF THE CODE ([SEEN]) BUT NOT RUN IN GAME.** (1) **People take 20 times the damage too**: the scalar is applied to every target ACF reports (props, engines, people). I did not exclude people; V25. (2) **A part that the bigger damage destroys stops shielding what is behind it**: in ACF's explosion a part that is killed is removed from the blockers and the parts behind it are reached (the Killed / Penetration branch of `explosion_sv.lua`; my scripted sphere world does not run that line-of-sight part). (3) **A part the blast destroys gets no blast heat** (a destroyed part is excluded from the heat pass, `CanHeat`), so in the middle, where parts now die, the blast heat is gone, not 0.6 times. (4) The heat is ACF's damage after the scalar times `PPCHeatPerDamage`, so **the heat follows the damage scalar**: change one and the other has to be re-tuned (damage scalar x heat per damage = 600,000 gives the 0.6 times of 16.6). (5) The cap `PPCHeatMaxPct` (300) is unchanged, so a part that 16.6's heat already held at the cap is still held at the cap: for those the heat is between 0.6 and 1 times, not 0.6.

The five variables (`sbox_maxacf_lazers`, `_capacitors`, `_generators`, `_radiators`, `_ppcs`) are created by each entity's own file with `FCVAR_ARCHIVE`, and `CreateConVar` keeps a value that is already saved: **your 16.5-and-older saved numbers (20 / 20 / 10 / 10 from the old defaults) win over the knobs' 40, even though every `*MaxPerPlayer` knob already said 40 [GUESS: I cannot see your saved settings; this is the only way the code makes a limit other than 40 appear].** The NEW `acf_lazers_limits.lua` (it was comment-only; it now has code) waits for the game to finish loading (hook `InitPostEntity`, after every entity file has created its variable), then sets each variable to the knob's number (`LazerMaxPerPlayer`, `CapMaxPerPlayer`, `GenMaxPerPlayer`, `RadMaxPerPlayer`, `PPCMaxPerPlayer`; 40 when a knob is missing or not a number) and writes ONE line to the server console, e.g. `[acf_lazers] player limits: sbox_maxacf_lazers was 20, now 40, sbox_maxacf_capacitors 40, ...`: **a "was 20" in that line confirms my guess; all 40 already means the limits were never the problem** (then tell me what you saw). New optional knob **`EntityLimitsFromConfig`** (true): set it to false and the file does nothing (no line), and the saved numbers stay. **Consequence: a limit you type in the console (`sbox_maxacf_ppcs 60`) now lasts only until the next start, then the config's number is set again** (change the knob, or set `EntityLimitsFromConfig false`). Single-player GMod never checks limits at all (a listen or dedicated server does).

**What 16.6 is (its numbers hold except where the 16.7 block above replaced them: the PPC masses and heat limits, `PPCPropDamageScalar` and `PPCHeatPerDamage`; your Fri Oct 30 18:14 to 18:23 messages; CUMULATIVE on 15 to 16.5; untested in game).**
- **Your messages, recorded:** (18:14) "also the ppc is adding heat to the lazer when i shoot it at it, but its changing its cooling rate, to where its permanently stuck cooling waaay slow. figure that out. also the minimum size needs to be upped to 50mm"; (18:14) "rather semi permanently, its like when it cooled down it reset to a usable rate"; (18:16) "its not the efficiency of the radiators, that part seems to work fine, its like the lazer refuses to get rid of its heat at any speed faster than default without cooling."; (18:21) "oooo i figured it out, the heatsinks are eating the prop heat, i cant see the prop heats, so im assuming otherwise, i think we may be dealing too much heat damage to props, rather i think i need a heat checker tool to verify"; (18:23) "ah the explosion is setting too many entities hot, its grabbing internal things too"; (18:23) "i only want the explosion to heat props that recieve damage, proportional to the amount of damage received."
- **WHY THE LAZER'S COOLING WAS STUCK ([SEEN] in the code, not measured in game; your 18:21 finding is the same thing).** The radiators share what they can take out between EVERY hot part in their reach by how far above room temperature each one is (`RadWeightPower` 1: a part twice as hot gets twice the share). Since 16.1 the PPC's blast gave EVERY ACF thing inside a sphere around it a few thousand kJ whatever it weighed (with the 16.2 energy: about 25,900 kJ at the centre of a 100 mm blast). A light prop holds only 0.49 kJ per kelvin per kg, so that is thousands of kelvin; a lazer limited to 900 F is a few hundred. Dozens of props at several times the lazer's temperature took nearly all of the radiators' work and the lazer got a sliver (a tenth of a percent), so it cooled at about its own default rate however many radiators you added (your 18:16 "at any speed faster than default"); when the props had cooled it was "reset to a usable rate" (your 18:14). The radiators' efficiency was never the problem (you said so at 18:16, and the test for it still passes). **I did NOT change the radiator's sharing** (see V21): the cause was the heat that was handed out, not the way it is shared.
- **WHAT I DID, 1: the blast heats ONLY what it damages, in proportion to the damage.** The 16.1 pass (every ACF thing inside the blast radius, falling off with distance) is GONE, and with it the knobs `PPCHeatScalar` and `PPCHeatRadiusMult` and the helpers `GetPPCHeat` and `GetPPCHeatRadius` (a config that still has the two lines is simply not read for them). Now, for every part ACF's explosion hurts, the damage it took from THIS blast (ACF health points, the blast and fragment parts added, after `PPCPropDamageScalar`, `PPCFragmentScalar` and the shot's age factor: the very number ACF works out) times **`PPCHeatPerDamage`** (1,000,000 kJ per damage point) is added to the part as heat, but never more than **`PPCHeatMaxPct`** (300) percent of the heat that takes THAT part from room temperature to the HOT temperature, so a thin, light prop is not cooked to thousands of kelvin. ACF decides who is hurt (the line of sight to the blast, the armour in the way, the shooter's contraption and safezone filters of 16.1 all apply), so **a part behind armour, out of sight, in a safezone, one ACF's damage permission protects, a person, a part ACF is already destroying, and the PPC's own contraption get NO heat from the blast** (the PPC still heats itself with its own shot, 16.2 to 16.5). The heat is added once per part after the explosion is over.
- **THE CATCH, read this one (it is ACF's maths, not mine).** (16.7: the damage numbers below are 20 times larger now and the heat numbers 0.6 times; see the 16.7 block.) ACF's blast damage to a part is (its area / 264.7 x a falloff with the distance) x (the blast's penetration / the part's armour)^2, the ratio never counted above 1, and a PPC blast's penetration is only 1 to 4 mm. So the damage, and with it the heat, drops with the SQUARE of the armour: a 5 mm plate takes about 16 times what a 20 mm one does and about a hundred times what a 50 mm one does. The damage of a 3000 cm2 part to a fresh 100 mm shot (radius 400 units), at 5% / 25% / 50% of the radius: **5 mm: 1.79 / 0.167 / 0.043; 20 mm: 0.112 / 0.010 / 0.003; 50 mm: 0.018 / 0.002 / 0.0004** (a 300 mm shot, radius 911 units: 2.98 / 0.278 / 0.071; 0.186 / 0.017 / 0.004; 0.030 / 0.003 / 0.0007). With the default `PPCHeatPerDamage` a 50 mm-armoured part (a 100 mm lazer) at 5% of the radius gets about 18,000 kJ and a 20 mm one 112,000 kJ (before the cap), and at half the radius 400 kJ (50 mm armour), 3,000 kJ (20 mm) or 43,000 kJ (5 mm), all before the cap. **So a lazer inside the blast gets little heat, thin props a lot, and a bigger shot does hardly more damage (the blast reaches further and pushes through a little more armour), so the blast's heat no longer grows with the caliber the way the 16.1 heat followed the shot's energy.** If you want it different, `PPCHeatPerDamage` is the one knob (V17, V18).
- **WHAT I DID, 2: the smallest PPC is 50 mm.** (16.7: the 163 kg, 37,317 kJ limit and 26,588 kJ shot heat below are the 16.6 numbers; with `PPCMassMin` 120 a 50 mm PPC is 222 kg, limit 50,824 kJ, shot heat 36,212 kJ; the table in the 16.7 block.) `PPCCaliberMin` 20 -> 50 (the tool's slider is 50 to 300, a smaller number typed in, a dupe or a console command is made 50 mm with a note). **Every number at 50 mm and above is exactly what it was**: the size curves (mass, energy, speed, heat per shot, fire pitch) used to start at `PPCCaliberMin`, so raising the minimum would have moved all of them; they now start at the new knob **`PPCCurveStart`** (20), the caliber the "Small" knobs belong to. The 50 mm PPC: 163 kg, a limit of 37,317 kJ, one shot 71.25% of it (26,588 kJ), 6,181 kJ per shot, 0.773 kg of HE, blast radius 289 units, 621.43 m/s, fire pitch 120. Nothing is built at 20 mm any more; the 20 mm rows of the older tables are only where the curves begin. The `lz_toy sizes` table starts at 50.
- **WHAT I DID, 3: the Heat Checker.** A new tool, **"Heat Checker"** under "ACF - LaZers" (`acf_heat_tool.lua`, a NEW file). **While it is out, look at any part within `HeatCheckDistance` (4096) units** and next to the crosshair it shows: the temperature (F, or C with the checkbox) and the share of the HOT temperature, for a lazer / PPC / capacitor also the share of ITS OWN limit; the class and the mass; the heat it holds above room temperature and how much of it it loses BY ITSELF in the next second; and, in green, **how much the radiators took out of it in their last sweep (kJ per second) and what the whole pool they belong to took**, or "no radiator is taking heat out of it". **Left-click** lists where the heat is around the spot you aim at (the radius is the slider, 50 to `HeatCheckRadiusMax` 3000, default `HeatCheckRadius` 600): a one-line summary goes to chat, the list to your console: how many parts hold heat and how much, the machines (lazers, PPCs, capacitors; `HeatCheckMachineClasses`) against everything else, the hottest `HeatCheckListMax` (8) parts, and **what share of a radiator's work the machines would get** (the number your problem was about). The same list without the tool: **`acf_lazers_heat_scan [radius]`** in the console (one a second). The tool changes nothing; it only reads. To feed it the radiators' side the radiator file now writes three small fields onto each part it cools (`ACFL_RadOut`, `ACFL_RadPool`, `ACFL_RadOutT`); nothing else of the radiator changed (a script checks that exactly one existing heat line moved).
- **KNOB NAME I HAD TO AVOID.** The tool's question interval first got the name `HeatCheckInterval`, which is ALREADY the lazers' and PPCs' own check of their overheat damage (0.1 s). My PPC test caught it the moment I ran it. The tool's knob is **`HeatCheckQueryInterval`** (0.25 s) and `HeatCheckInterval` is as it was; a test pins both.
- **SIDE EFFECTS TO KNOW.** (1) The PPC's own contraption now gets no blast heat (16.1 to 16.5 heated it, whatever it was welded to); it still takes no blast damage. (2) The shot's blast heat is ACF's damage, so `PPCFragmentScalar` (0 by default: no fragments) only adds heat when you turn the fragments on. (3) The `lz_toy blast` toy now prints the damage and the heat of a part YOU describe (area, armour, mass; change them with `lz_toy blast armour=5 mass=300`) at 2 / 5 / 10 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 99% of the radius, with one orange sphere (the heat zone is gone). (4) The ammo type refuses to explode (removes the projectile with no blast) when the config has no `GetPPCDamageHeat`, never an unprotected blast; the PPC init now asks for `GetPPCDamageHeat` instead of `GetPPCHeat` when it loads.
- **WHAT I DID NOT CHANGE.** The radiator (efficiency, sharing by `(kJ / capacity)^RadWeightPower`, the flush, the sound, the readouts), the lazer, the capacitor, the heat store's maths, the PPC's own shot heat (16.2 to 16.5: 71.25% at 50 mm, 77% at 100 mm, 100% at 300 mm of its limit), the cooling, the damage law, the death explosion, the sounds, the energy. Offered, not done: a priority tier so machines always get their share of a radiator's work (V21); heating by mass so a blast makes parts equally hot instead of equally many kJ; tuning `PPCHeatPerDamage` with you using the checker.

**What 16.3 is (its KNOB (PPCHeatSmall / PPCHeatBig 8.465) and its per-size table are REPLACED by 16.4 above: the 77% is now the same at every size; the 100 mm numbers, the 153% for two shots and the waits hold; your Fri Oct 30 17:24 message; CUMULATIVE on 15 to 16.2; untested in game).**
- **Your message, recorded:** "alright, make the ppc do about 77% of their max heat per shot, seems more reasonable than 51, cause cooling is a tad too easy".
- **WHAT I DID (my reading; tell me if it is wrong).** "Max heat" = the PPC's limit (`PPCHeatCapF`, 900 F = 100%), "per shot" = the heat one shot gives the PPC, "51" = the 51.09% a 100 mm shot gave in 16.2. So **ONE shot of a 100 mm PPC now takes it to 77.0% of its limit.** I moved the one lever that does that: **`PPCHeatSmall` and `PPCHeatBig` 5.616 -> 8.465** (kJ of heat per kJ of a shot; x1.507). **CORE FIX** (a knob; no code changed, the PPC init and the PPC tool are the 16.2 files). Because both ends moved by the same factor, EVERY size takes 1.507 times its 16.2 percentage (table below). The comment table next to the knob, the in-code defaults of the helper and the tool's text ("Each shot heats the PPC by 139268 kJ (77% of its limit, before cooling)") follow.
- **WHAT THIS MEANS (read this, it is a different weapon from 16.2).** In 16.2 you asked for "twice ~102%"; 77% per shot replaces that. A 100 mm PPC now:
  - **fires ONE shot safely** (77%, no damage at all under 100%);
  - **a second shot a second later puts it at 153.4%**, where the lazers' damage law (quadratic part plus the turbo from 120%) takes **88.5% of its max health per second**: it is dead in about a second. A third shot would be 229% (967% per second), a fourth 303%;
  - to fire again without going over 100% the 77% has to fall to 23%: **with no radiator that takes about 200 seconds** (one shot falls to 50% after 63 s, to 25% after 184 s, to 10% after 372 s, to 2% after 739 s: natural cooling of an `acf_` entity is slow, your `HeatCoolHotBoost` 1.3 and `HeatCoolMultACF` 1). So the radiators are now what decides the rate of fire, which is what "cooling is a tad too easy" asked for;
  - a second shot after a wait of (seconds): 1 -> 153.4%, 2 -> 152.9%, 5 -> 151.2%, 10 -> 148.5%, 30 -> 139.1%, 60 -> 127.9%, **200 -> 100.0%**. Heat from the first shot is still there for a long time.

A 300 mm PPC takes 82% and a 200 mm 49%. Ways to fix it if you want it fixed: a lower `PPCHeatSmall` than `PPCHeatBig` (the knobs are a straight line from 20 mm to 300 mm, so this also moves the 100 mm), a different `PPCMassExponent` (moves the mass of every size, the 100 mm too), or a minimum caliber. I would not touch it until you have seen the 100 mm.

If you also want the cooling slower: `HeatCoolMultACF` (1 now [SEEN, heat file line 63]: it is the natural cooling speed of capacitors, generators and PPCs; lazers have their own `HeatCoolMultLazer` and radiators `HeatCoolMultRad`, so changing it leaves the lazers alone) or the radiators' own numbers. Question V11.
