**What 16.2 is (its HEAT numbers, 5.616 / 51% per 100 mm shot / 102% after two, are replaced by 16.3 above; everything else in it holds; your Fri Oct 30 06:33 message and the 06:55 one; CUMULATIVE on 15 to 16.1; 16.1 is installed and "they seem to spawn fine, shoot and deal damage"; everything of 16.2 is untested in game).**
- **Your messages, recorded:** (06:33) "ok, they seem to spawn fine, shoot and deal damage, i need them to take 1.3x the amount of energy to fire than they do now, change the knobs to accomplish this, i need them to generate heat, look at lazers for reference, and we will go from there, basically need all of its managing scalars except for the more beam of light focused ones. i basically want a 100mm ppc to be overcharged and dealing damage to itself just like lazers but the kill effect is reduced to about a third of what it is, it makes enough heat that shooting a 100mm ppc twice without cooling linked, sets it ~102%, shooting again without cooling risks damage. also i need fire sounds, is there anything that sounds like a burst of plasma with electricity in hl2? not to mention explosion noise and effects may need changed later.., we will see how that works later." (06:55) "make that take 2.5x the energy, not 1.3".
- **WHAT I DID WITH EACH PART (my reading; tell me if one is wrong):**
  1. **"take 2.5x the amount of energy to fire than they do now ... change the knobs" (the 06:55 message replaces the 1.3 of the 06:33 one).** "Now" = 16.1, so a shot costs 2.5 times what it cost: `PPCEnergySmall` 200 to **500** and `PPCEnergyBig` 80,000 to **200,000** (the lazer's draw times 5 now, one second of it; 16 and 16.1 had it times 2). Only those two knobs moved; the curve is the same, so EVERY size costs exactly 2.5 times its old price (a test checks 9 sizes). A 100 mm shot is **16,452 kJ** (was 6,581), a 300 mm shot 200,000 kJ (was 80,000), a 20 mm shot 500 (was 200). **CORE FIX** (a knob, not code). What it means for your capacitors: a 100 mm shot needs a capacitor at least 26 units across (16,565 kJ) or two smaller ones linked (the pool is shared); a 200 mm shot needs 38 across; **the biggest capacitor (60 across, 203,575 kJ) holds one 300 mm shot and 1.8% of another**, so a 300 mm PPC fires once per full capacitor.
  2. **THE BLAST FOLLOWS THE ENERGY (something you did not ask for; read this one).** In 16 the explosion's size is the shot's energy over 8000 kJ per kg of HE (`PPCBlastScalar` 1), and what the blast heats is its kJ times `PPCHeatScalar`. So with 2.5 times the energy the HE is **2.5 times bigger as well**: a 100 mm shot is **2.06 kg of HE** (was 0.82), its blast radius **400 units / 10.1 m** (was 295 / 7.5 m), and the heat it gives a prop at the centre **25,853 kJ** with your lazer numbers (was 10,341). I left it because that is how you specified the PPC in 16 ("damage relative to the amount of charge used to fire"). **If you wanted the shot to cost more but hit as before, set `PPCBlastScalar` 0.4 and `PPCHeatScalar` 0.04 in the config** (the 16.1 damage and heat come back exactly; the energy stays 2.5 times). Question V1.
  3. **"I need them to generate heat, look at lazers for reference ... all of its managing scalars except for the more beam of light focused ones".** The PPC now has the lazer's heat behaviour with its own knobs (block `THE PPC'S OWN HEAT` in the config, every one optional): **`PPCHeatSmall` / `PPCHeatBig`** (kJ of heat made per kJ of a shot, 5.616 at both ends, a straight line between), **`PPCHeatCapF`** (900 F: its 100%, the same number as your `HeatLazerCapF`), **`PPCHeatDamage`** (4: percent of MAX health lost per second at 200%, growing with the square of how far over it is, like `HeatLazerDamage`), **`PPCHeatExplodeChance`** (0.8, your `HeatExplodeChance`), **`PPCHeatExplosionMult`** (1/3), `PPCHeatDeathDebrisPower` (20000, like the lazer's), `PPCHeatFiringSeconds` (5). Left out because they are about the beam: the lazer's heat per second of beam time, the beam's hit multiplier on plates, the plate pass-gate (`HeatPen...`) and the focus. Shared with the lazers (read from the lazer's knobs, not copied): the TURBO damage past 120% (`HeatTurboStartPct`, `HeatTurboPct`, `HeatTurboPower`), the check clock (`HeatCheckInterval`, `HeatMaxDeltaTime`), the readout clock (`HeatOutputInterval`), the cooling laws (`HeatCoolTau`, `HeatCoolSpeed`, `HeatCoolHotBoost`, `HeatCoolMultACF`) and the ignite rules. Heat capacity is the PPC's mass (kg x `HeatSpecificHeat`), like everything. **A shot adds its heat at the moment it is RELEASED and PAID** (a dud shot with no energy, or one ACF has no slot for, heats nothing). **Radiators cool it with no link at all**: a radiator cools every entity with a physics object in its contraption and radius except players, NPCs and radiators ([SEEN] `acf_radiator/init.lua` ~467; a test runs the heat store, not the radiator, so this part is untested). **Wire outputs `HeatKJ`, `HeatF`, `HeatPct`** and the hover lines **Temperature** and **Heat per shot**, with a red "Overheated: taking damage" over 100%, like the lazer's. The PPC tool's text under the slider says what one shot does to the PPC itself.
  4. **"a 100mm ppc ... overcharged and dealing damage to itself just like lazers ... it makes enough heat that shooting a 100mm ppc twice without cooling linked, sets it ~102%".** `PPCHeatSmall` / `Big` 5.616 is worked out (with your `HeatCoolHotBoost` 1.3, `HeatLazerCapF` 900 and the shipped cooling) so a 100 mm PPC that fires two shots **one second apart** (its `PPCRefireSeconds`) with nothing cooling it sits at **101.85%**: 51.09% for one shot, then a second of natural cooling, then another 51.09%. A test runs exactly that through the real code. **This number goes WITH the energy knobs: if you change the shot's energy again, change the heat knobs the opposite way** (twice the energy, half the 5.616) to keep the table below; I wrote that next to the knob.
  5. **"shooting again without cooling risks damage".** At 102% it hurts itself hardly at all (the quadratic part: about 0.0014% of its max health per second; at 110% 0.04%, at 120% 0.16%). **The third shot, a second later, takes it to 152%, and from 120% the lazers' TURBO law starts: at 152% that is 84% of its max health per second, so it is dead in about a second and a half; a fourth shot at 202% is 453% per second.** That is the lazer's law with the lazer's knobs. If "risks damage" meant something gentler, the way to get it is a PPC-only turbo (new knobs) or a higher `HeatTurboStartPct` (which moves the lazers too). Question V4.
  6. **"the kill effect is reduced to about a third of what it is".** When its own heat kills it: if it fired within `PPCHeatFiringSeconds` (5 s) or Fire is held, with chance `PPCHeatExplodeChance` (0.8) it explodes with **`PPCHeatExplosionMult` 1/3 of the HE a lazer of the same size makes** when it explodes (a 100 mm PPC: 0.274 kg of HE, the lazer's is 0.823; the size follows the lazer's knobs, so retuning those retunes this one), it clears the props it carried with the same filler, draws ACF's explosion picture, lets go of its constraints and is removed; otherwise it just dies and the pieces fly (`ACF.APKill`, debris power `PPCHeatDeathDebrisPower` capped by `MaxDebrisPower`). The death runs once. The explosion never hurts the PPC itself (it is in its own filter), like the lazer's.
  7. **"fire sounds ... a burst of plasma with electricity in hl2?"** Every released shot plays TWO Half-Life 2 sounds at the PPC, both on the game's own sound list so no file has to be added: the burst **`ambient/energy/whiteflash.wav`** and, on top, a random one of the electric crackles **`ambient/energy/zap1.wav` ... `zap9.wav`** (there is no zap4). Level 100 dB (`PPCFireSoundLevel`), volume 1 and 0.8, pitch by size (`PPCFirePitchSmall` 125 at 20 mm, `PPCFirePitchBig` 75 at 300 mm: a big PPC sounds deeper; 100 mm is 111), `CHAN_AUTO` so the tail of one shot does not cut the next. Each layer's knob takes a text (one sound), a list `{ "a.wav", "b.wav" }` (one picked at random per shot) or `""` / `{}` / `false` (silent). **I checked the 9 paths against the Garry's Mod wiki's Half-Life 2 sound list [SEEN, read today]; I have NOT heard them: the choice is a GUESS by name.** To audition one, type `play ambient/energy/whiteflash.wav` in the game console. Other candidates on the same list: `ambient/levels/citadel/portal_beam_shoot1.wav` .. `6` (a Citadel portal beam), `ambient/levels/citadel/weapon_disintegrate1.wav` .. `4`, `ambient/levels/labs/electric_explosion1.wav` .. `5` (a loud electrical bang, more an explosion), `ambient/energy/weld1.wav`, `weld2.wav`, `spark1.wav` .. `6`; the physcannon's `weapons/physcannon/superphys_launch1.wav` .. `4` [KNOW, not checked on the list]. Question V6.
  8. **"explosion noise and effects may need changed later".** NOT built, as you said. The explosion still looks and sounds as ACF's own HE does. Recorded under LATER.

(2) A PPC is an `acf_` entity, so it cools at `HeatCoolMultACF` times a prop's speed, not at the radiator's. - **WHAT I DID WITH EACH PART (my reading; tell me if one is wrong):**
  1. **THE CRASH (fixed, a core fix).** ACF's `Ballistics.BulletClient` builds its effect with `EntIndex = not IsUpdate and Bullet.Crate or nil` and then calls `EffectData:SetEntIndex`, which needs a NUMBER. ACF's own ammo crates put `Entity:EntIndex()` in `Bullet.Crate`; the PPC put the ENTITY there (a userdata): that is your error. The PPC now passes `self:EntIndex()`. The test now runs ACF's real `effects_sh.lua` on an EffectData that refuses anything but a number, so the same mistake fails the test. [SEEN] ACF's client effect (`acf_bullet_effect.lua:51`) turns that number back into the crate entity with `Data:GetEntity()`, so the ball's material and life hook on the client still get an entity.
  2. **AUTOMATIC FOCUS (lazer, new knobs `LazerAutoFocus` true and `LazerFocusSpeed` 1000, both optional).** While a lazer fires, at the end of every beam tick (`ENT:AutoFocus`, called at the end of `FireLaserBeam`) its focal length moves toward the distance from the muzzle to the FIRST thing the beam hit, by at most 1000 units a second times the tick's seconds. Both the target and the current value are clamped to the lazer's focal limits first (`GetFocalLimits`: 200 up to 3/7 of the effective range, the same limits as the tool's slider), so a wall 50 units away focuses it to 200 and a wall 10,000 units away to the far limit; the slider on the tool is now where the lazer STARTS (its label says so). A beam that hits nothing leaves the focal length where it is. `self.Focal` (what the damage curve and the duplicator use), the NetworkVar `FocalLength` (what the clients and the tool show) and the wire output `FocalLength` all follow. The damage of a tick uses the focal length that tick STARTED with (the move happens after the damage: one tick of lag, which is 1/20 s). `LazerAutoFocus false` (or 0) = the old behaviour; `LazerFocusSpeed 0` = frozen. A burn-through beam keeps focusing on the FIRST plate, not on the last thing it reaches (question U2).
  3. **PPC SAFEZONES, COMPLETELY.** One set of rules, now in the config and shared by the lazer and the PPC (the lazer's own copy is gone: its init.lua aliases the shared functions). An entity counts as protected when it carries a safezone flag (`ACFLazers.SafezoneFlagNames`: `InSafeZone`, `InSafezone`, `IsSafe`, `GetInSafezone`; a flag that is a METHOD is called and its ANSWER is used: the old lazer code counted the flag's mere existence as "yes", which was a bug) or when it stands inside one of ACF's own safezones (`ACF.EnableSafezones` and `ACF.Permissions.IsInSafezone`). For a PPC: (a) a PPC that stands in a safezone when it fires makes harmless projectiles (`PPCSafe` on the bullet): NO explosion, NO heat, the ball still flies and the picture is still drawn, and the owner gets a notice that says what the check saw; the shot still costs its energy (question U6); (b) for a PPC outside, EVERY protected entity inside the blast sphere goes into the explosion's FILTER: it takes no damage, no fragments, no push, and gets no heat; (c) people who are protected get NOTHING (the lazer's persons in a safezone take a reduced `SafezoneDamageMult` 1/6 instead: question U7); (d) ACF's own damage-permission check runs on top, as before; (e) if the safezone helper is missing (an old config) there is NO blast, never an unprotected one. [SEEN, ACF's real explosion code in my harness] a filtered entity does not shield what is behind it either (question U8).
  4. **EXPLODES ON IMPACT, NO PENETRATION.** The projectile ends on the first thing it touches (a prop, the world, a player): `PropImpact` and `WorldImpact` return false, so there is no world penetration and no prop penetration, as before; 16.1 writes the rule down and tests it. LATER (recorded, not built): it may pass light props and explode on the HEAVIEST prop in its way, which you expect to be "a significant buff".
  5. **NOT ITS OWN CONTRAPTION, BUT IT HEATS IT.** The explosion gets a COPY of the projectile's filter (the contraption list ACF keeps for the shooter, or the PPC alone when there is none), so nothing welded or parented to the PPC takes damage. (A filtered entity is also see-through to the blast's line-of-sight trace, so your contraption does not shield what is behind it either.) After the blast a SEPARATE heat pass gives heat to everything ACF knows (props, guns, engines; not people, not things already being destroyed) inside the HEAT ZONE, the blast radius times `PPCHeatRadiusMult` (1): the shooter's own contraption included, the protected ones not, and other people's things only if ACF's damage permission allows damage to them (heat is to become damage later, so it follows the same rule). The heat of one entity is the shot's kJ times the lazer's heat per kJ and hit multiplier for that size times `PPCHeatScalar` (0.1) times the age factor (1 to 0.8) times (1 minus distance over the zone's radius), distance measured to the entity's nearest point. A failing heat pass cannot stop the blast or the removal of the projectile (and the reverse).
  6. **THE SPHERE.** `PPCBallModel` = `models/sprops/geometry/sphere_3.mdl`, `PPCBallMaterial` = `models/effects/comball_sphere` ([GUESS]: the combine ball's glowing sphere material, every GMod has it). The size is now set in WORLD UNITS: `PPCBallDiameter` 10 (units across at 100 mm; 20 mm a fifth of it, 300 mm three times) over `PPCBallModelDiameter` 3 ([GUESS] how wide the model is at scale 1: it is named sphere_3). `PPCBallScale` is GONE (the toy `lz_toy ball` measures the model in your game so you can put the real number in). When you make your own model with effects, change `PPCBallModel` and `PPCBallModelDiameter` and nothing else.
  7. **"lazer_ents".** The capacitor's hover text says "Powering lazer_ents (charging is paused)" and "N capacitor(s), N lazer_ents, N generator(s)". The key the ACF link source uses inside the code is still "Lazers" (it is an internal name, not text).
  8. **THE TOYS (new client file).** `lua\autorun\client\acf_lazers_toys.lua`: a console where bits of real Lua run on your own game, with the code printed first in colours (keywords, strings, numbers, comments, calls, the big tables, punctuation) with line numbers, like an editor. Commands: `lz_toys` (list), `lz_toy <name>` (show the code lit up, then run it), `lz_toy <name> key=number` (the same with a number changed: ONLY a line that starts `local key = <number>` is rewritten, only with a real number, so nothing you type is ever run as code), `lz_toy_src <name>` (only show), `lz_toy_stop`, `lz_hl <file> [first] [last]` (any .lua file of the addon in colours: `lz_hl autorun/acf_lazers_config.lua 780 800`; `..` is refused). Five toys: `focus` (a gold ball chases what you look at at `LazerFocusSpeed`: try `speed=250`), `ball` (measures the sphere model and draws the ball at a caliber's size next to you), `blast` (draws a PPC blast and its heat zone where you look and prints the heat at each distance), `curve` (the lazer's damage along its beam as a bar chart in the console), `sizes` (every PPC size in one table). Each toy is a `Toys.<name> = { about = "...", code = [[ ... ]] }` block at the bottom of the file; copy one to make your own. **How to use it to learn:** `lz_toy focus`, read the coloured lines, then `lz_toy focus speed=250` and see the ball lag; the line that did it is the one printed with the yellow mark. It is a client file: it never touches the server, a lazer or a PPC. Question U1 is whether this is what you meant by "lit up like a glua interpreter".
  9. **LATER (your 01:58 message, recorded, NOT built):** (a) PPC recoil the way ACF does it for parented and unparented weapons, so a PPC kicks like a cannon of similar size; (b) a bigger shove on the target: HE already pushes a little, and you suspect the push should go to the base of the parent chain of the prop that was hit. Both wait until you say go. They are on the next-steps list.

- **Your message, recorded:** "i need to add PPCs, i want to make them act like an acf projectile, but without drop, the model of the projectile needs to be some energy ball, or even just an sprops sphere with a material, and a projectile delete time of about 7 seconds. i want speed of the projectile to vary between big and small, 800 m/s for big, 600m/s for small. i want muzzle diameter to range from 20 to 300,  i want the projectile to deal pure explosive damage, scalar'ed with a var, but relative to the amount of charge used to fire, (im assuming this links to capacitors just like lazers, and acts the same way as a lazer, but this only fires one projectile when it "bites") i want the affected props that got hit with the "he blast" to receive a scalar'ed with a var, amount of damage, relative to the amount of HE blast the prop received. i also want damage to scale with size, the same way how in the lazers system, used "exponential" scale for overall lazer damage per caliber. i want only a 20% reduction in damage at 7seconds of travel. (make the "7 seconds of travel then delete" thing a var, and use that same var for this part.) the model is "models/lasers/ppc.mdl" scale it at the same rate (dont make it rely on the size of lazers, just match values roughly.) as lazers model for "caliber" and make that adjustable, and make the projectile start at the same end of the gun as the lazers. we are gonna work on effects after we get the damage working as intended, we will need some electrical spark effects, and like energy surrounding a projectile kind of thing. i think we will make PPCs take around the same amount of energy per caliber as lazers but doubled,  make sure to add the relevant vars."
- **WHAT I DID WITH EACH PART (my reading; tell me if one is wrong):**
  1. **"act like an acf projectile, but without drop":** the shot IS an ACF bullet (`ACF.Ballistics.CreateBullet`). It gets a ZERO acceleration (ACF uses its gravity when none is given: that is what makes bullets drop) and no drag, so it flies in a straight line at its muzzle velocity until it hits something or its time is up. Everything ACF does for bullets applies: its filters, its limit on bullets in the air, the hooks other addons use, the flying model drawn by ACF's own client effect.
  2. **[REPLACED BY 16.1: the ball is sphere_3, sized in units; see item 6 of the 16.1 block] "the model of the projectile needs to be some energy ball":** `PPCBallModel`, shipped as `models/effects/combineball.mdl` ([GUESS]: the Half-Life 2 energy ball, it carries its own glowing material). `PPCBallMaterial` (empty = the model's own) is put over it if you set one, so an SProps sphere plus a glowing material works too. `PPCBallScale` 0.25 is its size at 100 mm (20 mm: 0.2 of that, 300 mm: 3 times). All three are knobs because I cannot see the model's real size from here.
  3. **"projectile delete time of about 7 seconds":** `PPCLifetime` 7. ACF removes the projectile quietly when the time is up (no explosion). The client's ball is told to stop at the same time (ACF would otherwise keep drawing it for 45 s: that part rests on an ACF internal field, see the risks).
  4. **"800 m/s for big, 600 for small":** `PPCSpeedSmall` 600 at the smallest PPC, `PPCSpeedBig` 800 at the biggest, a straight line between: 100 mm is 657 m/s. The speed of the vehicle the PPC sits on is added, as for ACF guns.
  5. **"muzzle diameter 20 to 300":** `PPCCaliberMin` 20, `PPCCaliberMax` 300: the tool's slider (the entity clamps it again on the server).
  6. **"pure explosive damage, scalar'ed with a var, relative to the amount of charge used to fire":** the end of the flight (it hit anything) is ONE HE explosion made with ACF's own `Damage.createExplosion` and nothing else: no penetration, no ricochet, no kinetic damage. The explosive filler is the energy of the shot over ACF's kJ per kg of HE (`ACF.HEPower`, 8000) times `PPCBlastScalar` (1 = the shot's kJ become HE at ACF's rate). So the blast follows the charge used: a 100 mm shot (6,581 kJ) is 0.82 kg of HE, a 300 mm shot (80,000 kJ) is 10 kg. "Pure" is taken literally: ACF's HE also throws FRAGMENTS, and those are switched off (`PPCFragmentScalar` 0; 1 would be ACF's own HE round).
  7. **"links to capacitors just like lazers ... only fires one projectile when it 'bites'":** the PPC is linked with the same tools and takes its energy from the same capacitor group as a lazer, but ALL AT ONCE: a shot costs `GetPPCShotEnergy` kJ, taken from the pool the moment the projectile is made. "Bites" I read as: the moment the shot is released (Fire going from 0 to above 0, and the pool can pay). Nothing is taken if ACF could not make the projectile. Holding Fire repeats the shot every `PPCRefireSeconds` 1 (`PPCAutoRepeat` true); false = one shot per press (question S1).
  8. **"the affected props that got hit with the HE blast receive a scalar'ed amount of damage, relative to the amount of HE blast the prop received":** ACF already works out, for every prop the blast reaches, a damage that follows how close it was and how much of the prop the blast covers. `PPCPropDamageScalar` (1) multiplies THAT, per prop, so it is "relative to the blast the prop received". It is done with ACF's own `ACF_OnDamageEntity` hook, on the damage of OUR explosion only (an explosion started by something else, a prop blown up by ours for instance, is not touched).
  9. **"damage to scale with size, the same way the lazers used an exponential scale":** the SHOT ENERGY follows the lazer's draw shape: a mix of a straight line and an exponential between `PPCEnergySmall` and `PPCEnergyBig`, set by `PPCEnergyCurve` 0.75 (the same number as `LazerDrawCurve`). The HE follows the energy, so the damage per shot grows with size the way the lazer's draw does. (Question S2: damage is tied to energy, not to a separate curve.)
  10. **"only a 20% reduction in damage at 7 seconds of travel (the same var)":** the damage of the explosion is multiplied by a factor that falls in a straight line from 1.0 at the muzzle to `PPCDamageAtEnd` 0.8 after `PPCLifetime` seconds of flight: 0.9 at 3.5 s, 0.8 at 7 s. The time comes from the SAME `PPCLifetime` (the projectile is deleted when the factor reaches its end). It scales the damage, not the blast's radius. It covers the blast and the fragments both.
  11. **"models/lasers/ppc.mdl, scale at the same rate as the lazer's model for caliber, adjustable, not relying on the lazer's size":** `PPCModel`, and `PPCCaliberBase` 100: the scale is the caliber over this number (20 mm 0.2, 100 mm 1.0, 300 mm 3.0), the same rate as the lazer's model. Its own knob; the lazer's numbers are not read. [GUESS] the model's own barrel looks like about an 85 mm one (your Oct 19 note: 0.88 scale = 75 mm): set `PPCCaliberBase` 85 if you want it to match exactly (question S3). The mass is the lazer's curve with the PPC's own knobs (`PPCMassMin` 60 to `PPCMassMax` 9000, exponent 2), and the armour and health multipliers are the lazer's.
  12. **"start at the same end of the gun as the lazers":** the projectile starts at the front of the model (`OBBMaxs().x`, 63 units in front of the origin at scale 1), the same rule the lazer's beam uses; it grows with the scale.
  13. **"around the same amount of energy per caliber as lazers but doubled":** one shot = ONE SECOND of the lazer's draw, doubled: 200 kJ at 20 mm, 6,581 at 100 mm, 80,000 at 300 mm (a test keeps it at exactly twice the lazer's draw at every size). It is its own set of knobs (`PPCEnergySmall`, `PPCEnergyBig`, `PPCEnergyCurve`), so it does not follow the lazer's if you change those.
  14. **"we are gonna work on effects after we get the damage working":** NOT in. No spark effects, no energy around the ball, no firing or impact sounds, no recoil, no muzzle flash. What you see is the ball (the model) and ACF's own explosion picture.

(2) The blast radius grows with the cube root of the filler (ACF's law), so doubling `PPCBlastScalar` makes it 26% wider and the damage per prop follows what ACF works out for that bigger blast. (3) [16.1: the BLAST now heats things, the shooter's contraption included; the PPC itself still makes no heat of its own from firing (question S7)].   1. **"60 in diameter":** the biggest roundsink is a DIAMETER of 60, a radius of 30. The slider stays the RADIUS (you called it the radius everywhere else), so it runs **12 to 30** (a diameter of 24 to 60) and starts at 13 (a diameter of 26). `RadRoundMax` 60 -> 30. I took the 12 as a radius (a diameter of 24) because you only corrected the 60; if the 12 was a diameter too (radius 6), set `RadRoundMin` 6 (question Q5). So the diameter cannot be mixed up again, the toolgun screen, the menu text and the hover text now show both ("radius 13 (diameter 26)"). A 60 on the slider (a leftover setting, a console command) is held at radius 30.
  2. **"dont know what radbeyondmax does":** it was a 1/0 switch for what happens to a roundsink that holds MORE volume than the biggest heatsink (60 wide). It was my own invention and I explained it badly. **It is gone.** The rule it switched, which was on in what I sent, is always on now (next point). A config that still has the line RadBeyondMax works: the line is ignored (a test checks that 0, 1, text and nothing all give the same numbers).
  3. **"scale weight by comparing against the volumes of the cube version":** this is the rule, for every size: the roundsink's volume is compared with the cube version's (the heatsink's). Up to the biggest cube (60 wide, 154,286 cubic units, 650 kg, 125,000 kJ/s) it weighs, and works, like the heatsink of the same volume. Past it, its weight is 650 kg TIMES how many times that volume it has (twice the volume, twice the weight), the sap rate is 125,000 kJ/s times the same number, and the reach goes on along its line. With the radius at most 30 the most it can have is 1.95 times the biggest cube: 1,264 kg, 243,171 kJ/s, reach 955. The waste, the tank and the hiss pitch follow the heatsink of the same volume (the tank is an eighth of the volume).
  4. **"keep my dims":** 13 and 46 are used exactly as you gave them (the mesh in the file measures about 13.8 x 46.2; your numbers win; this answers old question Q3). The model is still scaled EVENLY, like the heatsink: the height grows with the radius (46 tall at radius 13, 106 tall at radius 30). If "keep my dims" meant that the HEIGHT stays 46 and only the radius grows, say so (old question Q4, still open): that would be a flat disc at radius 30 (60 wide, 46 tall) with 0.84 times the volume of the biggest heatsink, and the rule past the biggest heatsink would never apply.

- **Your message, recorded:** "ALSO i need to implement a second model for the lazer radiators, i need a model selector for the tool, that lets me swap between the two.  the model is "models/lasers/roundsink.mdl" and it is cylinder shaped, starting radius is 13, height is 46, and i want it to act the same exact way as the other radiators per volume. radius ranging from 12 - 60"
- **WHAT YOU GET (still true).** A radiator has a KIND: `heatsink` (the box, as before) or `roundsink` (the cylinder). The radiator tool has a drop-down at the top of its menu ("Heatsink (box)" / "Roundsink (cylinder)"). Picking one puts the slider at that model's start (heatsink 42 wide, roundsink radius 13), relabels it ("Width (units)" / "Radius (units)") and gives it that model's range (heatsink 12 - 60 wide; roundsink 12 - 30 radius since 15.9, it was 12 - 60 in 15.8). Left-click spawns the picked kind; left-click on a radiator of the OTHER kind changes it in place (the model, scale, mass and tank follow, the coolant level stays, links stay).
- **"THE SAME PER VOLUME" (my reading; unchanged).** A radiator is worked out as the HEATSINK THAT HAS THE SAME VOLUME. A heatsink's volume is the box 42 x 42 x 30 at scale 1; a roundsink's is the cylinder pi x 13 x 13 x 46; both grow with the cube of the scale. The roundsink's volume is turned into the width of a heatsink with that volume (`ACFLazers.GetRadEquivWidth`), and mass, reach, sap rate, waste, flush pitch and the coolant tank follow THAT width through the heatsink's curves. A roundsink and a heatsink with equal volume are equal in every number. The heatsink's own numbers are unchanged to the last digit (all 49 widths are pinned against the 15.7 formulas, with and without the kind).
- **WHAT IS KEPT WHEN YOU UPDATE.** An update from the tool always carries the picked kind. An update whose kind is missing or not one of ours (a dupe or another tool) keeps the radiator's current model; the tool treats anything but the exact word `roundsink` as the heatsink.
- **OLD DUPES.** The duplicator is told to save `Kind` (a plain field, like `Width`). A dupe made before 15.8 has no kind and comes out as a heatsink 100% as before. [GUESS, in game] that ACF's registration passes the saved string back to the maker: it does for `Width` and the colour numbers, but `Kind` is the first string among the saved keys (T269).

- **Your messages, recorded.** 21:32: "ok so undo that last change to lazers init and radiator init, give me the version before, cause it broke the entities entirely". 22:34: "that seemed to have unfucked it, now try to figure out, why that change, made the lazers and radiators default to whatever defaults were, cause they were both informationless, and stock size." 23:14: "not worried about broken dupes, im still only using old dupes, no new dupes got made on the broken system", then your two values (`HeatLazerSmall = 10,` and `HeatMaxDeltaTime = 0.25,`) and "also i need a cvar for the limits of all of these entities. minimum on all should be 40 initially." 23:20: "also, i think i was imagining the cool rate problem, cause it seems fixed now, i really just want the output value of lazers to be updated at a constant .1s interval like when its firing so its not so confusing."
- **THE STATE YOU HAVE NOW (read from your machine on Oct 28).** The lazer and radiator init files are the 15.4 pair (byte-identical to the `out15_4` files; the revert I sent). Your config is 122,828 bytes: my 15.4 config plus your values, and it has NO `HeatOutputInterval` and NO `LazerOverlayDelay` (the 15.5 lines were never pasted), `CapMaxPerPlayer` 20, `GenMaxPerPlayer` 10, `RadMaxPerPlayer` 10, and (at the time I read it) `HeatLazerSmall` 15 and `HeatMaxDeltaTime` 0.5, so the two values you gave me at 23:14 were not in the file yet. `lua\autorun\server\acf_lazers_limits.lua` (121 bytes) makes `sbox_maxacf_lazers` at 20.
- **WHY 15.6 BROKE THE ENTITIES (root cause; my mistake, disclosed).** The 15.5 lazer file and the 15.6 lazer and radiator files began with a load check: if the config had no `HeatOutputInterval` / `LazerOverlayDelay`, print one red line and `return` from `init.lua`. Your config did not have them. I had never looked at your config on disk before I built 15.6, and I wrote in the 15.6 document that you had pasted them: I had no basis for that, and I also made the radiator depend on those lines for no good reason (the 15.5 lazer fix did not need the radiator). A `return` at the top of `init.lua` is not "the entity does not load": `shared.lua` is already included, and what stays behind is a **shell class**. Measured in the harness with your config (`sim/diag_bare_lazer.lua`, `sim/diag_bare_rad.lua`): the lazer class keeps 4 functions (`GetBeamDirection`, `GetCaliber`, `GetMuzzlePos`, `SetupDataTables`), the radiator class keeps 1 (`GetWidth`); `Initialize`, `ACF_UpdateOverlayState`, `TriggerInput`, `Think`, the wire outputs and the scaling code are missing; and `Entities.Register` (the last lines of the file, which registers ACF's duplicator hook for the class) is never called. What that means in game ([KNOW] from the wiki page of `GenericDuplicatorFunction`, which applies only the model and the position; the harness cannot show it, and I have not seen it in game): a duplicated lazer or radiator is created with no `Initialize` (no wire inputs or outputs), no `SetScale` (so the model's stock size), no `ACF_UpdateOverlayState` (ACF shows hover info only if the entity has it, `overlay_sv.lua:48`: so no information), and the shared getters answer from the config defaults (`GetCaliber` gives `CaliberBase`, `GetWidth` gives `RadWidthDefault`). That is exactly your "informationless, and stock size, defaults". A spawn from the tool had no maker either (`MakeACFLazer` / `MakeACFRadiator` are defined further down the same file, after the `return`). You told me you only use old dupes and made no new ones on the broken system, so nothing needs repair.
- **THE FIX (core for this failure).** The two readout knobs are OPTIONAL now. The lazer file uses `0.1 s` / `0.25 s` when `HeatOutputInterval` / `LazerOverlayDelay` are missing (or not numbers); the radiator timer uses `0.1 s` when `HeatOutputInterval` is missing. Nothing about the readout can stop either file any more, and the 15.5 lines are NOT needed. **What is still a shell hazard (not fixed, not a regression):** the OTHER load checks in the entity files (no config, no heat file, an old config without a helper that the file calls) still `return` at the top and leave a shell class, in all four entity files. Those checks are right to refuse (the file would throw on every tick otherwise) but the way they refuse leaves a shell that looks like a working entity. The proper fix would make a refusing file leave nothing that duplicates can attach to (see question O1). Bandaid level of what I did: **none for the readout (it removes the cause); the refusal pattern itself is untouched.**
- **THE CONSTANT 0.1 s LAZER READOUT (your 23:20; this is the 15.5 design, delivered safely).** The three heat wire outputs of a lazer (`HeatKJ`, `HeatF`, `HeatPct`) and its hover text are refreshed on their own clock, every `HeatOutputInterval` = **0.1 s**, whether the lazer is firing, wants to fire (Fire held on an empty pool) or idles, while it holds heat, and once more when it has cooled to room temperature (so the readout ends at 0 kJ / 59 F / 0%). The radiators have the same clock (one timer for all of them). The 15.5 finding stands: the REAL heat was identical in every state (the heat store is exact); only the readout lagged (up to a second when idle). You now think the cooling-rate problem was imagined; that matches the measurement (nothing about cooling changed in any version since 15.4). **NOT on this clock:** the lazer's `Energy` output (written every `LazerEnergyReadInterval`, 0.25 s while Fire is held or wanted, once a SECOND when idle): see question O2.
- **THE LIMITS (your 23:14).** One server variable per entity, the sandbox convention `sbox_max<kind>`: `sbox_maxacf_lazers`, `sbox_maxacf_capacitors`, `sbox_maxacf_generators`, `sbox_maxacf_radiators`. Now ONE more timer for all radiators (`ACFLazers_RadiatorReadout`, every `HeatOutputInterval` = **0.1 s**, never less than 0.02 s) calls `HeatReadout` on each radiator: while the radiator holds heat it writes the three outputs (`UpdateHeatOutputs`), and once more when it has cooled to room temperature (so the readout ends at 0 kJ, 59 F, 0%); a radiator at room temperature that already shows 0 costs one table lookup per interval. `UpdateWireOutputs` no longer writes them (one owner, like the lazer). **No new knob: it is the 15.5 `HeatOutputInterval` knob (which was NOT in your config: that was the breakage, see 15.7).** The radiator file refuses to load without it (one red console line that names it and `config_lines_15_5.txt`). Fixed: the flag starts as "not yet shown", so the first readout writes 0 kJ / 59 F / 0% once (lazer and radiator). A hot lazer, an idle one, a dud: unchanged. Pinned by new tests (t_heat H6c, t_rad R19). Effect for you: only a lazer that was never heated showed 0 F; the readouts you looked at (hot ones) were right.

**What 15.5 is (your Tue Oct 27 05:28 message: "the only thing that seems off, is the timing for how much heat gets taken from lazers, if im not inputing fire, it seems to count its heat down slower, like the heat cycle does not react as fast as it should, leading to the lazer being more hot, but if im inputing fire, and the lazer cannot discharge because capacitor isnt ready for its bite, it cools down faster. it seems to happen at some rate related to the bookkeeping vars. cooling is at .1s intervals, where as fucked up slow cooling is at .5 sec intervals, 5x less cooling or something."):**
- **Your reports first (Oct 27 05:13 - 05:28), recorded as facts about your setup:** you changed `HeatCoolHotBoost` (0.8, then **1.3**), `HeatLazerSmall` **15** / `HeatLazerBig` **30** (was 5 / 5), `HeatLazerCapF` **900** (was 1100) and `HeatExplodeChance` **0.8** (was 0.5) in your own config, and you wrote "the purge coolant system works as intended now" (I take that as T245 - T247 of 15.4 reported working; which parts you tested, and what `RadFlushKJPerLiter` you ended with, I do not know: M3). **Your local config differs from mine in these five values (and possibly `RadFlushKJPerLiter`): any future FULL config I send must carry them, so 15.5 does NOT replace your config file: it gives you two lines to paste.**
- **What was wrong (found by reproducing it, `sim/diag_readout.lua`).** Your numbers were right and your guess about the bookkeeping was close: the REAL heat is identical in both states (a lazer idle and a lazer with Fire held on an empty capacitor have the same kJ after 6 s, ratio 1.000000000: the heat store is exact, `ACFLazers.HeatCool` gives the same answer for one big step or many small ones, and nothing thinks per entity). What differed was the READOUT: the three wire outputs `HeatKJ` / `HeatF` / `HeatPct` and the hover text were written by `UpdateWireOutputs`, which only runs from `RefreshPower`, and `RefreshPower` waits `LazerEnergyReadInterval` (0.25 s) while the lazer is firing or Fire is wanted, but a hard-coded **1 second** when it is idle. On top of that ACF itself rebuilds a hover text at most once per `OverlayDelay` (ACF's base: 1 s). So an idle lazer's readout was up to 1 s behind the real heat (harness: up to 17.8 points of its limit behind while cooling fast, a dud 6.4 points), and a lazer that was just heated from outside showed 0 for up to a second. For anything that reads `HeatPct` (an E2, a purge controller) a lazer then looks hotter than it is and reacts late. My numbers: the idle interval is 1.0 s, not 0.5 s, and the "fast" one is 0.25 s, not 0.1 s; the 0.1 and 0.5 that sit in the config under the heat knobs are `HeatCheckInterval` (the lazer's self-damage check, 0.1 s) and `HeatMaxDeltaTime` (a cap on one check's time step, 0.5 s): neither touches the readout or the heat store.
- **The fix (core, not a bandaid).** The heat readout got its OWN clock: `HeatReadout(now)` runs from the lazer's `Think` and, every `HeatOutputInterval` (new knob, **0.1 s**) while the lazer holds heat, writes the three outputs (`UpdateHeatOutputs`, which `UpdateWireOutputs` no longer does: one owner) and asks for the hover text; when the lazer has cooled to room temperature it does ONE more refresh (the readout ends at 0 kJ, 59 F, 0%) and then only a table lookup per interval. The lazer's own hover-text buffer is the new knob `LazerOverlayDelay` (**0.25 s**; `ENT.OverlayDelay`, the same field ACF's piledriver sets to 0.1), so the hover text is at most 0.25 s old instead of 1 s. Nothing about the heat itself changed: no cooling, radiator or flush number is touched. Harness: with the old lazer file the new tests reproduce your report (idle readout 17.8 points behind, dud 6.4, heat arriving from outside shown after 1.0 s vs 0.26 s); with the fix every readout is at most `HeatOutputInterval` + two ticks old in all three states (idle, dud, Fire released).
- **Cost.** A hot lazer asks for its hover text up to 10 times a second; ACF buffers that to at most 4 rebuilds a second, and sends the result only to players who are looking at it. A cold lazer costs one table lookup per 0.1 s.
- **What I did NOT change (tell me if you want it) [15.6: the radiators' heat outputs DID get the 0.1 s clock, see above].** The capacitor, generator and radiator readouts were checked: their wire outputs are not gated by Fire (capacitor every `CapThinkInterval` 0.1 s, generator every `GenOutputInterval` 0.25 s, radiator every sweep 0.25 s), so they do not have this problem; but their HOVER texts still use ACF's 1-second buffer (question M2).
- **Radiators measured (`sim/diag_radreadout.lua`; your follow-up: you watched the lazers AND the radiators in the debugger).** A radiator writes all its outputs at the end of every sweep (`RadSweepInterval` 0.25 s) whether it is hot, cold or idle: no gate. Four cases (idle and self-cooling from 100% of HOT; the same with a cold prop in range; working on a 5,000 kg prop at HOT; at 60% with a hot prop in range): the output changed 4.0 times a second in every one, and was never more than 1.0 point of HOT behind the real heat (1.15 with your HotBoost 1.3; 0.5 working at 60%). So radiators never had the idle-versus-firing difference. After 15.5 a lazer's heat steps every 0.1 s and a radiator's every 0.25 s; if radiators look steppy next to lazers, that is the sweep (question M2).
- **What to paste.** `config_lines_15_5.txt`: two lines into your `acf_lazers_config.lua`, inside the config table right after the line `HeatMaxDeltaTime    = 0.5,`: `HeatOutputInterval  = 0.1,` and `LazerOverlayDelay   = 0.25,` (each with its comment; the file has them ready). Then `acf_lazer_init.new.lua` over `lua\entities\acf_lazer\init.lua`. If you forget the two lines the lazer refuses to load with one red console line that names them.
- **STATUS: UNTESTED IN GAME.** Offline (my harness): t_heat 428 (H6c NEW: the readout clock in all three states, the knob sets the clock at 0.5 s and at 0.02 s, the hover text asked for on the same clock, room temperature ends at 0 and then nearly nothing, the death check comes first, a server without Wire, the load check for the two knobs, the shipped values; H12 now expects version 15.5), t_pen 88 (the lazer announces 15.5), t_cool 468, t_ignite 111, t_rad 2241, t_rad_pins 104, t_radui 259, t_gen 350, t_tools 74, t_toolui 57, t_lazer_draw 51, t_life 21: **all 4,252 pass, 0 failed.** Backups of the 15.4 versions: `t_heat.lua.bak154`, `t_pen.lua.bak154`. **Mutation testing:** **36 deliberate breakages of the 15.5 code** (the two knob values, the readout interval taken from the wrong knob, the readout never called / only while firing / only while Fire is wanted / before the death check, never rescheduled, the final refresh at room temperature, the shown flag never or always set, each of the three outputs not written or in the wrong unit, the energy read writing the heat again, the Wire guard, the hover-text buffer not set / hard-coded to ACF's 1 s / from the wrong knob, the load check for each knob, its message and its stop, the version line), run with `sim/mutate155.py`. The first pass let 4 through: two became tests (the readout runs after the death check; a server without Wire), one was redundant code that I DELETED instead (the energy read also wrote the heat outputs: now one owner), and 1 is EQUIVALENT (the shown flag starts false instead of true: a lazer that is cold at its first readout is refreshed once more, and nothing differs). **Final pass (`sim/mut155_new.log`): 35 caught, 1 equivalent, 0 other survivors, 0 skipped.**
- **What your five values do (computed with the real config maths on a copy of the 15.4 tree with your five values: `sim/cfg_numbers.lua` with a `wuser` tree for the timelines, radiators and plate, `sim/cfg_numbers2.lua <tree>` for the burn times and the 115% content; the flush number from `sim/calib154.lua` re-solved at CapF 900):**
  - *Lazer heat now (kJ/s):* 20 mm 1,500; 60 mm 27,883; 100 mm 63,458; 300 mm 1,200,000 (15.4 defaults: 500 / 8,133 / 16,452 / 200,000). Seconds of continuous fire with no radiator to 500 F / 100% of the limit / 120% (the turbo): 20 mm 4.9 / 9.5 / 11.5; 60 mm 1.0 / 2.0 / 2.4; 100 mm 1.5 / 2.9 / 3.5; 300 mm 0.9 / 1.7 / 2.1. Dead after (Fire held, no radiator): 20 mm 14 s, 60 mm 3.6 s, 100 mm 4.9 s. 500 F is now 52% of a lazer's limit; the turbo starts at 1,068 F; plates still have HOT 1,100 F.
  - *Radiators needed (simple model: lazer heat minus a constant sap rate; the radiators' waste heat and the pool sharing are ignored):* a 20 mm lazer is cancelled by any radiator (a 12-wide's 1,500 kJ/s equals its heat). 60 mm: a 12-wide changes nothing (100% at 2.1 s), a 30-wide 2.8 s, a 42-wide 14.3 s (500 F at 7.3 s), a 50-wide or a 60-wide holds it cold. 100 mm: a 42-wide 100% at 4.6 s, a 50-wide 13.9 s, a 60-wide holds it. 300 mm (1.2 GJ/s): nothing holds it, only the flush.
  - *The flush at CapF 900:* a 100 mm lazer at 115% now holds 207,991 kJ (257,454 at CapF 1100). The shipped 125,000 kJ per litre takes it from 115% to **4.5%** in the 3 x 12-wide, 2 s scenario (not 25%); **100,357 kJ per litre** gives exactly 25.0% (spread with the sweep timing 14 - 25%). `RadFlushKJPerLiter` is yours to set; I have not changed it.

**What 15.4 is (your Mon Oct 26 05:22 message: "i renamed it radiator in the git, it should be fine now. DO need an exception for the lazers hitbox hitting the crew of a vehicle,  i only intend on that damaging players outside the vehicle. also i need to remove the heat overclocking fearture, im gonna focus more on coolant expulsion as a way to make radiators do the thing. dont damage them if overheated. completley strip overclocking, and just make the heat eating rate a knob variable. also make that standard heat loss rate slower, make that multiplier 1.25 instead of 1.5x. also the effects are missing texture, and the sound keeps playing after starting. i need it to stop slowly, or reduce in volume within a second and stop playing or something. oh, and i want coolant flushing to basically act as a heat obliterator, so you shit your coolant out, two seconds on three 12 sized radiators, should get a 100mm lazer back down to 25% heat,  from 115%, and radiators need to grab heat, and cool down like 5x faster on both than whatever standard is now."):**
- **Your radiator error (04:04 / 04:05: "the spawn function for acf_radiator didn't return an entity").** It meant `acf_radiator` had no entity file: the radiator files sat in `entities\acf_heatsink\`. You renamed the folder in the git. THIS delivery names the folder `entities\acf_radiator\` (init, cl_init, shared) and nothing in it is called heatsink. If your game folder still has a stray `.lua` in `acf_heatsink` from before, it does nothing and can go.
- **The crew exception (your "an exception for the lazers hitbox hitting the crew of a vehicle ... only damaging players outside the vehicle").** A player who is `ply:InVehicle()` (any seat, pod, driver seat: whatever GMod counts as a vehicle) is never set on fire by a hot lazer. The ignite check is now `ply:Alive() and (seated or not ply:InVehicle())` with `seated = CFG.HeatIgniteSeated == true` (new knob, default **false**; true = seated players burn like everybody else). A seated player is skipped BEFORE the `ACFLazers_CanIgnite` veto hook is asked, and does not renew the touch time: a player who is burning when he sits down is put out `HeatIgniteLinger` (1 s) later. **I read "hitbox" as the ignite box (the lazer's body), the thing I built in 15.3. The beam's own damage to players is untouched. If you meant the beam, tell me.**
- **The overclock is gone, completely (your "completley strip overclocking, and just make the heat eating rate a knob variable").** Removed: the `Overclock` wire input, `RadOverclockDefault` / `RadOverclockMax`, `ClampRadOverclock`, the overclock rows of `GetRadReadout`, the hover line and the tool's lines, the "switched off" state, `RadDamage`, and the "OVER ITS LIMIT" state. The heat a radiator eats per second is `RadSapSmall` (width 12) to `RadSapBig` (width 60) with `RadSapCurve`, **5x the 15.3 numbers: 1,500 and 125,000 kJ/s** (see the table below), times the strength its tank gives it. A dupe with an `Overclock` wire on a radiator just loses that wire [GUESS: Wire's dupe code ignores a link to an input that no longer exists].
- **"Dont damage them if overheated".** A radiator takes NO damage from heat at all (the quadratic law and the turbo term both skip it). It does NOT die at any temperature (the harness checks 99% up to 500% of HOT, about 5,260 F, a simulated second each: no damage at all). I kept the radiator's waste heat (`RadWasteSmall / Big` 1%) and its efficiency curve (100% up to 50% of HOT, a line down to 40% at 100% of HOT and above): at the 15.4 defaults a radiator working flat out for ever settles at 15% / 18% / 21% / 23% / 26% of HOT (12 / 30 / 42 / 50 / 60 wide), so the curve is never reached unless you put heat in it from outside (`acf_lazers_heat_set`) or raise the waste. A hot radiator still works, at the efficiency floor (250%: 40% efficiency). If you want the waste and the curve gone as well: `RadWasteSmall` / `RadWasteBig` 0 and `RadEffFloorPct` 100.
- **Natural cooling (your "make that multiplier 1.25 instead of 1.5x").** There is no 1.5 anywhere in the code: 15.3 was TWICE the speed of 15.2 (`HeatCoolTau` 150 instead of 300). I took "1.25" as the multiplier on the 15.2 speed: `HeatCoolTau` is back to 300 and the NEW knob `HeatCoolSpeed` = **1.25** sits on top of it (1 = 15.2, 2 = what 15.3 had), so the time constant is 240 s. The one place that works the rate out is `ACFLazers.GetHeatCoolRate(mult) = mult x HeatCoolSpeed / HeatCoolTau`. A prop at 800 F: 726 / 662 / 558 / 410 / 200 F after 15 / 30 / 60 / 120 / 300 s (15.3: 687 / 596 / 461 / 298 / 121). If you meant something else, `HeatCoolSpeed` is the one number to change (L1 below).
- **Radiators "grab heat, and cool down like 5x faster on both".** (a) GRAB = the sap rate x5 (1,500 / 125,000 kJ/s, above). (b) COOL DOWN = the radiator's own heat leaves 5x as fast as a prop's: new knob `HeatCoolMultRad` = **5** (`Heat.GetCoolMult` gives it to the class `acf_radiator`; lazers stay `HeatCoolMultLazer` 1, other ACF parts `HeatCoolMultACF` 1). A radiator at 1,000 F with nothing to work on: 837 / 613 / 414 / 223 / 101 F after 5 / 15 / 30 / 60 / 120 s (a prop: 964 / 897 / 810 / 670 / 480). "On both" read as these two (L2 below).
- **The flush is a heat obliterator (your "two seconds on three 12 sized radiators, should get a 100mm lazer back down to 25% heat, from 115%") [15.5: you report the purge works as intended; this calibration is for CapF 1100, at your CapF 900 the same scenario ends at 4.5% and needs about 100,357 kJ per litre for 25%].** `RadFlushKJPerLiter` 2260 -> **125,000** (about 55x). It is solved from your scenario with `sim/calib154.lua`: three 12-wide radiators (2.5 L tank each, vent 0.25 L/s each) flushing for 2.0 s from a full tank take a 100 mm lazer (790 kg, 387 kJ/K, limit 578 K) from 115% (1,256 F) to **25.1%** (about 319 F) in my harness (the exact solve is 125,196 kJ/L for 25.00%). In the game the flush flag is read once per 0.25 s sweep, so "2 seconds" is 1.75 - 2.25 s of venting: **expect about 25% +/- 5 points.** It assumes the lazer is the only hot thing in range: the heat is shared among the hot parts by temperature. A wide radiator removes far more: a 42-wide (10.8 L/s) about 1.36 GJ/s, a 60-wide (31.6 L/s) about 3.95 GJ/s, so a flush from a big radiator puts anything in range at room temperature in well under a second. That is the obliterator, on purpose; `RadFlushKJPerLiter` is the knob (L4).
- **The sound (your "the sound keeps playing after starting. i need it to stop slowly, or reduce in volume within a second and stop playing").** [GUESS at the cause] In 15.3 the opening burst was fire-and-forget (a long sample that nothing could stop or fade) and the hiss was faded with `FadeOut`, which I do not trust to stop a looping patch. Now BOTH are `CreateSound` patches (`SetSoundLevel(75)`, `PlayEx(volume, pitch)`), and when the flush ends each is faded out over `RadFlushFadeSeconds` (new knob, **0.8 s**; 0 = stop at once, at most 10) and then `Stop`ped 0.1 s after the fade ends, so it is silent at about 0.9 s whatever `FadeOut` does. A radiator that is removed while venting stops both at once. A flush that starts again during the fade gets a NEW pair (the old pair finishes fading). The sound names are unchanged (`ambient/gas/steam2.wav` burst, `ambient/gas/steam_loop1.wav` hiss).
- **The steam texture (your "the effects are missing texture").** The 15.3 sprite `particle/particle_smokegrey` was a guess and did not draw. `RadPuffMaterial` is now `RadPuffMaterials`, a LIST of ACF's own smoke sprites `particle/smokesprites_0001` .. `_0009` (a random one per puff; [SEEN] used by ACF's own effects in the reference copy of ACF I have; NOT checked in your game's files). `ACFLazers.PickRadPuffMaterial(r)` picks; a text instead of a list is that one sprite; an empty or broken knob falls back to ACF's nine. To check in game: `lua_run_cl print(Material("particle/smokesprites_0001"):IsError())` must print `false`.
- **STATUS: UNTESTED IN GAME.** Offline (my harness): **t_cool 468** (C9 NEW: the obliterator calibration, the fading sounds), **t_ignite 111** (I7 NEW: the crew), **t_heat 386** (the cooling speed helper), **t_rad 2241** (R2, R10, R11 rewritten: no overclock, no heat damage, the efficiency floor at 150 / 250 / 500%), **t_rad_pins 104**, **t_radui 259** (the tool without the overclock lines, the steam sprites), t_gen 350, t_tools 74, t_toolui 57, t_lazer_draw 51, t_pen 88, t_life 21: **all 4,210 pass, 0 failed.** The older test files kept their 15.3 versions as `.bak153` (t_cool, t_rad_pins, t_radui). **Mutation testing:** **136 deliberate breakages of the 15.4 code** (the crew exception in the ignite check, the removal of the overclock and of the radiator's heat damage, the cooling speed helper `GetHeatCoolRate` and the radiator's 5x cooling, the radiator's sap rate without the overclock, the flush calibration and the heat of the vent, the fading sounds, the sprite list pick, the tool's readout lines, the load checks), run with `sim/mutate154.py`. The first pass let 16 through: most became tests (the radiator at 150 / 250 / 500% of HOT still working at the efficiency floor, a tiny cooling multiplier used as it is, a cooling speed of 0, the exact fade and stop times of both sounds, the sprite list edges, the seated player and the veto hook), one was redundant code that I DELETED instead (a second floor inside the settle maths that the outer one already covers), one was a bogus breakage that I replaced by a real one, and 2 are EQUIVALENT breakages that no test can tell apart from the real code (a negative fade time behaves as 0 = "stop at once"; "a second flush reuses the old sound pair" cannot happen because the pair is always forgotten when a flush ends). **Final pass (`sim/mut154_new_final.log`): 134 caught, 2 equivalent, 0 other survivors, 0 skipped.** Then the whole 15.3 list (280 breakages, `ONLYOLD=1 python3 mutate154.py`, `sim/mut154_old.log`) was re-run against the 15.4 tree: 255 caught, 0 survived, 25 skipped because the code they break is gone or was rewritten in 15.4 (the overclock, the old sound path with its separate burst, the 2260 / 150 / sprite knobs, the "over the limit" error, the 15.2 version line); the 15.4 list has its own breakages for what replaced them.
- **Calibration you should know about (computed with the real config maths, `sim/numbers154.lua`, `sim/calib153.lua` run on the 15.4 tree):**
  - *Radiator table, 15.4 (defaults; "settle" = where the radiator's OWN heat levels off working flat out for ever, % of HOT; the flush column is the EXTRA heat the flush removes, on top of the sap rate):*

| Width | Mass | Radius | Sap kJ/s | Settles at | Tank | Flush vent | Flush heat |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 12 | 15 kg | 300 | 1,500 | 14.8% | 2.5 L | 0.25 L/s | 31,604 kJ/s |
| 30 | 62 kg | 488 | 7,878 | 18.2% | 39.5 L | 3.95 L/s | 493,807 kJ/s |
| 42 | 158 kg | 613 | 23,801 | 21.1% | 108.4 L | 10.84 L/s | 1,355,005 kJ/s |

  (An empty tank still works at 25% of the sap rate, a half-full one at 62.5%, as in 15.3: the strength line `RadDryPct` is unchanged.)
  - *A lazer firing for ever with natural cooling only (no radiator), seconds to 500 F (burning) / 100% of its limit / 120% (the turbo starts) / 130% (speed 1.25):* 20 mm (500 kJ/s) 15.0 / 38.9 / 49.1 / 54.7; **60 mm (8,133 kJ/s) 3.6 / 8.7 / 10.5 / 11.5**; 100 mm (16,452 kJ/s, 790 kg) 5.9 / 14.3 / 17.4 / 19.1; 300 mm (200,000 kJ/s) 5.5 / 13.4 / 16.3 / 17.8. The damage law of 15.3 is unchanged (a lazer at 121% of its limit dies in 4.7 s, 125% 3.9 s, 130% 3.1 s, 150% 1.3 s, 200% 0.2 s).
  - *A 60 mm lazer firing with radiators in its contraption (a simple model: the lazer's heat minus a constant sap rate, natural cooling on; the radiators' own waste is ignored):* none 500 F 3.6 s / 100% 8.7 s / 120% 10.5 s; one 12-wide (1,500 kJ/s) 4.4 / 10.7 / 13.0; two 12-wide 5.8 / 14.0 / 17.1; three 12-wide 8.2 / 20.3 / 24.9; one 30-wide (7,878 kJ/s) reaches 500 F at about 181 s and never gets near 100%; one 42-wide stays cold.
  - *Generator hub (T196):* a 350 hp hub (400 kg, 1,820 kJ/s) gains +91 K after 10 s, 57% of HOT after 40 s, reaches HOT after about 83 s and settles near 1,647 F (153% of HOT). Generators still do nothing about it (J8 / I5 still open).
- **Decisions I made myself while building (tell me if any is wrong; most are one knob or one line):** (1) "the crew" = anybody `InVehicle()`, one knob, no check of WHICH vehicle (a player in any seat inside the lazer's box is spared). (2) "Hitbox" = the ignite box; the beam is untouched. (3) "1.5x" does not exist: `HeatCoolSpeed` 1.25 on the 15.2 speed. (4) "5x on both" = sap x5 and the radiator's own cooling x5 (`HeatCoolMultRad`), nothing else is multiplied (the flush has its own number). (5) "Dont damage them" = no heat damage of any kind for a radiator, including the turbo; the waste heat and the efficiency curve stay as knobs. (6) The overclock is deleted, not switched off (no input, no knob, no state). (7) The flush number is solved from your scenario; with other hot parts in range the heat is shared by temperature, so the scenario is the lazer alone. (8) Fade 0.8 s, `Stop` 0.1 s after: "within a second". (9) The steam sprite is picked at random from ACF's nine smoke sprites. (10) `HeatCoolSpeed` 0 means nothing cools by itself (negative is clamped to 0); `HeatCoolMultRad` is only for radiators.
- **[GUESS] list for this batch (all need the in-game tests below):** that the fading sounds fix "keeps playing" (the cause is a guess: a burst that could not be stopped, and `FadeOut` not stopping a loop); that `particle/smokesprites_0001..9` exist in your game (ACF's own effects use them; not checked in your VPKs); that `Player:InVehicle()` is true for the seats you use; that the sweep timing gives 25% +/- 5 points in the 2 s scenario; that a dupe with an old Overclock wire loads without an error; the remaining ones of 15.3 (CFW hook argument order, model up axis, `weapon:GetMode()`, Wire `Src`, the sound names, the supply-crate unit, 5x heat not applied to capacitors / generators).
- **Housekeeping:** the stub folder `acf_heatsink` (if your game folder still has one) is not used by anything. This batch does not change the lazer init (still says version 15.3), the link kit, the capacitor or the generator, and the radiator `shared.lua` is the 15.3 file. An optional improvement I have NOT made: a clearer error message when `ents.Create` fails (the "wrong folder" case of the radiator error).
- **Files in this batch (CRLF, ASCII only; install ALL of them, game folder AND server, then restart the map):** `acf_lazers_config.new.lua` 122,826 bytes (CHANGED), `acf_lazers_heat.new.lua` 19,092 bytes (CHANGED: the crew exception), `acf_lazers_linkkit.new.lua` 14,474 bytes (as in 15.3), `acf_lazer_init.new.lua` 80,554 bytes (as in 15.3), `acf_radiator_init.new.lua` 37,513 bytes (CHANGED: no overclock, the fading sounds, VERSION "15.4"), `acf_radiator_shared.new.lua` 1,325 bytes (as in 15.3), `acf_radiator_cl_init.new.lua` 6,502 bytes (CHANGED: the sprite list; client only), `acf_radiator_tool.new.lua` 18,360 bytes (CHANGED: the readout without the overclock). The config, the heat file and the radiator init go together: the radiator refuses to load with an older config.

**What 15.3 is (your Mon Oct 26 01:51 message: "go ahead and continue where you left off, and i need heatsink tool to test how cooling works!!!, i also have some more ideas, scrap the 25% till fire thing, let people shoot the lazers regardless, they have to make their own safeguards with wire, and i would say anything over 120% should basically be turbo obliteration in terms of health damage, as it is, its way too slow. id say play with the knobs for me on that one, natural cooling needs to happen like 2x as fast, if a lazer ent is over 500 degrees it sets players on fire when their hitboxes are intersecting, for until a second after they get off the lazer, also i plan on doing like a coolant flush, where as you tell the radiators to shit coolant, (it has some, in tool colorable puff of smoke/liquid, that represents that) it makes that puff of liquid, and drains whatever volume of that liquid each one has stored in it. id like the max volume of coolant to be around 1/8 of how much volume the bounding box is for it. and for it to drain half of its supply in 5 seconds. needs to make some steam releasing noise when it happens. need a wire input for this ofc. i also need it to restock its coolant, like its taking fuel from an acf supply crate."). CUMULATIVE on 15, 15.1 and 15.2, so it is 15.3, not 16. EIGHT code files (six changed since 15.2; the link kit and the radiator's `shared.lua` are the 15.2 files, included so that ONE copy of the folder installs everything). `[CHANGED n]` tags restart in every file; each file's header lists them.**
- **The heatsink tool is the "Radiator Spawner" of 15.2** (Q menu > Tools > ACF - LaZers; model `models/lasers/heatsink.mdl`). It came with 15.2; whether or not you installed 15.2, it is in THIS folder too (`acf_radiator_tool.new.lua` + the radiator files), so installing the 15.3 folder gives you the whole cooling system. Spawn it, weld it into a contraption with the hot parts, and read the temperature of any part with the tool out (look at it, within 256 units). 15.3 adds to the tool: the coolant colour picker and the tank / flush lines.
- **No fire gate (your "scrap the 25% till fire thing").** A lazer fires whenever it has power (`HasStartEnergy`), at ANY temperature. Gone: the 90% mark, the "cooling mode / unavailable until 25%", the `Cooling` wire output, the knobs `HeatLockStartPct`, `HeatLockEndPctSmall`, `HeatLockEndPctBig` and the helper `GetLazerLockEndPct`. Safeguards are yours, built on the lazer's wire outputs `HeatPct` (percent of its limit), `HeatF`, `HeatKJ`: e.g. an E2 or a comparator that cuts `Fire` when `HeatPct` > 90. The hover text says "Ready to fire" or "Firing" and, over the limit, "Overheated: taking damage". A lazer that is a "dud" (no power) still starts by itself the moment the capacitors can pay, however hot it is.
- **[15.4: a radiator takes no heat damage at all any more, the turbo term included; the lazer and capacitor law below is unchanged] Turbo damage (your "over 120% should be turbo obliteration"; "play with the knobs").** The old quadratic damage (`HeatLazerDamage` / `HeatCapDamage` / `RadDamage`, 4% of max health per second at 200% of the limit) is still there, and PAST `HeatTurboStartPct` (120%) of its limit a thing takes this on top: `HeatTurboPct` (20) percent of its MAX health per second at exactly 120%, growing with (how hot / 120%) ^ `HeatTurboPower` (6). It is a cliff on purpose: below 120% there is no turbo at all. Applies to lazers, capacitors and radiators (each against its own limit: lazer `HeatLazerCapF` 1,100 F, capacitor = its critical heat about 580 F, radiator = HOT 1,100 F); a thing whose own damage knob is 0 takes none; `HeatTurboPct` 0 switches it off. **Percent of max health lost per second (lazer): 100% 0.00, 110% 0.04, 119% 0.14, 120% 0.16, 121% 21.2, 125% 25.8, 130% 32.7, 150% 77.3, 200% 432.7.** What that means for a lazer sitting still at a temperature (`acf_lazers_heat_set`, natural cooling on): 115% and 119% survive for ever (0 to 1% lost), 121% loses 11% in the first second, then it has cooled under the cliff and stays at 89%; 125% ends at 38% health; **130% dead after 3.7 s, 150% after 1.4 s, 200% after 0.3 s.** Capacitor at 130% of its critical heat (736 F): dead after 3.5 s, at 200% (1,100 F) 0.3 s. Radiator at 130% of HOT (1,412 F): 3.7 s, at 200% (2,141 F) 0.3 s. Tune with the three knobs (a start at 110% or a power of 4 makes it gentler).
- **[CHANGED IN 15.4: the speed is x1.25 of 15.2 (`HeatCoolSpeed`), no longer x2, and a radiator cools 5x as fast as a prop; the numbers in this bullet are the x2 ones] Natural cooling twice as fast:** `HeatCoolTau` 300 -> 150 s. Everything that is not a radiator cools twice as fast (a prop at 800 F: 687 F after 15 s, 596 after 30 s, 461 after 60 s, 298 after 2 min, 121 after 5 min). **Side effect you should know about:** a radiator's OWN heat (it makes 1% waste of the work it does) levels off lower, so the 15.2 radiator table changes (see the calibration below) and **no radiator can destroy itself with the overclock at the default waste any more** (the 60-wide at x6 settles at 71% of HOT). Setting `RadWasteSmall` / `RadWasteBig` to 0.02 gives back exactly the 15.2 table. Your call (J1).
- **Hot lazers set players on fire (your "over 500 degrees ... until a second after they get off the lazer").** One timer (`ACFLazers_IgniteSweep`, every `HeatIgniteInterval` 0.1 s) looks only at the lazers in the heat registry (nothing cold is ever looked at). A lazer of a class in `HeatIgniteClasses` (default: `acf_lazer` only) that is at least `HeatIgniteF` (500 F) hot sets every living player on fire whose COLLISION box (the 32 x 32 x 72 box that stops you walking through things; it follows ducking) touches the lazer's box (the model's real box, turned with the lazer, grown by `HeatIgniteReach`, default 0). Touch test = a separating-axis box test (`ACFLazers.ObbTouchesAabb`, config) that I checked against an independent vertex / edge oracle on 30,000 random pairs. The fire lasts until `HeatIgniteLinger` (1 s) after the last touch of a hot lazer (or the lazer cooling under 500 F), then we put it out with `Extinguish`. We light a fire for `HeatIgniteLife` (5 s) at a time; while you keep touching the lazer, we light you again at the next check whenever the fire has burnt out or water put it out. **Fires that are not ours are left alone**: a player who is already burning from anything else is never lit again and never put out by us. Dead players are not lit. Veto hook for safe zones: `hook.Add("ACFLazers_CanIgnite", "name", function(ply, lazer) return false end)`.
- **The coolant (your flush / tank / restock ideas).** Every radiator has a TANK.
  - **Size:** `RadCoolantBoxFrac` (1/8) of the volume of the radiator's bounding box (`RadModelWidth` x `RadModelLength` x `RadModelHeight` = 42 x 42 x 30 units at scale 1, times the scale cubed), in litres (`RadCoolantLitersPerUnit3` 0.016387 = one cubic INCH, ACF's unit): **12 wide 2.5 L, 30 wide 39.5 L, 42 wide 108.4 L, 50 wide 182.9 L, 60 wide 316.0 L.** A new radiator is full (`RadCoolantStart` 1). A resize (tool left-click on it) keeps the LEVEL (share of the tank).
  - **What the tank does for the cooling [GUESS, my own design]:** a full tank = full strength; an empty tank = `RadDryPct` (25%) of the sap rate; a straight line between (62.5% at half). 42-wide at x1: 4,760 kJ/s full, 2,975 half, 1,190 empty. The strength is read at the START of each 0.25 s sweep. An empty radiator says in red "OUT OF COOLANT: working at 25% strength. A supply crate refills it".
  - **FLUSH (wire input `Flush`, any number above 0; NaN, text and 0 and below do nothing):** the radiator vents `RadFlushFraction` (0.5) of a FULL tank in `RadFlushSeconds` (5 s) = a constant litres a second whatever the level (42 wide: 10.84 L/s: half in 5 s, empty in 10 s; a half-full tank empties in 5 s). It stops by itself when the tank is dry or Flush goes back to 0. By default only the radiator whose own `Flush` is on vents (`RadFlushPool` false); true = the Flush input of one radiator flushes its whole linked pool.
  - **What a flush does to the heat [GUESS, my own design]:** every litre that leaves takes `RadFlushKJPerLiter` (2260 kJ, what boiling a litre of water to steam takes) out of the hot parts of the pool, shared by temperature exactly like the radiator's normal cooling, from what the parts hold after the normal cooling of the same sweep, and never more than they hold. 42 wide: 10.84 L/s x 2260 = **24,498 kJ/s on top of the radiator's own 4,760** (about 5x). 0 makes the flush a show only.
  - **The steam:** while it vents the server sets a network flag and the CLIENT draws a cloud of grey-sprite puffs from the top of the model (`RadPuffsPerSecond` 45 at scale 1, more for bigger radiators; `RadPuffLife` 1.6 s, `RadPuffSize` 12 growing to 4x, `RadPuffSpeed` 110 upward; capped at `RadPuffMaxPerFrame` per frame, none beyond `RadPuffDistance` 3000 units or when the radiator is dormant), in the COLOUR picked in the tool (default 90, 220, 150). **Sounds** (server): a burst `ambient/gas/steam2.wav` when a flush starts and a looping hiss `ambient/gas/steam_loop1.wav` while it lasts (faded out in 0.4 s, stopped when the radiator is removed), pitch 125 (12 wide) to 80 (60 wide) by width (97 for the 42), volume `RadFlushVolume` 0.9. **[GUESS] the file names and the sprite `particle/particle_smokegrey` are Half-Life 2 names from memory; if you hear nothing or see nothing, change `RadFlushStartSound`, `RadFlushLoopSound`, `RadPuffMaterial` (the comments in the config give a console line to check a sound file).**
  - **Colour:** the tool has a colour picker (three ConVars `acf_radiator_tool_coolantr/g/b`, starting at the config's `RadCoolantColor`). Left-click spawns with that colour or recolours the radiator you aim at. The colour is a dupe key (stays when copied) and travels to the clients as one number.
  - **Wire:** [15.4: the `Overclock` input is gone] inputs `Overclock`, `Flush`; new outputs `Coolant` (litres), `CoolantPct` (percent of the tank), `Flushing` (0/1).
  - **Restock from an ACF supply crate (your "like its taking fuel from an acf supply crate"):** the radiator registers itself in `ACF.FuelTanks` (the list ACF's supply crate walks) with the same members a fuel tank has (`Amount`, `Capacity`, `GetUnitMass()`, `Consume(units)` where a negative number fills it, `FuelType` "Coolant"), so a supply crate that is within ACF's supply distance (300 units) and in the same CFW contraption tops it up exactly as it tops up a fuel tank. I ran ACF's REAL `acf_supply/modules/supply.lua` in the harness against a radiator and it fills it. A litre costs the crate `RadCoolantUnitMass` (1) kg of its own stock; coolant adds NO mass to the radiator.
- **STATUS: UNTESTED IN GAME.** Offline (my harness): **t_cool 359 (NEW: the tank, the flush, the heat of the vent, the dry strength, the sounds, the supply crate (ACF's real code), the colour, and the edges found by mutation testing), t_ignite 91 (NEW: the box test against an independent oracle, the sweep, the linger, other people's fires, the veto hook, the timer), t_heat 352 (the no-gate lazer, the turbo law, the cooling law), t_rad 2279, t_rad_pins 109, t_radui 202 (the tool's colour picker and lines, the steam Think against a stand-in particle emitter), t_gen 350, t_tools 74, t_toolui 57, t_lazer_draw 51, t_pen 88, t_life 21: 4,033 checks, all pass, 0 failed.** Mutation testing: **271 deliberate breakages of the 15.3 code** (the no-gate lazer, the turbo law, the ignite timer and box test, the coolant tank / flush / strength / colour / sounds / steam, the tool's colour picker and lines, the supply-crate registration), run with `sim/mutate153.py`. The first pass (278 breakages) let 37 through: 29 of them became tests (the heat of a pool with several radiators venting, the heat taken from what is LEFT after the radiator's own work, a tank with less in it than a sweep could vent, the exact edges of the ignite limit and the one-second linger, the length of the fire, the reach on all three sides, the shipped steam numbers, the steam after a pause, the hover bar and its red error, the refresh when the tank moves, the volume default, a coolant knob that is not a table ...), 7 were redundant code that I DELETED instead (two clamps inside the tank size that the outer one already covers, a clamp on the fill that `SetAmount` already guarantees, a NaN check that `NaN > 0` already covers, a clamp on the heat per litre and a `min` on the vent heat that the weighted fill and the `> 0` check already cover, a venting flag in the hover refresh key that the state kind already carries) and 1 was an equivalent breakage (`>=` vs `>` in the box test, hidden by its 1e-9 slack). **Final pass: all 271 caught, 0 survivors, 0 skipped.** Then the whole older list (the 340 breakages of the 15.2 radiator code, `ONLYOLD=1 python3 mutate153.py`) was re-run against the 15.3 tree: 326 caught, 9 skipped because 15.3 changed the text they break (the wire input name, the over-the-limit hover line, the warning hover line, the version number and its bump, the dupe key, the two load checks, the tool panel height) and 5 survivors, all of them the harmless ones already listed for 15.2: the `>=` vs `>` in the weighted fill (both branches give the same number), the `IsValid` guard in the hot-part scan (the member list and the heat store drop a removed part first in my harness), the two "damage with no time" ones (`dt <= 0` gives 0 either way) and the self-link check (ACF's own link registry refuses a link to itself first). For the 9 skipped ones I wrote the same breakage in the current text (`regress:` entries, 9 of them, so the list is now 280); the warning-line one got through at first because only the "hot" state's yellow hover line was tested, so I added the missing check (every radiator state shows its kind of line: not constrained, no CFW, nothing in range and flushing are warnings, cooling is green, nothing hot and switched off are plain) and it is caught now. **All 280 of the 15.3 list are caught.**

- **Decisions I made myself while building (tell me if any is wrong; most are one knob or one line):** (1) "half of its supply in 5 seconds" = a constant rate of half a FULL tank per 5 s (linear), not a half-life; so a tank empties in 10 s and a half-full tank in 5 s. (2) The turbo is a function of the same limit the damage already used (lazer 1,100 F, capacitor critical heat, radiator HOT), not a flat temperature. (3) Only lazers ignite by default; a hot capacitor or radiator does not. (4) Ignite uses the player's collision box, not the bone hitboxes ("hitboxes intersecting" read as "the body box touches the lazer's box"; GMod has no cheap per-bone test for this). (5) Fire is relit while touching (when it burnt out or water put it out) and put out by us one second after the last touch; fires we did not light are never touched. (6) A flush takes heat from the pool on top of the normal cooling (the heat leaves WITH the litres). (7) Coolant has no mass. (8) The empty-tank strength (25%) and 2260 kJ/L are my guesses. (9) `Flush` > 0 vents; the radiator's own input only, unless `RadFlushPool`. (10) The steam is drawn on the client from an NW2 flag and one packed colour number; the server plays the sounds. (11) A full tank is the default for a new radiator; a resize keeps the share. (12) The tool left-click on an existing radiator also recolours it.
