**What 15.2 is (your Oct 24 05:49 message: "sp heat generation for lazers needs to be like 5x what it is across the board, make that change in the config, also i need the radiator tool? where is all of that?"). CUMULATIVE on 15 (15 is installed in your game folder but not tested), so it is 15.2, not 16. It contains 15.1 (the x5) and the radiators I had held back as "batch 16" (my mistake: you were right to ask). `[CHANGED n]` tags: config 1 to 3 (15 / 15.1) and 4 to 6 (15.2); heat file 1 (15.2).**
- **15.1 = lazer heat x5 (config only, `[CHANGED 3]`):** `HeatLazerSmall` / `HeatLazerBig` 1 -> 5. A lazer makes 5x the heat per kJ it spends, and its beam heats what it hits 5x as fast (the hit heat is built from the lazer's heat rate). Heat while firing: 20 mm 500, 60 mm 8,133, 100 mm 16,452, 160 mm 32,562, 300 mm 200,000 kJ/s. Continuous fire with natural cooling only (computed with the real config maths): 90% / 100% of the limit at 20 mm 33.5 / 37.8 s, 60 mm 7.8 / 8.7 s (was 43 / 48 s), 100 mm 12.7 / 14.2 s, 160 mm 19.0 / 21.2 s, 300 mm 11.9 / 13.3 s. The 20 mm lazer used to settle at 91% of its limit; now it passes the limit. I did NOT touch the capacitor and generator heat scalars (`HeatCap*` 1/3, `HeatGen*` 1): you said "for lazers". Tell me if you meant those too.
- **The radiator (new, `acf_radiator`).** Tool: "Radiator Spawner" in the ACF - LaZers tab. Model `models/lasers/heatsink.mdl` (42 x 42 x 30 at scale 1). **Size:** the slider is the WIDTH in units, 12 to 60, default 42; scale = width / 42 (0.29 to 1.43). The width sets mass 15 to 650 kg (power law), radius 300 to 800 units (straight line), sap rate 300 to 25,000 kJ/s at overclock 1 (`RadSapCurve` 1 = the same power law as the mass) and the waste share (1%, `RadWasteSmall` / `RadWasteBig`). Left-click spawns (standing on the surface you aim at) or updates the radiator you aim at; right-click / shift / reload link and unlink radiators with the usual link kit.
- **Who it cools:** every entity with a physics object that is in the radiator's CFW contraption (ACF's own contraption tracking: constrain / weld the radiator to what it should cool) AND inside its radius (centre to centre), except players, NPCs and radiators (a radiator that could cool itself could never overheat). The list is rebuilt when CFW reports a change (added / removed / merged / split), when you resize or link the radiator, and every `RadRefreshInterval` (15 s) as a safety net.
- **[15.4: no overclock factor, the rate is the table's sap rate times the strength of the tank] How it cools:** ONE timer (every `RadSweepInterval` 0.25 s) works every radiator. A pool takes (sap rate x overclock x seconds) kJ out of the hot members, each giving in proportion to (its temperature above room temperature) ^ `RadWeightPower` (1 = proportional to the temperature, so the hottest part gives the most); a part never goes below room temperature and what it cannot give goes to the others. If the members hold less than the radiators could take, they all do proportionally less work (and make less waste). **Pools:** radiators linked to each other (link tools) are ONE pool: one combined member list (a part in range of two counts once), the sum of their rates; each keeps its own heat, overclock and efficiency.
- **[15.4: it takes no heat damage any more, cools 5x as fast as a prop, and the efficiency curve is not reached at the defaults] Its own heat:** working makes waste heat = `RadWaste` (1%) x the work it TRIES to do (not what it removes). Its efficiency is 100% up to `RadEffStartPct` (50% of HOT), falls in a straight line to `RadEffFloorPct` (40%) at 100% of HOT, and stays at that floor above. Over 100% of HOT it damages itself (`RadDamage` 4% of max health per second at 200% of HOT, growing with the square of how far over, through ACF's damage pipeline) and dies like a prop (no explosion). It cools by itself like any ACF entity.
- **[REMOVED IN 15.4 - there is no overclock any more; this bullet is history] Overclock (wire input):** multiplies the sap rate and the waste; unwired = `RadOverclockDefault` (1); 0 = off; clamped to `RadOverclockMax` (6); NaN counts as 0. **Wire outputs:** `SapRate`, `Attempted`, `Efficiency` (percent), `Members`, `HotParts`, `Pool`, `Radius`, `HeatKJ`, `HeatF`, `HeatPct` (percent of HOT).
- **Seeing it:** (1) aim at a radiator with the toolgun out (any tool, not in a seat): a wire sphere (+ a faint fill) of its radius, `RadDraw*` knobs. (2) Hover text: one state line (over its limit / no CFW / not constrained / off / nothing in range / nothing hot / cooling at x% efficiency / cooling) plus size, radius, sap rate, overclock, taking out, temperature and pool. (3) With the radiator tool out, look at ANY part within `RadReadoutDistance` (256 units): its temperature is shown next to the crosshair ("700 F (58% of HOT)", kJ above room, kg), asked of the server (rate-limited per player); a checkbox on the tool switches that readout to Celsius (that readout only: the Celsius client switch stays shelved for everything else).

The 4: (a) `>=` vs `>` in the weighted fill: when the share is exactly equal to the limit both branches give the same number; (b) the `IsValid` guard in the hot-part scan: the radiator's member list and the heat store both drop a removed part before that line can see it in my harness, so it is a one-frame safety net for the real engine that I cannot make a test fail; (c) `dt <= 0` in the radiator's self damage: with no time the damage is 0 either way; (d) the self-link check in the radiator's own link function: ACF's own link registry (util_sv.lua line 449, the real file runs in my harness) refuses a link to itself first, so ours is a second lock
- **Decisions I made myself while building (tell me if any is wrong; most are one knob or one line):** (1) radiators never cool other radiators (the doc: a radiator that could cool itself could never overheat). (2) Only the contraption counts as "constrained" (CFW), not "directly constrained". (3) The waste comes from the work it TRIES to do. (4) `RadDamage` 4 and the 50% / 40% efficiency curve are the doc's numbers. (5) A dying radiator stops working at once. (6) The radiator is tool-only (not in the Q menu). (7) Per-player limit `sbox_maxacf_radiators` 10 (default from `RadMaxPerPlayer`). (8) Radius is a whole number of units (so the hover, the tool and the sphere all agree). (9) The readout reaches 256 units; the server accepts up to 2 x that + 64 because the client measures to the hit point and the server to the nearest point of the part. (10) The pool of a radiator is found by following links (any chain); the groups are rebuilt when `ACFLazers.LinkVersion` changes.

The lazer, capacitor and generator `init.lua` files are the 15 files, unchanged (VERSION "15").

**What 15 is (Oct 24: your heat blurb of 03:43 and your answers of 04:27). FIVE files change and ONE is NEW; 14 is applied and "working as intended", so this is a WHOLE number (`[CHANGED n]` tags restart at 1; the 14 tags are gone). Batch 15 = the HEAT CORE, no radiators. Batch 16 = the radiator entity, its tool and the shared pools.**
- **What it does (your spec, the defaults are the numbers you chose):** every entity can hold heat; its heat capacity is its MASS (kg x `HeatSpecificHeat` 0.49 kJ per kg per K, steel [KNOW]); everything sits at room temperature (`ACF.AmbientTemperature`, 59 F [SEEN `acf/core/globals.lua:252`]) and costs nothing until something heats it. Heat comes from: a firing **lazer** (1x the kJ it spends), a lazer **beam** hitting a prop (the lazer's heat rate x `HeatHit` multiplier x the same focus / angle / burn-through weakening as the damage), a **capacitor** moving energy (1/3 of the kJ moved, in AND out), a **generator hub** making energy (1x the kJ made). Everything cools by itself toward room temperature (`HeatCoolTau` 300 s, faster when hotter; lazers and other ACF entities have their own multiplier knobs).
- **The plate rule (`HeatPenGate`, default on):** a beam burns THROUGH a plate only if the plate is HOT (at least `HeatHotF` 1,100 F = 100%) AND the old rule allows it (armour under 1/3 x caliber x the hit's damage multiplier). Still true: one burn-through per beam, a destroyed plate lets the beam through. **Cold thin plates now BLOCK the beam until they are hot (accepted by you).** `HeatPenGate false` brings back the 14 behaviour exactly.
- **[REMOVED IN 15.3 - there is no lock-out any more, a lazer fires whenever it has power; this bullet is history] The lazer lock-out:** at 90% of its limit (`HeatLockStartPct`) a lazer is marked overheated but the beam goes on while Fire is held. When it STOPS (Fire released, or the capacitors ran dry) it goes into cooling mode, "unavailable", until its heat is down to 25% (`HeatLockEndPctSmall` / `Big`). If it stopped under 90% it was never marked and fires again whenever the capacitors hold the power. If Fire is still held when the cooling ends it starts again by itself. Over 100% of its limit (`HeatLazerCapF` 1,100 F) it damages itself, quadratically (`HeatLazerDamage` 4 % of max health per second at 200%). Dying of heat: while firing, a coin flip (`HeatExplodeChance` 0.5) makes it explode with energy = 2 x its draw kJ through `ACF.HEPower` (kJ / 8000 = kg of HE, NOT literal kg); otherwise it just dies (debris).
- **Capacitors:** critical heat = half of the HOT excess (about 580 F, `HeatCapCritFrac` 0.5); over it they damage themselves quadratically (`HeatCapDamage` 4); killed by heat they detonate like any destroyed capacitor (and a damaged-but-alive one may roll its usual random explosion, because heat damage is damage). **Generators:** only get hot (no penalty in this batch).
- **Outputs:** wire `HeatKJ`, `HeatF`, `HeatPct` on lazer (percent of ITS limit; the `Cooling` output was removed in 15.3), capacitor (percent of its CRITICAL heat) and generator (percent of HOT); hover text shows the Fahrenheit temperature and the percent (15.3: there is no lock-out text any more). Fahrenheit only; a client Celsius switch is shelved.
- **New console commands (testing aids, server console or your own console):** `acf_lazers_heat_info` prints the heat of what you look at (class, mass, F, kJ, % of HOT, plus lazer/capacitor details); `acf_lazers_heat_set <F>` (admins only) sets the temperature of what you look at (it replaces the heat it had). Both need the heat file.

Mutation testing: **357 deliberate breakages, all caught, 0 survivors, 0 skipped (203 of them new in 15 plus the 154 from before)** (see section 7).
- **Decisions I made myself while building (tell me if any is wrong, they are all single knobs or one-line changes):** (1, GONE IN 15.3) the lock-out MARK is set the moment the heat touches 90% and cooling mode starts when firing stops; an idle lazer pushed over 90% by a beam (or by the test command) goes to cooling at once. (2) A beam hit heats the prop by `focal x angle spread x burn-through scale` (the small-lazer damage boost is NOT in the heat). (3) Heat damage is credited to the OWNER (like a hit), so a lazer inside a safe zone may never die of its own heat, exactly like it cannot be damaged there. (4) A hit that ACF refuses (blank result) adds no heat. (5) A capacitor killed by heat is detonated by its existing `ACF_OnDamage`, which may also roll the random explosion on non-lethal heat damage. (6) `HeatDeathDebrisPower` 20000 is a [GUESS] (capped by `MaxDebrisPower` 65535). (7) Lazer and capacitor heat checks run every `HeatCheckInterval` (0.1 s) with the time capped by `HeatMaxDeltaTime` (0.5 s). (8) Restart of a lazer after cooling uses the same "dud" path as running dry.
- **[OUT OF DATE since 15.1 and 15.3: lazer heat is x5, cooling is twice as fast and the lock-out is gone; the current numbers are in 'What 15.3 is'] Calibration you should know about (the defaults, computed with the real config maths):** a 20 mm lazer (100 kJ/s, 60 kg) with natural cooling alone levels off at about 91% of its limit, i.e. it touches the 90% lock-start but never passes the limit; a 60 mm lazer (1,627 kJ/s, 242 kg) reaches 90% in about 43 s of continuous fire and 100% in about 48 s; a 100 mm lazer 75 s / 86 s; a 300 mm lazer 69 s / 79 s. Natural cooling from 90% down to 25% takes about 259 s at the defaults (`HeatCoolTau` 30 makes it 26 s: the way to test quickly). A plate takes about 0.174 s per kg to reach HOT under a 60 mm beam at multiplier 1 straight on (50 kg: 8.7 s, 400 kg: 70 s); a 20 mm lazer needs about 1,130 s for a 400 kg plate, so it effectively cannot open one. **A 350 hp hub running flat out (1,820 kJ/s into 400 kg) reaches HOT after about 77 s and settles near 1,880 F (175% of HOT); in this batch that does nothing.** A full 60-unit capacitor charge heats it only 6.8 K (2.4% of its critical heat); it needs about 42 full charges' worth of energy moved to reach critical.

- **Penetration (his stated priority; answered, plate data still owed):** a 60 mm lazer at multiplier 1 has a pass line of 20 mm (`PenArmourFraction` 1/3 x 60 x 1), at most 28 mm with the 1.4 focus boost; armour never falls below half of `MaxArmour` (`acf/damage/damage_sv.lua:245`), so a 60 mm plate (30 mm at worst) is only ever passed by DESTROYING it. There is no design path where a 60 mm lazer passes a 60 mm plate instantly. The "instant pass-through" he saw was the parented-prop miss above. Damage = Area x min(1, pen / thickness)^2 with pen = caliber x 0.3 = 18 mm, so a 60 mm plate takes (18/60)^2 = 9% of the area per hit. Health is area / `ACF.Threshold` (264.7), independent of thickness.

**What 13.5 adds on top of 13.4 (your messages: "lower diameter max to 60", "damagemult=1", "blue halo is not showing still, but the alpha being set lower now works", and the penetration request: pen per mm 0.3, "the max pen should hover around 1/3rd maximum", "that 1/4 damage needs to be using the same multipliers that the first plate took damage to"):**
- **What you told me you applied:** the lazer `cl_init.lua` from 13.3 (alpha under 255 now works). I do NOT know whether the rest of 13.4 (config, the two generator files) is on the server, so 13.5 is cumulative on 13.4: it resends every file. The blue halo on the lazer is STILL missing: that is no longer the draw code (see "Halo" below).
- **Answers recorded:** G15 = capacitor `CapDiameterMax` goes 100 to **60** ([CHANGED 10] in the config; a 60 unit capacitor holds 203,575 kJ and weighs 20,358 kg, so no capacitor can reach the engine's 50,000 kg physics limit). G16 = "1x" means `DamageMult` = 1 (the pass line of a 60 mm lazer is 20 mm of armour at 1).
- **`PenetrationPerMM` 1.0 to 0.3** ([CHANGED 9] in the config). ACF's penetration number for the damage maths is now caliber x 0.3 (a 60 mm lazer = 18 mm). That is ACF's formula: damage = area x min(1, pen / plate thickness)^2, so a plate thicker than 18 mm takes less than the full damage, 36 mm takes 1/4, 60 mm 9%, 100 mm 3.2% (with 1.0 only plates over 60 mm took less: a 100 mm plate 36%). Things at or under 18 mm take what they took before. This is the "obliterating stuff" fix; a 60 to 100 mm plate now takes about 11 times less damage than with 1.0, a 40 mm plate about 5 times less. It does NOT move the pass-through line (that is `PenArmourFraction` x caliber x the hit's multiplier = 20 mm for 60 mm at multiplier 1, unchanged and independent of `PenetrationPerMM`).
- **Behind a burned-through plate (code change, `acf_lazer/init.lua` is shipped for the first time in this series, VERSION "13.5", `[CHANGED 1]`, `[CHANGED 2]`):** before, the stuff behind the plate was hit with the damage multiplier worked out at ITS OWN (farther) distance, times the 1/4 slice. Now `DamageProp` remembers the multiplier the burned-through plate took (`beam.penMult = focal`, `init.lua:996`) and `ProcessLaserImpact` uses it for everything behind it (`init.lua:1028-1030`), times the usual slice `beam.scale` (`PenDamageFraction` 1/4 x the 0..1 ramp, `init.lua:995`). People behind the plate follow the same rule. What stays per surface: the hit angle effect (`spread`), because it depends on the surface that is hit. The pass line of the plate behind is not used (one burn-through per beam; the next surviving prop stops it).
- **Halo (still missing after the 13.3 fix):** the lazer now calls the very same ACF `ENT:Draw` as the generator hub, so the draw code is not the cause any more. The halo needs, all at once ([SEEN] `acf_base_simple/modules/cl.lua:14-30`, `acf/core/utilities/rendercontext_cl.lua:19-41`): `acf_show_entity_info` not 0, `wire_drawoutline` 1, and `ACF.RenderContext.LookAt` being this entity, which needs a client eye trace to HIT the entity (the client needs its collision model, `PhysicsInitMultiConvex`) and the entity ORIGIN within `MaxWorldTipDistance or 256` units. [GUESS] one of those is false for the lazer (or your older ACF-3 branch differs). **New file `acf_halo_check.new.lua`** (goes to `lua/autorun/client/acf_halo_check.lua`, client only): the console command `acf_halo_check` prints those values for the entity under your crosshair and watches 3 s of drawing; run it looking at the lazer and again at a generator hub and send me both printouts (T167).

**What 13.3 adds on top of 13.2 (your report: "whenever i color the lazer entity with any color alpha less than 255, it becomes completely invisible, and it does not display the blue halo effect like other wire/acf entities"):**
- **One new file: `acf_lazer_cl_init.new.lua`** (goes to `lua/entities/acf_lazer/cl_init.lua`, client only). The other three files are byte-for-byte the 13.2 files, resent because the series is cumulative.
- **Cause [SEEN in the files]:** the lazer's old `cl_init.lua` (a) set `ENT.RenderGroup = RENDERGROUP_BOTH`, (b) drew the model with a bare `self:DrawModel()` in `ENT:Draw()`, and (c) its `ENT:DrawTranslucent()` drew ONLY the beam. ACF's own `ENT:Draw` ([SEEN] `acf_base_simple/modules/cl.lua:14-30`) is what draws the blue outline when you look at an entity (it checks `ACF.RenderContext.LookAt` and `wire_drawoutline`, [SEEN] `acf/core/utilities/rendercontext_cl.lua:19-41`) and calls `Wire_Render`; the lazer never ran it, so it never had the outline. The generator and ACF's engine call `self.BaseClass.Draw(self)` ([SEEN] generator `cl_init.lua`, `acf_engine/cl_init.lua:165-170`) and the capacitor inherits `base_wire_entity` directly, which is why those two show the outline and colour fine.
- **Why alpha under 255 made it vanish [GUESS, matches the symptom exactly]:** the engine puts a see-through entity (alpha under 255, or render mode not Normal) in the see-through pass, and a model drawn in the solid pass is not shown. With `RENDERGROUP_BOTH` the see-through pass is the lazer's `DrawTranslucent`, which never drew the model. (GMod's default `ENT:DrawTranslucent` calls `ENT:Draw`, [KNOW] `base_anim.lua`; ours replaced it and dropped that call.)
- **Fix:** `ENT:ModelIsSeeThrough()` (`GetColor().a < 255` or `GetRenderMode() ~= RENDERMODE_NORMAL`). `ENT:Draw` runs `self.BaseClass.Draw(self)` only when NOT see-through. `ENT:DrawTranslucent` runs it when see-through, then draws the beam exactly as before. So the model is drawn exactly once per frame, in the right pass, through ACF's Draw (outline included). The beam is untouched (it never depended on the model's colour; the beam colour/alpha picker stays gone since 9.7).
- Offline: new `t_lazer_draw.lua` (51 checks): reproduces the report on your current file (31 failures) and passes on the new one; 10 new deliberate breakages, all caught. NOT proven in game.

**What 13 changes (your Oct 19 request, still part of this batch):**
1. A hub no longer breaks engine links when engines push more than its rating. It takes AT MOST its rated hp from the engines in total (engines "go over HP" and the extra is ignored). Nothing breaks, nothing to set.
2. The energy-per-hp scalar is `GenKJPerHP` (it already existed; 5.2 from 13.1). It is now shown on the tool and has a damage multiplier on top: `GenDamagedEfficiency` (0.5): 1x at full health, 0.5x at 0% health, a straight line between.
3. Lazer energy use: new third knob `LazerDrawCurve` (0 = the old straight line, 1 = fully exponential, default 0.5). The two ends stay where the config puts them (13.2: 100 and 40000 kJ/s, your server values), so the curve reshapes only the middle sizes.
4. The generator tool shows the kJ/s big and amber (menu panel AND the toolgun's own screen).

Files in this batch (everything else is as in 12): `acf_lazers_config.new.lua`, `acf_generator_init.new.lua`, `acf_generator_tool.new.lua`, `PROJECT_STATE13.1.md`. Your capacitor model change is in the config (`CapModel = models/lasers/capacitor.mdl`).
The 12.1 file you told me to disregard is not part of 13. (`acf_lazers_linkkit.lua` in my working copy has `if SERVER then AddCSLuaFile() end`, which is correct practice and harmless; your copy is fine without a re-copy.)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------
## 1. What this project is

A modular, ACF-style energy-weapon addon for the user's older ACF-3 branch, for a multiplayer proof of concept, balanced through config knobs:

- **lazers** (`acf_lazer`), **capacitors** (`acf_capacitor`), **generator hubs** (`acf_generator`) that turn ACF engine power into capacitor energy, **radiators** (`acf_radiator`, 15.2) that carry heat away from the parts around them, **PPCs** (`acf_ppc`, 16): capacitor-fed guns that release one energy projectile per shot (16.1: its blast respects safezones, spares its own contraption but heats it, and the lazers focus themselves).
- **Heat core: built in 15, radiators and the x5 lazer heat in 15.2, no lock-out / turbo damage / ignite / coolant in 15.3, no overclock / the obliterator flush / fading steam sounds / the crew exception in 15.4 (all untested in game).** PPCs were built in 16 and fixed in 16.1 (untested in game; their effects come after the damage works). Next: the PPC effects, PPC recoil and the parent-chain shove when you say go, then the custom lazer firing sound. Shelved for later: gauss, a second generator type (mechs: speed and acceleration drive engine throttle), cancelling a shot / dumping with no room, a Celsius client switch (except the radiator tool's readout-only checkbox, which the design doc approved).

Energy chain: **engine > generator hub > capacitor(s) > lazer(s)**. Every link is made with ACF's own link system, with any of the three tools. Mechs are intended to carry about 3 or 4 lazers. Heat chain (15.2): lazers, capacitors, generators and beam hits make heat; radiators constrained to the same contraption take it away. 15.4: no overclock (a radiator eats a fixed kJ/s and never hurts itself from heat), the flush removes about 125,000 kJ per litre, the steam sounds fade out, a player in a vehicle is not set on fire. 15.3: radiators have a coolant tank (weaker when it is low, a flush vents it with steam and takes extra heat with it), a lazer over 500 F sets players on fire, and anything that heats itself past 120% of its limit is destroyed fast (turbo damage).

### The user
GMod/GLua beginner with an E2 background. ADHD. Prefers systems engineering to raw maths. I am the pair-programmer and teacher.

### Standing rules (keep them)
1. Tag every claim [SEEN] / [KNOW] / [GUESS]. Cite function + file + line.
2. Always ask before assuming what is done or tested. **List missing test results FIRST in every reply.**
3. Deliver WHOLE files the user copies by hand: CRLF line endings, ASCII only, named `<name>.new.lua`, state file `PROJECT_STATE<N>.md`. While the previous batch is not applied, the next is `.1/.2...` and CUMULATIVE. A whole number comes after he applies and tests. Changed-files-only is fine for whole-number handoffs. `[CHANGED n]` tags restart at 1 with each whole number.
4. Every number is a config knob. No dead-end or duplicate variables.
5. Give a prediction with each test. Occasional easy Lua exercises. No raw maths lessons.
6. No chat type-outs from the addon beyond the 30 s "no capacitor linked" reminder.
7. Use task lists and short progress notes. End with a concise outcome.
8. If the session is unattended: build with defaults and put the open questions at the end of the final reply.
9. **Core issue, always.** Label every fix CORE FIX or BANDAID (small / medium / large). A diagnostic is not a fix. A workaround is opt-in, never on by default (the auto-restart in `acf_halo_check` v2.2 was a mistake).
10. Source console commands are cut at about 255 characters: keep every `lua_run` line under it (split into several lines with throwaway `zz...` globals).
11. I only have READ access to `E:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\GarrysMod\garrysmod\addons` and to `C:\Users\yomust0of\Desktop\GIT PROJECTS\Acf_LaZers\acf_lazers`. I do not write into his folders; I deliver files for him to copy.
12. Whenever I name a file, give its complete absolute path; every delivered file has a "Goes to:" line in its header AND its full destination path in the reply.
13. Do not push a theory he has rejected twice (Seamless Portals, collisions); say what is measured and what is not, and move on to the next measurable thing.
14. Leave shelved ideas shelved (dump with no room, cancelling a shot, a Celsius switch) until he brings them back.
15. "We ALWAYS go for the core issue, bandaids are discouraged": tell him when a fix is a bandaid and roughly how big (see 9).

---------------------------------------------------------------------------
## 4. How the systems work (current)

### Lazer and capacitor
- **The muzzle hum (21; how it works now, from the code).** The hum is a looping sound patch made when the lazer spawns. When the lazer starts firing it takes `GetMuzzleSound(caliber)` (the volume, the pitch and the level for this caliber) and starts the sound WITH them: `SetSoundLevel(level)` first, then `PlayEx(volume, pitch)`. 16.1 to 20 started it with `Play()` and changed the volume and pitch a moment later with a glide of 0, and the numbers they changed it to were held at the engine's limits (pitch 255, volume 1) for most of the size range, which is why the size could not be heard (the 21 block has the numbers).
- Capacitors linked to each other form a group. A lazer linked to any of them draws from the whole pool.
- Capacity = `CapEnergyPerVolume` (0.3) x `CapEnergyScale` (1) x pi x d^3 kJ (d = diameter, 6 to **60** since 13.5). d = 20 holds 7,540 kJ. d = 60 (the biggest) holds 203,575 kJ (= 25.4 kg of ACF HE). A bigger capacitor on an old dupe or a tool update is squeezed down to 60 (`VerifyData`, `acf_capacitor/init.lua:132`).
- **Lazer draw (13):** a mix of a straight line and an exponential between LazerDrawSmall (100 kJ/s at 20 mm) and LazerDrawBig (40000 kJ/s at 300 mm), set by `LazerDrawCurve` 0..1. Start energy = `LazerStartSeconds` (1 s) of draw, or the pool being at least 90% full. The firing draw, the PowerDraw wire output and the hover text all call `ACFLazers.GetDrawRate`, so they all follow the curve.
- A lazer bite pauses the capacitors' own chargers for 0.25 s (CapNoChargeWhileDrained).
- **Dump** input = kJ/s pushed into the free room of other capacitors in the group.
- **AllowCharge** is the only on/off input on a capacitor: unwired = on, wired = above 0 is on. Off stops its own charger and refuses dumps and generator energy.
- Focus system, falloff, penetration-follows-multiplier: as in handoff 10 (see config comments), with your server numbers: effective range 15000 (20 mm) to 30000 (300 mm) where the damage has fallen to 3/4. The falloff line would reach 0 at 4x that, but `BeamRangeCap` 50000 ends every beam first: a 20 mm beam still does 17% there, a 300 mm beam 58%. Small lazers hit props harder: 2x at 20 mm, tapering to 1x at 300 mm (a 160 mm lazer: 1.5x).

### Heat system (15; all numbers are knobs in the HEAT block of the config)
- **Store:** `ent.ACFL_HeatKJ` = kJ above room temperature, `ent.ACFL_HeatT` = when it was last brought up to date. Cooling is worked out only when something reads or adds heat (exact closed form of `d(theta)/dt = -a theta (1 + boost theta / hot)`, `a = multiplier / HeatCoolTau`), so an idle world costs nothing. A weak-keyed registry plus a sweep every `HeatSweepInterval` (5 s) forgets cooled and removed entities. Temperature = room + kJ / (mass x `HeatSpecificHeat`). Mass: the physics object, else `Entity.ACF.Mass` ([SEEN] `contraption_sv.lua:326`), else `HeatMinMassKg`.
- **Percentages:** a prop's 100% = `HeatHotF` (1,100 F; 0% = room temperature, 59 F). A lazer's 100% = `HeatLazerCapF` (own knob, 1,100 F). A capacitor's 100% = critical = `HeatCapCritFrac` (0.5) x the HOT excess. A generator shows percent of HOT.
- **Where heat comes from:** lazer firing = the kJ it pays each tick x lazer scalar; beam hit = lazer heat rate x hit multiplier x dt x focal x cos(angle x `AngleInfluence`) x burn-through scale (only hits ACF accepted); capacitor = kJ that really moved x scalar, charging, discharging, dumping and generator input alike; generator = the kJ stored into capacitors that tick x scalar. Small .. Big scalars run in a straight line over the size (lazer caliber, capacitor diameter, generator hp).
- **Plate rule:** `DamageProp` in the lazer `init.lua`: the hit's heat is added first, then the beam passes a plate only if (not `HeatPenGate` or the plate is HOT) and the armour is under the pass line (and, as before, only one burn-through per beam; a destroyed plate lets the beam through). With debug on, a plate that is under the line but too cold prints a "cold" line.
- **Damage and death:** `Heat.Hurt(ent, hp)` goes through ACF's own pipeline (`Damage.dealDamage` with a `DMG_BURN` `DamageInfo`, attacker = the owner), so safe zones and damage modes apply. Lazer: `HeatThink` every `HeatCheckInterval`; over its limit it loses `HeatLazerDamage` % x (how far over)^2 of max health per second; a kill calls `HeatDeath` (explosion while firing by the coin flip, else `ACF.APKill`). Capacitor: same law with `HeatCapDamage`; a kill reaches its existing `ACF_OnDamage` and detonates.
- **[REMOVED IN 15.3] Lock-out state machine (lazer):** `HeatHot` set when heat >= `HeatLockStartPct`; `HeatCooling` set when `HeatHot` and not firing; both cleared when heat <= the lock-end percent (`HeatLockEndPctSmall .. Big` along the caliber, always kept at least 1 point under the start). `CanStart` = not cooling and the capacitors hold the start energy. While cooling the lazer is not a "dud" (`NoPower` stays 0).

### The PPC blast's heat and the Heat Checker (16.6; how they work now, from the code)
- **The blast.** `lazers_ppc.lua` `Ammo:OnFlightEnd` sets up `Active = { Info, Blast, Frag, Hits }` and calls ACF's `Damage.createExplosion`. ACF's explosion deals damage to each target it reaches (`Damage.dealDamage`: `ACF.Check`, then the hook `ACF_PreDamageEntity` (ACF's permission core, and a `false` from any hook ends it there), then `ACF_OnDamageEntity`, the damage, `ACF_PostDamageEntity`). Our hook `ACFLazers_PPCBlast` on `ACF_OnDamageEntity` only acts while `Active` is set and the `DamageInfo` is the explosion's own: it scales the `DamageResult`'s area by `PPCPropDamageScalar` (blast part) or `PPCFragmentScalar` (fragment part) times the age factor, then adds `DmgResult:Compute().Damage` (a pure call: it is the very call ACF's handler makes next) to `Hits[Ent]`. After the explosion `HeatPass(Hits)` gives every part in `Hits` that `CanHeat` (an ACF thing, not a person, not `ACF_Killed` / `Exploding`) and is not in a safezone `GetPPCDamageHeat(damage, Heat.GetCapacity(ent))` kJ through `Heat.Add`. A part ACF refused (permission) never reaches `ACF_OnDamageEntity`, so it is not in `Hits`.
- **`GetPPCDamageHeat(damage, capacity)`** = `damage x max(PPCHeatPerDamage, 0)`, held to `PPCHeatMaxPct / 100 x capacity x GetHeatRiseHot()` when `PPCHeatMaxPct` is above 0 and a capacity above 0 is given (no capacity or a 0 percent: no cap); junk, nothing, 0 or less give 0; an infinite or NaN result gives 0.
- **The checker's numbers.** `Heat.Describe(ent)` = class, mass, capacity (mass x `HeatSpecificHeat`), kJ, kelvin above room, F, the share of HOT, the share of the part's OWN limit (lazer: `HeatLazerCapF`; PPC: `PPCHeatCapF`; capacitor: its critical heat; -1 for other parts), what it loses by itself in the next second (the cooling law on its own heat), and what the radiators took in their last sweep (the three `ACFL_Rad*` fields, shown while the note is no older than `max(RadSweepInterval, 0.05) x 4 + 1` seconds). `Heat.Scan(center, radius)` lists the parts in the heat store inside the radius (50 to `HeatCheckRadiusMax`), `ACFLazers.HeatScanSummary` sums them (machines against the rest, and the share of a radiator's work the machines would get: sum of `riseK ^ RadWeightPower`) and `Heat.ScanLines` prints.
- **The tool.** The client asks the server about the part under the crosshair at most every `HeatCheckQueryInterval` seconds (the server answers at most every half of it, within `HeatCheckDistance` + 64 units), draws the reply next to the crosshair for 2 seconds, and shows "..." until one comes. The left-click is server side.

### The damage scalar, the radius and the limits (16.7; how they work now, from the code)
- **Why the damage scalar cannot move the radius.** `Ammo:OnFlightEnd` (`lazers_ppc.lua`) works out the HE filler from the shot (`GetPPCBlastRadius` / the filler = shot kJ x `PPCBlastScalar` / 8000) and calls ACF's `Damage.createExplosion`. ACF turns the filler into the radius (`Damage.getBlastRadius`), searches `ents.FindInSphere(position, radius)`, and for each target works out an area, a penetration and a `DamageResult`. Our hook `ACFLazers_PPCBlast` on `ACF_OnDamageEntity` runs once per target AFTER that choice and multiplies the `DamageResult`'s area by `PPCPropDamageScalar` (blast part) or `PPCFragmentScalar` (fragments) times the age factor. The radius, the target list and the line-of-sight / armour losses are decided before and independently (ACF's loss of "power" along the way uses the penetration and the armour, not the area). So only the damage dealt moves, and the heat (damage x `PPCHeatPerDamage`) follows it.
- **The PPC blast's own damage (20; how it works now, from the code).** The same hook `ACFLazers_PPCBlast` runs per target AFTER ACF chose it. For a PROP (not "Squishy", with a usable `Ent.ACF.Area`) and with `PPCOwnDamage` on, it works out `GetPPCBlastDamage(area, thickness, penetration, |HitPos - Origin| / radius, caliber)` x the age factor and puts it in the damage result with `SetDamage` (ACF's "custom damage": `Compute()` returns it as the damage; the thickness and penetration are the ones ACF put in the result). Nothing else of ACF's explosion changes, so the targets, the line of sight and the penetration loss are ACF's. For people, for a hit it cannot measure, and with the flag off, the hook takes the 19 path described above (the damage scalar on the area).
- **The limits file.** (16.8: REMOVED, the file is comment-only again; this paragraph describes 16.7 only.) `acf_lazers_limits.lua` (`autorun/server/`) holds a list of the five variables and their knobs, a function `ApplyLimits` and `hook.Add("InitPostEntity", "ACFLazers_EntityLimits", ApplyLimits)`. Autorun files run BEFORE the entity files, so setting the variables at load time would be too early (the entities create them later with `CreateConVar`, which keeps an existing or saved value); `InitPostEntity` runs after all of them. `ApplyLimits` does nothing if the config table is missing or `EntityLimitsFromConfig` is `false`; for each variable it reads the knob (`math.floor(tonumber(knob) or 40)`), compares with `GetConVar(var):GetInt()` and, if different, queues `RunConsoleCommand(var, want)` (the command runs a moment later, so the line says "was X, now Y" and is not read back); a variable that does not exist (its entity file did not load) is named in the line.

### Changes of 19 (code map)
- **Config (`acf_lazers_config.lua`, [19]):** `PPCBallMaterial` -> `models/alyx/emptool_glow` (knob line and `PPCStringDefaults`); five new number knobs (`PPCBallSizeSmall` 0.5, `PPCBallSizeBig` 3, `PPCBallColorR` 0, `PPCBallColorG` 255, `PPCBallColorB` 255) in the knob block AND in `PPCNumberDefaults` (a config without the lines does the same); `ACFLazers.GetPPCBallMult(caliber)` is now the 16.9 ramp (`Lerp(t, 1, PPCBallBigMult)`) TIMES the new line (`Lerp(t, PPCBallSizeSmall, PPCBallSizeBig)`), the same `t` = (caliber - `PPCCaliberMin`) / (`PPCCaliberMax` - `PPCCaliberMin`), each end held at 0 or more; `GetPPCBallScale` is unchanged (it multiplies by `GetPPCBallMult`); new `ACFLazers.GetPPCBallColor(r, g, b)` returns three whole numbers 0 to 255 (a missing or junk part is the config's colour for that channel, a number outside the range is held to it, a fraction is rounded); the comment of `PPCBallBigMult` had the 150 and 200 mm sizes wrong (15.9 and 21.6: they are 16.2 and 22.4) and is corrected; header item 34.
- **PPC entity (`entities/acf_ppc/init.lua`, version 19):** `VerifyData` clamps `Data.BallR/G/B` through `GetPPCBallColor`; `ApplyData` stores them on the entity (`self.BallR/G/B`, BEFORE `ApplyCaliber`, which runs `NetworkBolt`); `NetworkBolt` sets the networked vector `FlightColor` = `Vector(r, g, b)` from those fields (nil fields = the config's colour); `Entities.Register("acf_ppc", MakeACFPPC, "Caliber", "BallR", "BallG", "BallB")`; the load guard also asks for `GetPPCBallColor`. An update that names no colour puts the default back (the tool always sends one).
- **Ammo type (`acf/entities/ammo_types/lazers_ppc.lua`):** the client hook `ACFLazers_PPCBall` (ACF's `ACF_OnCreateBulletEffect`) reads `FlightColor` from the PPC (the "crate" of the bullet), defaults to the config's colour, and calls `Effect:SetColor(Color(r, g, b, 255))` inside `pcall`, after the material and before the lifetime adjustment. Server code and the blast: untouched.
- **PPC tool (`weapons/gmod_tool/stools/acf_ppc_tool.lua`):** `TOOL.ClientConVar` `ballr`, `ballg`, `ballb` (start at the config's colour); `panel:ColorPicker("Ball colour", ...)` with the three convars and no fifth (opacity) one; `LeftClick` sends `BallR/G/B` (`ply:GetInfoNum`) in `Data` for a spawn and for an update; the tool refuses to load without `GetPPCBallColor`.
- **Test harness:** `sim/t_ppc.lua`: the file runs with `PPCBallSizeSmall` and `PPCBallSizeBig` at 1 as REFERENCE values (the older ball pins are the 18 ball; the knob-equals-default test knows), the dupe-keys, material, version and notice pins are updated, the client-hook mocks have `GetNW2Vector` and `SetColor`, the panel mock has `ColorPicker`, and the new section **N13** pins everything listed in the status paragraph. `sim/t_toys.lua`: the toy ball's material. `mapbuild/tests19.py` makes both from the 18 versions (`sim/*.bak190`), `mapbuild/mut19.py` runs the hand mutants (`mut190.log`).

Builders: `mapbuild/patch16_3.py` (w163 from w162), `mapbuild/mkconfig16_3.py` (your full config), `mapbuild/deliver16_3.sh` (the checks), `mapbuild/state16_3.py` (this document), `sim/mutate163.py` (the breakage run on w163).

### Changes of 16.2 (code map; the numbers are knobs in the config)
- **Config (`acf_lazers_config.lua`, [CHANGED 24]):** (a) the PPC knob block replaced in place: `PPCEnergySmall` 500, `PPCEnergyBig` 200000 and the comments and the size table that quote them; then two NEW sections at its end, THE PPC'S OWN HEAT (`PPCHeatSmall`, `PPCHeatBig`, `PPCHeatCapF`, `PPCHeatDamage`, `PPCHeatExplodeChance`, `PPCHeatExplosionMult`, `PPCHeatDeathDebrisPower`, `PPCHeatFiringSeconds`) and THE PPC'S FIRE SOUND (`PPCFireSound`, `PPCFireSound2`, `PPCFireSoundLevel`, `PPCFireVolume`, `PPCFireVolume2`, `PPCFirePitchSmall`, `PPCFirePitchBig`); (b) the PPC helper block replaced: the in-code defaults for every new knob (`PPCSoundDefaults` is exposed as `ACFLazers.PPCSoundDefaults`), `PPCSoundList` (a layer's sounds as a list: a text is one, junk is dropped, `false` / `""` is none), `PickPPCSound(name, pick)` (one at random; nil for a silent layer; `pick` only for tests), `GetPPCFirePitch` (a straight line over the size, held to 20 .. 255), `GetPPCHeatRise` (the PPC's 100% in kelvin), `GetPPCHeatScalar` (kJ of heat per kJ of shot, a line over the size, never below 0), `GetPPCShotHeat` (the shot's energy x that), `GetPPCDeathFiller` (the lazer's explosion filler x `PPCHeatExplosionMult`), and `GetPPCReadout` gains `heat`, `heatPct`, `deathFiller`; (c) `HeatIgniteClasses` gets `acf_ppc`.
- **PPC (`acf_ppc/init.lua`, VERSION "16.2"):** the load check now names the 16.2 helpers and the heat store (one red line, nothing half-defined); the sounds are precached when the file loads; `Initialize` adds the fields `HeatDying`, `NextHeatCheck`, `LastHeatCheck`, `NextHeatRead`, `HeatShown`, `LastShotTime` and the outputs `HeatKJ`, `HeatF`, `HeatPct`; `AddShotHeat(now)` (called by `TryShoot` only after a shot is RELEASED and PAID: it stamps the shot time, adds `GetShotHeat()` to the heat store and makes the readout due at once); `HeatThink(now)` (every `HeatCheckInterval`: over 100% it calls `TakeHeatDamage(over, dt)`, which asks the lazer's damage law for the percent per second and hurts the PPC through `Heat.Hurt`, i.e. ACF's `dealDamage`); `HeatDeath()` (once; explosion with chance `PPCHeatExplodeChance` if `IsFiring`, else `ACF.APKill`); `HeatReadout(now)` (the three outputs and the hover text on the lazer's `HeatOutputInterval` clock while it holds heat, once more when it has cooled, then silence); `PlayFireSound()` (two `EmitSound` calls, called by `Shoot` only when ACF made the projectile); `Think` runs `TryShoot` while Fire is held, then `HeatThink`, then (only if the PPC is still there) `HeatReadout`, and sleeps 0.2 s when cold and idle, 0.05 s while it holds heat, every tick while Fire is held or the refire wait runs.
- **PPC tool (`acf_ppc_tool.lua`):** the info line under the slider appends " Each shot heats the PPC by N kJ (N% of its limit, before cooling); over 100% it damages itself." when the readout has both numbers (an older config has neither: the sentence is simply left out).
- **Test harness:** `sim/t_ppc.lua` (1,305 checks) is now built each run by `sim/patch_tests162.py` from the 16.1 file `t_ppc.lua.bak161` (every pinned number moved to the 2.5x values by exact replacements with counts) plus the new sections in `sim/t_ppc162.body.lua`; `sim/h_env.lua` has `EmitSound`, `util.PrecacheSound`, `OBBCenter`, `VectorRand`; `sim/mutate162.py` (the mutation runner), `mapbuild/mkblocks162.py` (the two config blocks), `mapbuild/patch16_2.py` (builds the w162 tree from w161 + `mapbuild/p162/`), `mapbuild/mkconfig16_2.py` (your full config), `mapbuild/deliver16_2.sh` (the delivery files and their checks), `mapbuild/state16_2.py` (this document).

### Changes of 16.1 (code map; the numbers are knobs in the config)
- **Config (`acf_lazers_config.lua`, [CHANGED 23]):** (a) the PPC block replaced in place (30 knobs: 26 numbers, 3 texts, 1 flag; `PPCBallScale` removed; `PPCBallDiameter`, `PPCBallModelDiameter`, `PPCHeatScalar`, `PPCHeatRadiusMult` new); (b) after `FocalBoostMax`: `LazerAutoFocus`, `LazerFocusSpeed`; (c) the new helper section `SAFEZONE + AUTO-FOCUS HELPERS` before the heat helpers: `SafezoneFlagNames`, `ReadSafezoneFlag` (a method flag is CALLED, in a pcall, and its answer used), `IsEntityInSafezone`, `SafezoneFlags` (names the flags it found, "none?" if none), `DescribeSafezone` (the sentence the notices use), `IsInACFSafezone(pos)` (guards: ACF, `ACF.EnableSafezones`, `Permissions` is a table, `IsInSafezone` is a function; pcall), `IsProtectedBySafezone` (flag OR ACF zone), `AutoFocusOn`, `GetFocusSpeed(speed)`, `StepFocus(current, target, dt, caliber, speed)`; (d) the PPC helpers: `GetPPCBallScale`, `GetPPCHeatRadius`, `GetPPCHeat`.
- **Lazer (`acf_lazer/init.lua`, VERSION "16.1", [CHANGED 12]):** the safezone functions are aliases of the config's; `focusDist` is set in the beam loop at the FIRST hit only; `ENT:AutoFocus(dt, target)` (nothing if the target is nil or the focus is off; `StepFocus` with the lazer's caliber; writes `self.Focal`, `SetFocalLength` and the wire output only if the value changed); it is called as the last step of `FireLaserBeam`, after the damage and the sounds.
- **Lazer tool and capacitor:** see the delivery table.
- **PPC (`acf_ppc/init.lua`, VERSION "16.1"):** `Shoot` now (1) copies the contraption's filter list (ACF appends hits to a bullet's filter, so each bullet needs its own copy), (2) asks `IsProtectedBySafezone(self)` once and tells the owner if the PPC is protected, (3) passes `Crate = self:EntIndex()` (the crash), `Filter`, `PPCStart`, `PPCCaliber`, `PPCSafe`.
- **Projectile (`lazers_ppc.lua`):** `OnFlightEnd` makes the explosion only if the config has all the helpers and the shooter is not protected (at the shot or now); the explosion's filter is a COPY of the bullet's filter plus every protected entity in the blast sphere (`BlastFilter`); the multipliers are applied by the same `ACF_OnDamageEntity` hook as in 16; then `HeatPass` (inside its own pcall) gives `Heat.Add(ent, GetPPCHeat(...))` to everything `CanHeat` and not protected in the heat zone (other people's things only if `ACF.Permissions.CanDamage` does not say no); then the projectile is removed as before.
- **Toys (`autorun/client/acf_lazers_toys.lua`):** the tokenizer (`Tokenize`: one line to pieces with a kind each; carries long comments and strings from line to line), `PrintCode` (colours + line numbers + a mark on changed lines), `ApplyChanges` (the `key=number` rewrite, numbers only), `LZ.Draw3D` / `LZ.DrawHUD` / `LZ.OnStop` (helpers for the toys' drawing, each with a time limit), the five toys, and the five commands. Everything is wrapped so a failing toy prints its error in red and stops.
- **Test harness:** `sim/t_ppc.lua` (970 checks; 16.1 added N1 - N7: the control blast with no rule, own contraption, safezones, a PPC in a safezone, the heat, the failure cases, the shot itself; and the crash test through ACF's real `effects_sh.lua`), `sim/t_focus.lua` (148 checks: AF1 the helpers, AF2 the lazer's focus through the real `FireLaserBeam`, AF3 off / frozen / no knobs, AF4 the version and the refusal, SZ1 the shared safezone rules), `sim/t_toys.lua` (244 checks: T1 the colours, T2 printing, T3 changing a number, T4 the commands, T5 each toy, T6 `lz_hl`). The mutation runner for this version is `sim/mutate161.py`.

### PPC (16; all numbers are knobs in the PPC block of the config, everything starting with `PPC...`; 16.1 changed the parts listed above)
- **The fire sound (21).** `PlayFireSound` plays two layers from the PPC (`EmitSound`): the burst (`PPCFireSound`, now `beams/beamstart5.wav`) and a random zap from the crackle list (`PPCFireSound2`), both at `PPCFireSoundLevel` 100 dB and the pitch of the caliber (`PPCFirePitchSmall` 125 down to `PPCFirePitchBig` 75). Since 21 each layer's volume is its knob (`PPCFireVolume` 1, `PPCFireVolume2` 0.8) times `GetPPCFireVolumeMult(caliber)` (0.6 at 20 mm, 0.64 at 50 mm, 1 at 300 mm).
- **Flow of one shot:** Fire goes from 0 to above 0 (wire) > `TriggerInput` remembers the press > `TryShoot` (the cooling over? the pool can pay?) > `Shoot` asks ACF for a projectile (`ACF.Ballistics.CreateBullet`, type `LazerPPC`, zero acceleration, no drag, `KillTime` = now + `PPCLifetime`, the contraption's own bullet filter) > only if ACF made it, `TakeEnergy` pays the shot from the capacitor group > ACF flies the projectile (`CalcBulletFlight`) > on a hit (or when the time is up) ACF asks the ammo type > `OnFlightEnd`: if it HIT, one HE explosion in front of the surface, with the multipliers on; the projectile is removed.
- **Config (`acf_lazers_config.lua`, [CHANGED 22]):** the block `THE PPC` after the radiator block (27 knobs: 23 numbers, 3 texts, 1 flag; every one OPTIONAL: a config without it uses the number written in the helper, and a test keeps the two equal), and the block `PPC HELPERS`: `PPCNumber / PPCString / PPCFlag` (read a knob, fall back, never throw), `ClampPPCCaliber`, `GetPPCSizeFraction` (0 at the smallest, 1 at the biggest), `GetPPCScale` (caliber / `PPCCaliberBase`), `GetPPCMass`, `GetPPCShotEnergy` (the lazer's mix of line and exponential, `PPCEnergyCurve`), `GetPPCSpeed`, `GetPPCHePower` (ACF's 8000, with a fallback), `GetPPCFillerMass` (kJ x `PPCBlastScalar` / HE power), `GetPPCBlastRadius` (ACF's own law), `GetPPCDamageMult(age)` (the straight line from 1 to `PPCDamageAtEnd` over `PPCLifetime`), `GetPPCBallScale`, `GetPPCReadout` (every number the tool and the hover text show), `ACFLazers.PPCAmmoID = "LazerPPC"`. `LinkClasses` has `acf_ppc = "PPC"`.
- **Entity (`acf_ppc/init.lua`, VERSION "16"):** `Initialize` (wire: input `Fire`; outputs `Caliber`, `ShotKJ`, `MuzzleVel`, `BlastKg`, `BlastRadius`, `Energy`, `Shots`, `Ready`, `NoPower`); `ApplyCaliber` / `ApplyData` (model, scale, mass, ACF health: one function for spawn AND update, so they behave the same) and `NetworkBolt` (the values ACF's client bullet effect reads from the "crate" of a bullet, here the PPC); `ACF_Activate` (health and armour the lazer's way, with the PPC multipliers); the pool functions (copied from the lazer, see the bandaid); `Notify` (rate-limited chat notices); `RefreshPower` (the pool is read on an interval, forced when something changes) and the wire outputs; `Shoot` / `TryShoot` / `TriggerInput` / `Think`; the hover text (`ACF_UpdateOverlayState`: Ready / Cooling / Cannot fire, caliber, muzzle velocity, blast, projectile life, energy bar, capacitors, energy per shot); `Update` and `MakeACFPPC` (spawn, with the player limit `sbox_maxacf_ppcs` made from `PPCMaxPerPlayer`); the link functions (`ACF.RegisterClassLink("acf_ppc", "acf_capacitor")`: the PPC joins the capacitor's `Lazers` set, so a capacitor knows it and the pool is shared); the duplicator (`PreEntityCopy` / `PostEntityPaste`).
- **Shared (`acf_ppc/shared.lua`):** base class `acf_base_scalable`, the networked `CaliberSize`, `GetCaliber`, and `GetMuzzlePos` (ONE definition of where the projectile starts: the front of the model).
- **Client (`acf_ppc/cl_init.lua`):** includes the shared file and nothing else yet (the scalable base draws the model).
- **Projectile (`acf/entities/ammo_types/lazers_ppc.lua`):** `AmmoTypes.Register("LazerPPC", "AP")`, blacklisted for every weapon (not in any menu), `GetPenetration` 0, `PropImpact` / `WorldImpact` return false (any hit ends the flight), `OnFlightEnd` (the explosion, with the damage multipliers switched on only for the length of that one call, in a `pcall`, so an error inside ACF's explosion cannot leave them on), and the hook `ACF_OnDamageEntity` "ACFLazers_PPCBlast" (scales the Area of the DamageResult of OUR explosion by `PPCPropDamageScalar` x the time factor for blast damage, `PPCFragmentScalar` x the time factor for fragments, and leaves every other damage type and every other explosion alone). Client side: `ImpactEffect` (ACF's explosion picture, sized by the filler) and the hook `ACF_OnCreateBulletEffect` (puts `PPCBallMaterial` on the ball and ends it when the server's projectile ends).
- **Tool (`acf_ppc_tool.lua`):** category "ACF - LaZers", name "PPC Spawner". The slider (`acf_ppc_tool_caliber`, 20 to 300, from the knobs) and one readout line from `GetPPCReadout`; left-click spawns or, aiming at a PPC, updates it (clamped, with a chat note if the number was moved); right-click, Shift + right-click and Reload come from the shared link kit.
- **PPC ball colour (19):** the tool's "Ball colour" picker writes the convars `acf_ppc_tool_ballr/g/b`; left-click sends them as `Data.BallR/G/B`; `VerifyData` in the PPC clamps them (`GetPPCBallColor`); `ApplyData` stores `self.BallR/G/B`; `NetworkBolt` sets `FlightColor`; the ammo type's client hook tints the effect with alpha 255. A dupe keeps the colour (the three names are in `Entities.Register`).
- **Test harness:** `sim/t_ppc.lua` (836 checks, sections A1 - A5 the config maths, B1 - B5 the entity, C1 - C8 linking, energy and the fire logic, D1 - D8 ACF's real flight and damage, E1 the tool, F1 - F2 hover text and outputs, G1 - G3 the client, M1 - M14 the second pass), `sim/mutate160.py` (the mutation runner), `mapbuild/patch16.py` (builds the w160 tree from w159 + `mapbuild/p16/`), `mapbuild/mkconfig16.py` (your full config), `mapbuild/deliver16.sh` (the delivery files and their checks), `mapbuild/state16.py` (this document).

### Radiator changes of 15.9 (code map)
- **Config (`acf_lazers_config.lua`, [CHANGED 21]):** `RadRoundMax` 60 -> 30 and its in-code fallback `or 60` -> `or 30` (in `GetRadKind`); the knob `RadBeyondMax` and the line in `GetRadBeyond` that read it are GONE (`GetRadBeyond` is now: a heatsink is 1; a radiator whose equivalent width is at most `RadWidthMax` is 1; else `(equiv / RadWidthMax) ^ 3`); comments. Item 21 of the header list says all this. Nothing else in the config moved (checked: the only differing knob and code lines against 15.8 are those).

- **Tool (`acf_radiator_tool.lua`, [CHANGED 4]):** the toolgun screen reads "radius N (diameter 2N), M kg, reach R", the menu text starts "A roundsink radiator with a radius of N units (a diameter of 2N): ...", the assert names the 15.9 config (9 changed lines).
