### Radiator changes of 15.8 (code map; the range and the RadBeyondMax parts were changed by 15.9, see above)
- **Config (`acf_lazers_config.lua`, [CHANGED 20]):** knobs `RadRoundModel`, `RadRoundRadius` 13, `RadRoundHeight` 46, `RadRoundMin` 12, `RadRoundMax` 60, `RadRoundDefault` 13, `RadBeyondMax` 1 (all with in-code fallbacks: a config without them still gives the shipped numbers). New pure helpers: `GetRadKinds`, `ClampRadKind` (only the exact word `roundsink` is the roundsink), `GetRadKind(kind)` (key, base, label, word, model, ref, volume1, min, max, default), `GetRadVolume`, `GetRadEquivWidth` (a heatsink answers its own width, exactly), `GetRadBeyond` (1, or the volume past the biggest heatsink), `PickRadPuffSpot`. Every size helper (`ClampRadWidth`, `GetRadScale`, `GetRadSizeFraction`, `GetRadMass`, `GetRadRadius`, `GetRadSap`, `GetRadWaste`, `GetRadCoolantCapacity`, `GetRadFlushPitch`, `GetRadReadout`) takes the kind as its LAST argument; no kind is the heatsink. `GetRadReadout` also returns `kind`, `word`, `equiv`, `volume`, `beyond`.
- **Entity (`acf_radiator/init.lua`, [CHANGED 5]):** `Kind` is a plain field (`Initialize` sets "heatsink"; `ApplyData` sets the real one; the duplicator saves it: `Entities.Register(..., "Width", "Kind", "CoolantR", "CoolantG", "CoolantB")`); `VerifyData` clamps the kind then the size; `ApplyScale(width, kind)` swaps the model with `SetScaledModel` and scales; an update whose kind is not one of ours keeps `self.Kind`; the kind is published as the NW2 string `ACFRadKind`; the name is "N-radius Roundsink" or "N-unit Radiator"; the sweep, the waste, the member radius, the flush pitch, the status line, the Radius wire output and the armour pass the kind; the hover text of a roundsink reads "radius N units (model scale S), M kg, as much volume as a heatsink W units wide"; `MakeACFRadiator` picks the model by kind and its missing-model message names `RadRoundModel` or `RadModel`; the load check also needs `GetRadKind` and `GetRadEquivWidth`.
- **Shared (`shared.lua`):** `ENT:GetKind()` = the plain field on the server, the NW2 string on the client, always through `ClampRadKind`.
- **Client (`cl_init.lua`):** the sphere uses the reach of the kind; the steam's scale and spot follow the kind (a rectangle for the heatsink, an even disc in the middle of the bounding box for the roundsink).
- **Tool (`acf_radiator_tool.lua`):** `ClientConVar kind` ("heatsink"), a `DComboBox` selector, the slider relabelled and re-ranged on pick (`OnSelect` runs `acf_radiator_tool_kind` then `acf_radiator_tool_width` = the model's start), the readout cache keyed by size AND kind, the menu text `Head(show)`, and the server side of left-click sends `Kind` and clamps the size by the kind's own range (the note says "Radius limited to ..." or "Width limited to ...").

### Heat changes of 15.7 (code map)
- **Lazer (`acf_lazer/init.lua`, [CHANGED 10] and [CHANGED 11]):** the load check for `HeatOutputInterval` / `LazerOverlayDelay` and its `return` are GONE. Two locals, `READOUT_INTERVAL_DEFAULT = 0.1` and `OVERLAY_DELAY_DEFAULT = 0.25`, are used when the knob is missing or not a number: `ENT.OverlayDelay = math.max(tonumber(LASER_CONFIG.LazerOverlayDelay) or OVERLAY_DELAY_DEFAULT, 0)`, and `HeatReadout` sets `NextHeatRead = now + math.max(tonumber(LASER_CONFIG.HeatOutputInterval) or READOUT_INTERVAL_DEFAULT, 0)` (a knob of 0 still means every tick). After the version line: `CreateConVar("sbox_maxacf_lazers", tostring(tonumber(LASER_CONFIG.LazerMaxPerPlayer) or 40), FCVAR_ARCHIVE + FCVAR_NOTIFY, ...)` and `cleanup.Register("acf_lazers")` (the same two things your separate limits file did, at 20). The spawn function is unchanged: `ply:CheckLimit("acf_lazers")` first, `ply:AddCount("acf_lazers", ent)` after.
- **Radiator (`acf_radiator/init.lua`, [CHANGED 4]):** the `HeatOutputInterval` check and its `return` are GONE (the readout timer already read `tonumber(CFG.HeatOutputInterval) or 0.1`, floor 0.02 s). The variable line became `tostring(tonumber(CFG.RadMaxPerPlayer) or 40)`.
- **Capacitor and generator:** no code change. They read `CFG.CapMaxPerPlayer` / `CFG.GenMaxPerPlayer` (shipped 40 now) when their files load, with `CheckLimit("acf_capacitors")` / `CheckLimit("acf_generators")`.
- **`acf_lazers_limits.lua`:** four comment lines. It must not create `sbox_maxacf_lazers` any more: the first `CreateConVar` wins.
- **Config:** `LazerMaxPerPlayer = 40` (new, next to `LazerEnergyReadInterval`), `CapMaxPerPlayer` / `GenMaxPerPlayer` / `RadMaxPerPlayer` 40 (were 20 / 10 / 10); comments only for the two readout knobs ("OPTIONAL since 15.7").

### Heat changes of 15.6 (code map; the radiator readout design is still current, its load check is gone)
- **Radiator readout (radiator `init.lua`):** `Rad.Readout()` walks `Rad.List` (the radiators; `IsValid` first) and calls `radiator:HeatReadout()`. The timer `ACFLazers_RadiatorReadout` (made at file scope next to the sweep timer) runs it every `math.max(tonumber(CFG.HeatOutputInterval) or 0.1, 0.02)` seconds, forever. `ENT:HeatReadout()`: `hot = Heat.GetKJ(self) > 0`; returns at once when the radiator is cold AND `HeatShown` is false; otherwise `HeatShown = hot` and `UpdateHeatOutputs()` (HeatKJ, HeatF, HeatPct = percent of HOT; nothing without Wire). `Initialize` sets `HeatShown = true`, so the first readout writes the cold state once. `UpdateWireOutputs` writes the other ten outputs (no heat ones); `Finish` (end of a sweep) still calls it.
- **Load check [REMOVED in 15.7, it caused the breakage]:** the radiator file needed `HeatOutputInterval` to be a number, else one `ErrorNoHalt` line (it names the knob and `config_lines_15_5.txt`) and the file stops (no class, no timers).
- **Lazer:** one line: `HeatShown` starts `true` (it started `false` in 15.5). Everything else of 15.5 is as it was.

### Heat changes of 15.5 (code map)
- **Readout clock (lazer `init.lua`):** `ENT:HeatReadout(now)` is called from `ENT:Think` every tick, after `HeatThink` and the `IsValid` check (a lazer its own heat just destroyed is not touched), before `RefreshPower`. It does nothing until `NextHeatRead`; then sets `NextHeatRead = now + HeatOutputInterval`, asks `Heat.GetKJ(self) > 0`, and returns at once when the lazer is cold AND the readout already shows cold (`HeatShown` false); otherwise sets `HeatShown = hot`, calls `UpdateHeatOutputs()` and `UpdateOverlay()`. `UpdateHeatOutputs` writes `HeatKJ`, `HeatF`, `HeatPct` (not at all without Wire). `UpdateWireOutputs` no longer writes them.
- **Hover-text buffer:** `ENT.OverlayDelay = LazerOverlayDelay` at file scope. ACF's `UpdateOverlay` rebuilds immediately on the first call, then at most once per `OverlayDelay` (it queues the calls in between and runs one when the timer ends); the overlay is sent only to players who are tracking the entity.
- **Load check [REMOVED in 15.7, it caused the breakage]:** the lazer file needed `HeatOutputInterval` and `LazerOverlayDelay` to be numbers, else one `ErrorNoHalt` line (it names both knobs and `config_lines_15_5.txt`) and the file stops.

### Heat changes of 15.4 (code map; all numbers are knobs in the HEAT and RADIATOR blocks of the config)
- **Cooling rate (config):** `ACFLazers.GetHeatCoolRate(mult) = mult x max(HeatCoolSpeed, 0) / HeatCoolTau` is the ONE place the rate `a` is worked out; `ACFLazers.HeatCool(theta, dt, mult)` uses it (it returns theta unchanged when `a <= 0`), `ACFLazers.GetCoolingSettleK` uses `math.max(GetHeatCoolRate(mult), 1e-12)`. `Heat.GetCoolMult(ent)`: `acf_lazer` -> `HeatCoolMultLazer`, `acf_radiator` -> `HeatCoolMultRad` (5), any other `acf_` class -> `HeatCoolMultACF`, anything else 1.
- **Crew (heat file, ignite sweep):** `local seated = CFG.HeatIgniteSeated == true`; a player is only looked at when `ply:Alive() and (seated or not ply:InVehicle())`, and only then is the `ACFLazers_CanIgnite` veto asked; a skipped player does not renew the touch time, so a burning player who sits down is put out after `HeatIgniteLinger`.
- **Radiator manager (`acf_radiator/init.lua`, `Rad.RunGroup`, every `RadSweepInterval` 0.25 s):** step 0 the strength of each radiator from its tank level (`GetRadCoolantMult(GetFill())`) and the vent (`Vent` drains the tank and sets the vent rate), gather the hot members, `sap = GetRadSap(width) x radiator.CoolantMult` (NO overclock), `RadPoolStep` (what the pool really takes: the sap or what the hot parts hold, shared by temperature in `WeightedFill`), step 3b the flush (litres vented x `RadFlushKJPerLiter`, taken from what the parts hold AFTER step 3), the radiator's own waste heat, `Finish()` (no argument any more). The radiator no longer has a `HeatThink` damage branch; its hover state has no "over" and no "off" kind (kinds now: nocfw, free, vent, dry, empty, cold, hot, work).
- **Flush drain:** `GetRadFlushRate(capacity) = capacity x RadFlushFraction / RadFlushSeconds` litres a second (a 12-wide: 0.25 L/s), constant whatever the level; the flag is read once per sweep.
- **Sounds (radiator `init.lua`):** `StartSound(ent, name, volume, pitch)` = `CreateSound` + `SetSoundLevel(75)` + `PlayEx`; `FadeOutSound(snd, seconds)` = `snd:FadeOut(seconds)` and `timer.Simple(seconds + 0.1, Stop)`, or `Stop` at once when `seconds <= 0`; `fade = ClampNumber(CFG.RadFlushFadeSeconds, 0.8, 0, 10)`. The burst (`VentBurst`) and the hiss (`VentSound`) are both such patches; when a flush ends both fade and are forgotten (`nil`), so a restart during the fade makes a new pair; `OnRemove` stops both at once.
- **Steam sprite (config + `cl_init.lua`):** `ACFLazers.PickRadPuffMaterial(r)` picks from `RadPuffMaterials` (`r` in 0..1, so it can be tested); the client asks it once per puff.
- **The tool (`acf_radiator_tool.lua`):** `GetRadReadout(width)` now returns width, scale, mass, capacity, radius, sap, wasteFrac, settle, eff, net, liters, flushRate, flushKJ (no overclock rows); the panel and the toolgun screen print the big sap number, the size line, "working flat out its own heat settles at N% of HOT", the efficiency line only below 100%, the tank and the flush lines.

### Heat changes of 15.3 (code map; all numbers are knobs in the HEAT and RADIATOR blocks of the config)
- **No gate:** `acf_lazer/init.lua` `ENT:TriggerInput("Fire")` starts the lazer at once when `HasStartEnergy()`; `ENT:Think` restarts a held-Fire dud the moment the pool can pay. Nothing else looks at the heat except `HeatThink` (damage) and the hover text. `CanStart`, `HeatEvaluate`, `HeatHot`, `HeatCooling` and the `Cooling` output do not exist any more.
- **Damage law** (config, `ACFLazers.GetHeatDamagePct(over, base)`; `over` = how far past the limit, 0.2 = 120%): `base x over^2 + HeatTurboPct x ((1 + over) / start)^HeatTurboPower`, the second term only when `1 + over > start` (start = `HeatTurboStartPct` / 100, never under 1). `base` 0 or `over` <= 0 gives 0. Used by the lazer (`TakeHeatDamage`), the capacitor (`HeatThink`) and the radiator (`HeatThink`), each with its own base knob and its own limit; ACF's pipeline (`Heat.Hurt`) applies it.
- **Ignite (heat file, `[CHANGED 2]`):** `Heat.Burning` (weak set of the players WE lit), `Heat.GetBox(ent, reach)` (centre, three unit axes, half sizes from `OBBMins/OBBMaxs` and `LocalToWorld`), `Heat.TouchesPlayer(box, ply)` (the player's `GetPos` + `OBBMins/OBBMaxs`), `Heat.IgniteSweep()` (hot lazers = registry entries of a class in `HeatIgniteClasses` at or over `HeatIgniteF`; for each living player: touching a hot lazer and not vetoed -> light (5 s) or renew, else put out `HeatIgniteLinger` s after the last touch, only for players in `Heat.Burning`), timer `ACFLazers_IgniteSweep`. The pure test is `ACFLazers.ObbTouchesAabb` in the config.
- **Coolant (radiator `init.lua`, `[CHANGED 1]`):** fields `Capacity`, `Amount`, `FuelType`, `FlushWire`, `Venting`, `VentRate`, `CoolantMult`, `PoolBoil`, `CoolantR/G/B`; methods `GetUnitMass`, `SetAmount` (clamped 0..Capacity), `Consume` (negative fills; NaN / text ignored), `GetFill`, `SetVenting` (NW2 bool `ACFRadVent`, burst + loop sounds), `Vent(on, dt)` (drains `min(Amount, rate x dt)`, returns litres). `Rad.RunGroup` order per sweep: (0) each radiator's strength from its tank, then vents if told to (own `Flush` or any in the pool with `RadFlushPool`); (1)-(3) as in 15.2 but the sap rate x strength; (3b) the vent heat `ventKJ` = sum of litres x `RadFlushKJPerLiter` taken from what the parts hold NOW (`WeightedFill` never gives more than they hold); (4) waste, readings, `Finish`. The colour is `NW2Int ACFRadColor` = r x 65536 + g x 256 + b (`PackRadColor` / `UnpackRadColor`, config).
- **Steam (radiator `cl_init.lua`):** `ENT:Think` calls ACF's base Think (it finishes the entity's set-up), asks to run every frame, and while the flag is on makes `ACFLazers.GetRadPuffs(scale, dt, carry)` puffs from a `ParticleEmitter` at random points on the top face of the model; the emitter is finished in `OnRemove`.
- **Supply crate:** `ACF.FuelTanks[self] = true` in `Initialize` (removed in `OnRemove`). ACF's `supply.lua` captures that table at load and, for each tank near the crate and in its contraption that is not full, calls `tank:Consume(-units)` and takes `units x tank:GetUnitMass()` kg from its own stock.

### Radiator (15.2; all numbers are knobs in the RADIATOR block of the config, everything starting with `Rad...`)
- **Files:** `acf_radiator/init.lua` (all the logic), `shared.lua` (base class `acf_base_scalable`, `GetWidth`), `cl_init.lua` (ACF's Draw + the radius sphere), the tool `acf_radiator_tool.lua`; the pure maths is in the config (`ACFLazers.GetRadScale / GetRadMass / GetRadRadius / GetRadSap / GetRadWaste / GetRadEfficiency / ClampRadOverclock / WeightedFill / RadPoolStep / GetCoolingSettleK / GetRadReadout`), so the tool, the hover text and the entity cannot disagree. The heat store (`acf_lazers_heat.lua`) answers `ACFLazers_HeatQuery` with `ACFLazers_HeatReply` (entity, kJ, F, fraction of HOT, mass).
- **Manager (`ACFLazers.Rad`):** `Rad.List` (weak set of radiators), `Rad.Groups` (pools: BFS over `Links`, sorted by entity index, rebuilt when `ACFLazers.LinkVersion` changes or a radiator appeared / went), `Rad.Sweep()` (dt = time since the last sweep, capped at `RadMaxDeltaTime`; the first sweep acts for 0 s), `Rad.RunGroup()` (rebuild member lists if dirty / due, gather hot members, `RadPoolStep`, `WeightedFill`, waste, then each radiator's `Finish`: own damage, state line, wire outputs, hover refresh). One timer `ACFLazers_RadiatorSweep`.
- **Members:** `ENT:RebuildMembers` walks `self:CFW_GetContraption().ents` once and keeps the eligible ones within the radius (squared distance of `WorldSpaceCenter`); eligible = valid, not a player / NPC / radiator, has a physics object. CFW events mark lists dirty (`cfw.contraption.entityAdded / entityRemoved` mark the radiators of THAT contraption and the ones with no contraption yet; `created / removed / merged / split` mark all). Without an event a change is found after `RadRefreshInterval`.
- **[15.4: sap x overclock is now sap only] Work:** per sweep `ACFLazers.RadPoolStep(items, avail, dt)`: every radiator tries sap x overclock x dt x efficiency; if the hot members hold less (`avail`), everybody does proportionally less (factor k); `work` = effort (the waste is a share of it), `removed` = work x efficiency. `ACFLazers.WeightedFill(weights, limits, total)` splits the removal over the hot parts by weight, capped at what each holds, the rest re-shared. Waste = `GetRadWaste(width)` x work, added to the radiator with `Heat.Add`.
- **[REMOVED IN 15.4 - radiators take no heat damage; this bullet is history] Self damage:** `HeatThink`: over 100% of HOT it hurts itself `RadDamage` % of max health per second at 200% x (over)^2 (`GetHeatDamagePct`), through `Heat.Hurt`; a kill calls `HeatDeath` (`ACF.APKill`, debris power capped by `MaxDebrisPower`); a dying radiator is skipped by the sweep.
- **Numbers at a glance (defaults):** width 42 = 158 kg, radius 613, 4,760 kJ/s; width 60 = 650 kg, radius 800, 25,000 kJ/s; width 12 = 15 kg, radius 300, 300 kJ/s. A 50 kg plate at 700 F next to a 42-wide radiator is below 400 F after 1.0 s, 100 F after 1.75 s and at room temperature after 2 s (on its own it is still 667 F after 10 s).

### Lazer draw table (kJ/s; start energy = 1x, LazerStartSeconds 1)
| mm | curve 0 (straight) | 0.25 | 0.5 (13 to 13.3) | **0.75 (default)** | 1 (exponential) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 20 | 100 | 100 | 100 | **100** | 100 |
| 50 | 4,375 | 3,329 | 2,283 | **1,236** | 190 |
| 100 | 11,500 | 8,763 | 6,027 | **3,290** | 554 |
| 160 | 20,050 | 15,538 | 11,025 | **6,512** | 2,000 |
| 200 | 25,750 | 20,489 | 15,229 | **9,968** | 4,707 |
| 300 | 40,000 | 40,000 | 40,000 | **40,000** | 40,000 |

Start energy at the default curve and 1 s (the same numbers as the draw): 100 mm 3,290 kJ, 160 mm 6,512 kJ, 200 mm 9,968 kJ, 300 mm 40,000 kJ. Smallest capacitor that holds it: 100 mm d = 16 (3,860 kJ), 160 mm d = 20 (7,540), 200 mm d = 22 (10,036), 300 mm d = 35 (40,409).

With your server's ends (100 and 40000) `LazerDrawCurve` 0 gives the straight line between them. **Your server value is 0.75 (13.4), which is the default now** (my 13 to 13.3 default was 0.5; this answers old question G11). Set `LazerDrawCurve = 0` for the plain straight line.

A 3,500 hp hub makes 18,200 kJ/s at full health. At the default curve that keeps 5.5 lazers of 100 mm, 2.8 of 160 mm, 1.8 of 200 mm, 0.5 of 300 mm firing nonstop; the capacitors carry the rest. A d = 20 capacitor (7,540 kJ) pays the start energy of anything up to 160 mm and then feeds a 100 mm lazer for 2.3 s (160 mm: 1.2 s). A 300 mm lazer needs d = 35 (40,409 kJ) to start from capacitors alone; a full 60 unit capacitor feeds it for 5.1 s (9.3 s with a 3,500 hp hub filling it at the same time). To make EVERY size cheaper (including the ends) turn `LazerDrawSmall` / `LazerDrawBig` down; the curve only reshapes the middle.

- **Mass (13.4):** lazer mass = `MassMin` (60) + (`MassMax` (9000) - `MassMin`) x t^`MassExponent` (2), t = (caliber - 20) / 280. 20 mm 60 kg, 50 mm 163, 100 mm 790, 160 mm 2,295, 200 mm 3,755, 300 mm 9,000. Capacitor mass = `CapMassPerVolume` (0.03) x pi x d^3: d 6 = 20 kg, 10 = 94, 20 = 754, 30 = 2,545, 60 = 20,358 (the biggest). [KNOW, not tested] the engine caps a physics object at 50,000 kg (above about d = 81); `CapDiameterMax` 60 keeps every capacitor well under it.

### Penetration (13.5: how it works now, from the code)
Three separate things decide what a beam does to a plate. Numbers below are for a 60 mm lazer (default `FocalDefault` 300, Focus 50):
1. **Does the beam get through? (the pass line, unchanged.)** `ACFLazers.GetPassLine(caliber, mult)` = `PenArmourFraction` (1/3) x caliber x the damage multiplier of THIS hit ([SEEN] config `acf_lazers_config.lua:548-549`, used at `acf_lazer/init.lua:984`). At multiplier 1 that is **20 mm of CURRENT armour** for a 60 mm lazer. The beam only gets through a plate whose current armour is BELOW the line. The multiplier is `ENT:CalculateDamageMult` (`init.lua:544-547`) = focal curve x distance falloff = the `DamageMult` wire output (`FocalMult` is the focal curve alone). It does not depend on `PenetrationPerMM`. A destroyed prop never blocks the beam and does not use up the burn-through. People always let it pass. One burn-through per beam: the next prop that survives stops it.
2. **How hard does the plate itself get hit? (ACF's formula, changed by `PenetrationPerMM` 0.3.)** `DamageProp` (`init.lua:883-906`) gives ACF the area (beam cross-section x `HitsPerSecond` x dt x multiplier x cos(angle x `AngleInfluence`) x `beam.scale` x the small-lazer boost) and the ACF penetration `pen` = caliber x `PenetrationPerMM` (`init.lua:903`) = **18 mm** at 0.3 (60 at 1.0). ACF ([SEEN] `acf/damage/objects_sv/damage_result.lua`, `Meta:Compute`) turns it into: effective thickness = armour / |cos(angle)|, ratio = min(1, pen / thickness), **damage = area x ratio^2**. So at angle 0: armour up to 18 mm takes the full area; 20 mm takes 0.81; 30 mm 0.36; 40 mm 0.20; 60 mm 0.09; 100 mm 0.032; 150 mm 0.014. With 1.0 everything up to 60 mm took the full area, 100 mm took 0.36 and 150 mm 0.16: a 60 to 100 mm plate now takes about 11 times less than before, a 40 mm plate about 5 times less (both from ACF's formula, tested in `t_pen.lua`).
3. **What does the stuff behind a burned-through plate take? (rule changed in 13.5.)** At the burn-through, `beam.scale` = `PenDamageFraction` (1/4) x howFar, where howFar = clamp((line - armour now) / (line x (1 - `PenRampEnd` 1/2)), 0, 1): the full 1/4 at armour 10 mm or less (for 60 mm at multiplier 1), a straight slide to nothing at the line (15 mm: 1/8; 20 mm: nothing) (`init.lua:987-995`). **New: `beam.penMult` = the multiplier the burned-through plate took, and everything behind uses THAT multiplier** (`init.lua:996` and `1026-1030`), not the one at its own distance. So behind-damage = (the plate's area) x 1/4 x howFar, distance does not make it smaller (before, a prop 7000 units farther behind took 1/4 x the weaker multiplier of its own distance, for example 0.6 of the new value at 1000 and 8000 units). The weakened beam still gets the angle effect of whatever it lands on, and the small-lazer boost is the same for all (it depends on the caliber).
- Pass line by distance (60 mm lazer): 20.0 mm at multiplier 1 (muzzle), 19.9 at the focal distance (0.9956), 19.0 at 1000 units (0.950), 14.1 at 5000 (0.705), 9.95 at 10000 (0.498), 4.7 at 20000 (0.236), 1.8 at 50000 (0.090). Sharp focus (Focus above 50) raises the centre multiplier up to 1.4x, so the line can reach 28 mm there. The 60 mm lazer's effective range is 17,143 units ([SEEN] `ACFLazers.GetEffectiveRange`, interpolated between 15000 and 30000).
- Which plates can ever be pierced: ACF lowers armour as health drops, never below half of the original (`doPropDamage`, [SEEN] `acf/damage/damage_sv.lua:244-245`). So only plates under about **40 mm original** (half under the 20 mm line) can be burned through at multiplier 1; thicker plates are only destroyed. The small-lazer boost (`GetSmallDamageBoost(60)` = 1.857) multiplies the damage area only, not the line and not `pen`.
- **Open (G17):** ACF's `pen` number does not follow the damage multiplier (only the area does), so far away a beam hits with the same 18 mm of ACF penetration even though its pass line has shrunk. Say so if you want `pen` to follow the multiplier as well.

### Fusion engine (17: what changed in the 16.9 section below; everything not named here is as in 16.9)
- **Heat (22, REPLACES the heat line below):** the engine heats itself every pass by `GetFusionStaticHeat(hp, charge, health) x dt` = `FusionStaticHeat` 0.15 x `GetFusionStandardHeat(hp)` (= `FusionHeatScalar` x `GetFusionRatedRate(hp)`, the full output) x charge / `GetFusionChargeMax(hp)` (held to 0..1) x `GetFusionHeatMult(health)` (1 at full health, `FusionDamagedHeatMult` 5 at none). The old term (stored kJ x `FusionHeatScalar` x the multiplier) is added only when `FusionEnergyHeatMult` is above 0 (x that number). The hover shows "Heat made = N kJ/s from its charge (X% charged, xY from damage)".
- **Throttle:** the Active input is a fraction 0..1 (`GetFusionThrottle`: under 0 and NaN = 0, over 1 = 1). The operating percent steps toward `GetFusionTarget(throttle, ceiling)` = min(throttle, ceiling) with the same `StepFusionOperating(operating, dt, hp, wantOn, target, canGrow)` (wantOn = throttle > 0 and parented). Lowering the throttle or the ceiling makes it fall at `FusionSpinDownMult` x the spin-up rate; raising it climbs at the spin-up rate; a PPC wait still blocks growth.
- **Size:** `GetFusionSizeFraction(hp)` = slider + `FusionSizeCurve` x ((1 - e^(-rate x slider)) / (1 - e^(-rate)) - slider), rate = `FusionSizeCurveRate` held to 0.001 .. 100, blend held to 0 .. 1. `GetFusionScale(hp)` = Lerp(size, `FusionScaleSmall`, `FusionScaleBig`), `GetFusionMass(hp)` = max(Lerp(**slider** (20; 17 to 19: size), `FusionMassSmall`, `FusionMassBig`), 1): the mass follows the rated power in a straight line, not the volume of the model. The armour (`FusionArmourAtOne` x scale x `ACF.ArmorMod`), the health (surface area) and the heat capacity (mass x 0.49) follow. Spin-up, PPC scalar, PPC wait stay on the straight slider. [20: `FusionSizeCurve` ships at 0.75, and the MASS is not on this curve any more.]
- **Heat:** per pass the engine gets `stored kJ x FusionHeatScalar x GetFusionHeatMult(health fraction)`; the multiplier is Lerp(health, `FusionDamagedHeatMult`, 1) (2 at 0% health by default; a knob under 0 is held to 0).
- **Hum:** `GetFusionHum(hp, operating)` returns `{ on, volume, pitch, level, size }`; the engine holds one `CreateSound` patch (see the code map). Server-side, so every nearby player hears it.

### Fusion engine (16.9, with the 17 changes in the section above; all numbers are knobs in the FUSION ENGINES block of the config, everything starting with `Fusion...`)
- **Entity:** `acf_fusion` (`entities/acf_fusion/init.lua`, `shared.lua`, `cl_init.lua`), based on `acf_base_scalable`. Spawn order as the generator's: `SetScaledModel`, `SetScale`, `ACF.Activate(self, true)`, then `Contraption.SetMass`. The rating (`RatedHP`) is a plain entity field (the duplicator saves it) and the network float `ACFFusionHP`.
- **The slider:** `FusionHPMin` 250 .. `FusionHPMax` 6500 (default 6500). `frac = (hp - 250) / 6250`; mass = 1,500 + 8,500 x frac kg; model scale = 0.25 + 0.75 x frac; spin-up = 8 + 7 x frac seconds; PPC scalar = 1 - 0.5 x frac; longest PPC wait = 3 + 2 x frac seconds (`FusionMassSmall/Big`, `FusionScaleSmall/Big`, `FusionSpinUpSmall/Big`, `FusionPPCScalarSmall/Big`, `FusionPPCDelayMaxSmall/Big`). A junk rating (nothing, text, NaN, under 250, over 6500) is clamped.
- **Operating percent (a fraction 0..1):** `StepFusionOperating(operating, dt, hp, wantOn, ceiling, canGrow)`. wantOn = Active (wire, nothing wired = `FusionActiveDefault`) AND parented. Off: falls to 0 at `FusionSpinDownMult` x the spin-up rate. Over the ceiling: falls to the ceiling the same way. Waiting out a PPC hit (`now < RecoverAt`): stays. Otherwise climbs at `1 / spin-up seconds` per second up to the ceiling. Not parented: set to 0 at once.
- **Ceiling (efficiency):** `GetFusionEfficiency(health, heat)` = health efficiency (1 at full health, `FusionDamagedEfficiency` 0.5 at none, a straight line) x heat efficiency (1 up to `FusionHeatEffStart` 0.5 of the critical rise, then a straight line down to `FusionHeatEffAtCritical` 0.5 at the critical heat).
- **Energy:** `ACFLazers.GetFusionRate(hp, operating)` = `hp x GenKJPerHP x operating` kJ/s. Every `FusionThinkInterval` (0.1 s) `Run(now, dt)`: `dt` is capped at `FusionMaxDeltaTime` (0.25 s), wanted kJ = rate x dt, stored kJ = min(wanted, free room of the group), shared by `ACFLazers.WaterFill` over the capacitors that have room and `AllowCharge` on. The reading (wire `Energy`, hover "Making") is the stored kJ per second smoothed with `FusionPowerSmoothing` (0.4 s).
- **Heat:** the engine gets `stored kJ x FusionHeatScalar` (0.25) kJ of heat per pass (`Heat.Add`); nothing stored (no room, off, not parented) = no heat. The heat store's own cooling applies as for any part. Heat capacity = mass x 0.49 kJ/K (4,900 kJ/K at 6500 hp). Critical = `FusionCriticalF` 1500 F = 800.56 K above room temperature. `GetHeatFraction()` = rise / critical rise.
- **Charge:** `StepFusionCharge(charge, operating, hp, dt)`: held = operating x store (store = `FusionChargeSeconds` 10 x the full rate: 13,000 kJ at 250 hp, 338,000 kJ at 6500 hp); the charge never falls below `held`, and otherwise drains by (store / `FusionChargeDrainSeconds` 60) per second, never above the store. It is the engine's blast.
- **Critical heat and destruction:** `CheckCritical()` at the end of every pass: heat fraction below 1 clears `AtCritical`; at 1 or more it sets it and calls `Detonate()` unless `ACFLazers.IsProtectedBySafezone(self)` (the config's `InSafeZone` flag or ACF's own zone test). `ACF_OnDamage` runs ACF's `Damage.doPropDamage` and detonates when the result says Kill (blaming the attacker). `Detonate()`: once only (`Exploding`); filler = `GetFusionBlastFiller(charge)` = max(charge, `FusionBlastMinKJ` 0.1) x `FusionBlastMult` / `ACF.HEPower`; charge and operating percent go to 0; in a safezone it just removes itself; otherwise `ACF.KillChildProps`, `Damage.createExplosion(pos, filler, filler / 2, {self}, info)`, `Damage.explosionEffect`, `constraint.RemoveAll`, `Remove`.
- **PPC hits:** `hook.Add("ACFLazers_PPCImpact", ...)` goes over every engine; `PPCHit(pos, caliber, now)` refuses (returns false) when the engine is exploding, in a safezone, farther than `FusionPPCRange` (300) from its centre, or idle (operating 0 and not waiting). The cut = `GetFusionPPCCut(caliber, hp)` = min(the PPC's percent per shot / 100 x the engine's scalar x `FusionPPCCutMult`, 1), taken off the operating percent; `RecoverAt = now + Lerp(random 0..1, FusionPPCDelayMin 1, the engine's longest wait)`.
- **The capacitor group, health and armour, links, hover text and wire outputs:** as in 16.8 (the group is `ACFLazers.GetCapGroup(self.Caps)` cached until `ACFLazers.LinkVersion` changes; health = area / `ACF.Threshold` x `FusionHealthMult`; armour = `FusionArmourAtOne` x scale x `ACF.ArmorMod`; links `acf_fusion` <-> `acf_capacitor` through the registry; dupes store the linked capacitors in `ACFFusionCaps`). New: the `Active` input, the outputs `Operating`, `Efficiency`, `Charge`, `ChargePct`, `HeatKJ`, `HeatF`, `HeatPct`, `Parented`, and the hover lines of section "What 16.9 is".
- **Tool readout:** `ACFLazers.GetFusionReadout(hp)` returns the energy at 100%, the damaged rate, the reference capacitor's fill time, the mass, scale, spin-up, charge store, the bang, the PPC scalar and waits; the menu panel and the toolgun screen draw it.

### Fusion engine (16.8, REPLACED by the 16.9 section above; kept only for the history: the two sizes, the Draw input and the types do not exist any more)
- **Entity:** `acf_fusion` (`entities/acf_fusion/init.lua`, `shared.lua`, `cl_init.lua`), based on `acf_base_scalable` like the generator. Spawn order (the same as the lazer, the capacitor and the generator use): `SetScaledModel`, `SetScale`, `ACF.Activate(self, true)` (that makes the physics object and calls `ACF_Activate`), then `Contraption.SetMass`.
- **Sizes:** `FusionTypes` in the config, a list of `{ id, name, mass, scale, hp }` in the order the tool lists them: Large (10000 kg, scale 1, 6500 hp) and Small (1500 kg, scale 0.25, 250 hp). A line that is not usable (no id, a mass or scale that is not above 0, an hp that is below 0 or not a real number) is skipped; if none is usable the built-in two are used. `FusionTypeDefault` ("large") is what a junk size becomes. The size is stored in the entity field `FusionType` (the duplicator saves entity fields) and in the network string `ACFFusionType`.
- **Energy:** `ACFLazers.GetFusionRate(id, health, draw)` = `hp x GenKJPerHP x GetGenEfficiency(health) x draw` kJ/s, with health and draw clamped to 0..1. Every `FusionThinkInterval` (0.1 s) `Run(now, dt)`: `dt` is capped at `FusionMaxDeltaTime` (0.25 s: a lag spike cannot hand out a pile), wanted kJ = rate x dt, stored kJ = min(wanted, free room of the group), shared by `ACFLazers.WaterFill` over the capacitors that have room and `AllowCharge` on, `cap:AddCharge(share)`. If `FusionHeatScalar` is above 0 the engine gets `stored kJ x FusionHeatScalar` kJ of heat (`Heat.Add`). The reading (wire `Energy`, hover "Making") is the stored kJ per second smoothed with the time constant `FusionPowerSmoothing` (0.4 s).
- **Draw:** `GetDraw()` = the wired value / `FusionDrawMax` (100) clamped to 0..1; unwired it is `FusionDrawDefault` (100) / `FusionDrawMax` = 1. A Wire that has no `Inputs` table still works through the value last sent to `TriggerInput`.
- **The capacitor group:** `ACFLazers.GetCapGroup(self.Caps)` (every capacitor connected to a linked one), cached until `ACFLazers.LinkVersion` changes. The room list leaves out capacitors that do not exist, are exploding, are full or refuse charge; a capacitor with room that refuses is counted (`Refusing`) so the status can say "AllowCharge is off".
- **Health and armour:** `ACF_Activate` copies the gun's recipe: area from the physics object's surface area x `ACF.InchToCmSq`; health = area / `ACF.Threshold` x `FusionHealthMult`; armour = `FusionArmourAtOne` x model scale x `ACF.ArmorMod` (40 mm at scale 1, 10 mm at 0.25 before the multiplier); the current armour falls with the health like ACF's other parts; a re-activation (a size change) keeps the health share.
- **Links:** `ACF.RegisterLinkSource("acf_fusion", "Caps")`, `RegisterClassLink("acf_fusion", "acf_capacitor", ...)` and the unlink twin; the link function refuses a pair that is already linked (the registry refuses every other class pair: only capacitors have a link); a link puts the capacitor in the engine's `Caps` and the engine in the capacitor's `Generators` set (the set the hub uses), bumps `ACFLazers.LinkVersion` (so every pool rebuilds) and refreshes both hover texts. `UnlinkAll`, `OnRemove` (every capacitor forgets the engine), `PreEntityCopy` / `PostEntityPaste` (the `ACFFusionCaps` modifier, ids of the capacitors) are the hub's pattern.
- **Hover text and wire outputs** refresh every `FusionOutputInterval` (0.25 s) and when the status, the size, the rate (in steps of 10 kJ/s), the draw, the efficiency or the heat (steps of 5%) change.
- **Tool readout:** `ACFLazers.GetFusionReadout(id)` returns the name, scale, mass, hp, rate, rate when damaged (x`GenDamagedEfficiency`), the reference capacitor's kJ (`FusionToolCapSize`, 60 units) and the fill times; the menu panel and the toolgun screen draw it, the client caches it per pick.

### Flamethrower (22; all numbers are knobs in the FLAMETHROWER block of the config, everything starting with `Flame...`)
- **The gun.** `acf_flamer` throws a projectile every 1/7 s while its Fire wire input is above 0 and a linked napalm tank has fuel. The schedule keeps its rhythm (70 projectiles in 10 s) and does not make up for a stall. Each projectile costs a seventh of a second's fuel (`GetFlameFuelPerShot`); the last drop is burned in a smaller puff. No free ACF projectile slot, or the ammo file missing = nothing is released, no fuel burned, the owner is told once. A gun in a safezone throws flames that touch nothing.
- **The projectile.** An ACF bullet of the type `LazerFlame` with ACF's gravity and drag (x`FlameDragMult`, 40 since 22.1), the speed of the shooter added, a COPY of the contraption's filter (its own parts are never touched; a player seated in its vehicle neither), and `KillTime` = 3 s (ACF removes it then before anything else is looked at). The thin ray (ACF's own) ends the flight on a prop or the world; it looks THROUGH people (the hook), so only a part, the ground or the 3 s end it.
- **The sphere.** After every step the code finds the entities near the step (`ents.FindInSphere`, one search per step, centred on the step) and tests the swept sphere against each entity's bounding box in its own turned space (`SegmentTouchesBox`), with the radius of the projectile's age (8 units at the muzzle, 24 at 3 s). A thing is touched once per projectile. On a hit of the ray the same test is run once more where the flight ended.
- **A part (a prop or any ACF thing that is not a person).** Allowed? (not in a safezone, the hook `ACFLazers_CanFlame` not false, ACF's damage permission not false). Then: `Heat.SetFire` (the cooling of a plain prop halves until the fire is out), heat (`FlameHeat` percent of HOT x the part's heat capacity), the game's fire (`Entity:Ignite`, at most once per half second per entity), and if the part is now at or over `FlameDamageHeatPct` of HOT: `Heat.Hurt` (ACF's damage call, so protection and hooks apply; it is DMG_BURN, and the attacker is what the heat store names for the part, `Heat.GetAttacker`) for `FlameHP`; a part that dies is destroyed the way ACF destroys what a bullet killed (`ACF.APKill`). The first part starts the linger fuze (the projectile ends 0.05 s later, at least the next server tick).
- **A person (ACF type "Squishy": players, NPCs).** Dead ones and ones at 0 health are not touched. A hit = `FlamePlayerDamage` as burn damage (the shooter is the attacker, the gun the inflictor) and `FlameIgnite` seconds of fire (at most one Ignite call per half second); the projectile goes on. A person is hit once per projectile; the next projectile hits again.
- **The heat store (shared with the heat checker and the other parts).** A plain prop on fire cools `FlameCoolMult` as fast until `ACFL_FireUntil`; an `acf_` entity never does (it cools as it always did). The cooling law is exact, so the time before and after the fire ends adds up.
- **Napalm.** The fuel type `Napalm` only exists for the flamethrower: an ACF engine links to a tank whose fuel is in its own list, and napalm is in nobody's.
- **Picture and sound (client).** ACF's own bullet picture is kept for the time it lives (its 45 s limit is moved to the 3 s life); the projectile's model is not drawn, a trail of fire sprites (60 a second; since 22.1 each is as wide as the sphere was when it was made and grows to the sphere's width when it dies) follows it, every 3rd projectile carries a quiet loop (only near you), a burst of 8 sprites where it ended. With `acf_lazers_flame_sphere 1` the real sphere is drawn as a wireframe.

### Generator hub (rating limit and damage new in 13)
- Left-click with the tool spawns it. The slider is the **rated power in hp** (50 to 3500). The model scale follows through GenScaleKnots: 50 hp = 0.5, 350 hp = 1, 3500 hp = 2. Left-click on an existing hub updates it in place.
- **Input point** `GenInputPos` (20, 0, 1) x scale, facing +X. An engine must sit within **45 degrees** of straight ahead of that point (hub half). ACF's own 85 degrees still applies to the engine half. Distance 650 units, stretch 1.5x the rope length at link time. Re-checked every second. A bad link breaks with a warning in chat and a clunk. (These are the ONLY things that break a link now.)
- **Private load:** the hub never enters the engine's own gearbox list. Each tick it lowers each linked engine's `FlyRPM` itself, the way ACF's engine loses rpm to a gearbox ([SEEN] `acf_engine/init.lua:812-859`).
- Load: `torque = Draw x GenLoadStrength x T_ref x (rpm / peak rpm)^GenLoadExponent`, `T_ref = peak kW x 9548.8 / peak rpm`. Flywheel loses `torque / Inertia x ticks`, never below idle.
- **Rating limit (13):** the hub first works out what its load would take from every engine this tick (`OfferedHP`, hp). If that is more than the rated hp, EVERY engine's load is scaled by the same share (rated / offered), so the hub takes exactly its rating in total and the engines feel no more drag than that. Nothing breaks. An engine held back this way simply revs higher (a 1,451 hp turbine on a 350 hp hub sits near its 13,000 rpm limiter, [SEEN] `turbine.lua` RPM Limit).
  - `OfferedHP` is the load the hub WOULD take at the engines' present rpm, not what an engine can give: it grows with rpm (load ~ rpm^2 at the defaults). So the hover text quotes the engines' RATED hp instead (`ENT:GetEnginesRatedHP`, peak power x 1.341 x GenEngineHPMult, added up).
- **Damage (13):** `efficiency = GetGenEfficiency(health)` = 1 at full health down to `GenDamagedEfficiency` (0.5) at 0 health, straight line. It multiplies the kJ stored. The engines are loaded exactly the same (same hp, same rpm); only the energy that lands in the capacitors drops. Health = ACF.Health / ACF.MaxHealth (counts as 1 when missing or NaN).
- **The hum (21).** `ENT:UpdateHum` runs once per output interval (0.25 s) from `Run`. Load = `PowerHP / RatedHP`; `GetGenHum(RatedHP, load)` gives the volume, pitch and level (the 21 block has the table). The patch is made when the hub first takes power (`CreateSound` on the hub, `SetSoundLevel`, then `PlayEx`), glides to the new volume and pitch as the load moves, and is stopped when nothing is taken, when the hub is removed, and when the file or the level (a resize) changes. Everything is wrapped in a `pcall`: a failing sound prints one console line and the hub keeps generating.
- Energy: `kJ = hp taken x GenKJPerHP x efficiency x seconds`, hp = flywheel kW x 1.341 x GenEngineHPMult. Default 5.2 kJ per hp-second (13.1; was 2.6): 3500 hp fills a 60 unit capacitor (203,575 kJ) in 11.19 s (healthy); 0.5 health efficiency doubles that. One flywheel kW is 6.97 kJ/s.
- **Wire inputs / outputs:** input `Draw` (0 take nothing, 100 take all, unwired counts as 100). Outputs `Energy` (kJ/s stored, includes the efficiency), `HP`, `Rated`, `Load`, `Engines`, `Full`.
- **No room** (all linked capacitors full, or AllowCharge off): the hub does not touch the engines.
- **Hover text:** Rated power, Taking from engines, Making (kJ/s), **Health efficiency**, Draw input, Linked. Status line: "Generating N kJ/s", or "At its N hp rating (the linked engines are rated M hp, the extra is ignored): generating N kJ/s".
- **Ropes** only while ACF's `acf_mobilityropelinks` is on ([SEEN] `acf/core/globals.lua:442`, default 0).
- Engines still need Active + Throttle from the player's own wiring. The hub takes nothing at zero throttle or on the rev limiter, like an ACF gearbox.

### Generator tool readout (13)
- Menu (under the slider): a dark panel, big amber "1,820 kJ/s" for 350 hp, with "350 hp x 5.2 kJ per hp", "at 0% health: 910 kJ/s (x0.5)" and "60 unit cap full in 1 min 51 s". Follows the slider live.
- Toolgun screen: the same block under "GENERATOR HUB / 350 hp rating makes".
- Below the panel: model scale, mass, the rule "it takes at most N hp from its engines, a bigger engine is not harmed", the reference capacitor size in kJ, the damage multiplier.
- All numbers come from `ACFLazers.GetGenReadout(hp)` in the config (so tool and hub cannot disagree). The 60 in "60 unit cap" is the knob `GenToolCapSize` (display only).

### Linking tools
All four tools (lazer, capacitor, generator and, since 16.8, the fusion engine tool) install the same link kit (`ACFLazers.LinkKit.Install`). Right-click selects (turns green), a second right-click links all selected to the aimed-at thing, Shift + right-click multi-selects, Reload unlinks, holster or right-click on nothing clears. Link pairs that work: engine > generator, generator > capacitor, **fusion engine > capacitor (16.8)**, capacitor <-> capacitor, capacitor > lazer.

### What ACF does (also in the Docs page)
- Engine tick `ENT:Think` ([SEEN] `acf_engine/init.lua:746`): rev limiter (780-788), friction drag `(1 - Throttle)` (812), torque = throttle x curve x peak (818), flywheel update (824), available torque above idle (831), gearbox requests (834-855, only with throttle above 0), flywheel pays (859).
- Power: kW = Nm x rpm / 9548.8 ([SEEN] `acf_engine/init.lua:538`), hp = kW x 1.341 ([SEEN] `globals.lua:198`). `ACF.TorqueMult` (5) only multiplies torque applied to wheels and display numbers.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------
## 5. Config knobs

Source of truth: `lua/autorun/acf_lazers_config.lua` (search `[CHANGED n]`). A readable knob table is on the Energy Map page (NOT yet updated for the heat knobs). Added or changed in 13, 13.1, 13.2, 13.4, 13.5, 14, 15, 15.1, 15.2, 15.3, 15.4, 15.5, 15.6 and 15.7:

| Knob | Default | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| HeatSpecificHeat | **0.49** (15, new) | kJ per kg per K (steel). The heat capacity of everything = mass x this |
| HeatMinMassKg | 1 (15) | kg. An entity with no/tiny mass counts as at least this |
| HeatAmbientK / HeatHEPowerFallback | 288.15 / 8000 (15) | used ONLY when `ACF.AmbientTemperature` / `ACF.HEPower` are missing |
| HeatHotF | **1100** (15) | F. 100% of a plate; a plate this hot can be burned through |
| HeatPenGate | **true** (15) | true: the beam burns through only HOT plates (plus the old armour rule). false: exactly the 14 behaviour |
| HeatCoolTau / HeatCoolHotBoost | **300** s (15.4: back to the 15.2 value; 15.3 had 150) / 1 (15) | the base cooling time constant; twice as fast at HOT with boost 1. Leave `HeatCoolTau` alone and use `HeatCoolSpeed` |
| HeatCoolSpeed | **1.25** (15.4, your request) | the multiplier on every natural cooling, relative to the 15.2 speed: 1 = 15.2 (300 s), 2 = 15.3 (150 s), 1.25 = 240 s. 0 = nothing cools by itself. Rate = mult x HeatCoolSpeed / HeatCoolTau (`ACFLazers.GetHeatCoolRate`) |
| HeatCoolMultLazer / HeatCoolMultACF | 1 / 1 (15) | cooling speed of lazers / of the other ACF entities, relative to props |
| HeatCoolMultRad | **5** (15.4, your request) | cooling speed of RADIATORS relative to props (only the class `acf_radiator`) |
| HeatForgetBelowK / HeatSweepInterval / HeatCheckInterval / HeatMaxDeltaTime | 0.5 K / 5 s / 0.1 s / 0.5 s (15) | bookkeeping and lag protection |
| HeatLazerSmall / Big | **5 / 5** (15.1, your request: x5; was 1 / 1) | lazer heat per kJ spent, smallest .. biggest lazer (also feeds the beam's hit heat) |
| HeatHitSmall / Big | 1 / 1 (15) | beam hit heat multiplier on top of the lazer's heat rate |
| HeatCapSmall / Big | 1/3 / 1/3 (15) | capacitor heat per kJ moved, each direction |
| HeatGenSmall / Big | 1 / 1 (15) | generator heat per kJ made |
| HeatLazerCapF | **1100** (15) | F. 100% of a lazer |
| HeatLockStartPct | REMOVED in 15.3 | (was 90: % of the lazer limit where it was marked overheated) |
| HeatLockEndPctSmall / Big | REMOVED in 15.3 | (was 25 / 25: the percent the lazer had to cool to before it could fire again) |
| HeatLazerDamage | **4** (15) | % of max health per second at 200% of the limit; quadratic |
| HeatExplodeChance / HeatExplodeKJMult / HeatExplosionMult | **0.5** / 2 / 1 (15) | death while firing: chance of an explosion, its energy as a multiple of the draw, extra multiplier |
| HeatDeathDebrisPower | 20000 (15, [GUESS]) | debris power of a plain heat death (capped by `MaxDebrisPower`) |
| HeatCapCritFrac / HeatCapDamage | **0.5 / 4** (15) | capacitor critical heat as a share of the HOT excess; its quadratic damage |
| HeatInfoDistance | 4096 (15) | units the `acf_lazers_heat_info` trace reaches |
| RadRoundModel | `models/lasers/roundsink.mdl` (15.8) | the roundsink's model; missing = a roundsink refuses to spawn and the chat names this knob |
| RadRoundRadius / RadRoundHeight | 13 / 46 (15.8, your numbers; the mesh measures 13.8 / 46.2) | the roundsink's radius and height at scale 1: the scale is radius / 13; the volume is pi x r x r x h |
| RadRoundMin / Max / Default | 12 / **30** / 13 (15.8, changed in 15.9: the max was 60) | the tool slider for the roundsink, in RADIUS units (30 = a diameter of 60); the start is held inside the range |
| (RadBeyondMax) | **GONE in 15.9** | was a 1/0 switch for the rule past the biggest heatsink; the rule is always on now; a leftover line in a config is ignored |
| RadModel / RadModelWidth | `models/lasers/heatsink.mdl` / 42 (15.2) | the radiator model and its width (and length) at scale 1; scale = width / this |
| RadWidthMin / Max / Default | 12 / 60 / 42 (15.2, [GUESS]) | the tool slider (units) |
| RadMassMin / Max | 15 / 650 kg (15.2, [GUESS]) | mass at the narrowest / widest; a power law between |
| RadArmourAtOne / RadHealthMult | 40 mm / 1 (15.2, [GUESS]) | armour at model scale 1 (scales with the model) and the health multiplier |
| LazerMaxPerPlayer | **40** (15.7, NEW; 40 also when the config has none) | the default `sbox_maxacf_lazers` gets when nothing else has set it (how many lazers one player may have; negative = no limit [GUESS]). **Since 16.8 nothing sets it at start (the 16.7 limits script is gone): the number on your server rules** |
| EntityLimitsFromConfig | **REMOVED in 16.8** (16.7 only) | the switch of the 16.7 limits script, which is gone. An old config that still has the line is harmless |
| CapMaxPerPlayer | **40** (15.7; was 20) | the default of `sbox_maxacf_capacitors` (your server rules since 16.8) |
| GenMaxPerPlayer | **40** (15.7; was 10) | the default of `sbox_maxacf_generators` (your server rules since 16.8) |
| RadMaxPerPlayer | **40** (15.7; was 10 from 15.2; 40 also when the config has none) | the default of `sbox_maxacf_radiators` (your server rules since 16.8) |
| RadRadiusSmall / Big | 300 / 800 (15.2) | radius (units), straight line along the width |
| RadSapSmall / Big / Curve | **1500 / 125000** kJ/s / 1 (15.4: 5x the 15.3 numbers; was 300 / 25000) | the heat a radiator eats per second with a full tank (there is no overclock any more); curve 0 = straight line, 1 = power law (default), in between = a mix |
| RadWeightPower | 1 (15.2) | a part's share of the cooling = (its temperature above room) ^ this (0 = everyone gives the same, 2 = the hottest takes far more) |
| RadOverclockDefault / Max | REMOVED in 15.4 | (was 1 / 6: the unwired Overclock input and the largest overclock) |
| RadWasteSmall / Big | 0.01 / 0.01 (15.2) | waste heat as a share of the work it tries to do, narrowest / widest |
| RadEffStartPct / RadEffFloorPct | 50 / 40 (15.2) | efficiency is 100% up to this percent of HOT, falls in a line to the floor percent at 100% of HOT |
| RadDamage | REMOVED in 15.4 | (was 4: radiators take no heat damage any more) |
| RadSweepInterval / RadMaxDeltaTime / RadRefreshInterval | 0.25 s / 1 s / 15 s (15.2) | bookkeeping: the one timer, the lag cap, the member-list safety refresh |
| RadDrawAimDistance / RadDrawColor / RadDrawFillAlpha / RadDrawLines | 4096 / { 255, 170, 40 } / 10 / 24 (15.2) | the radius sphere |
| RadReadoutDistance / RadReadoutInterval | 256 / 0.25 s (15.2) | the look-at temperature readout: how far it reaches, how often a player may ask |
| HeatTurboStartPct / HeatTurboPct / HeatTurboPower | 120 / 20 / 6 (15.3, your request; "play with the knobs") | turbo damage: past this percent of its limit a self-heating thing takes `HeatTurboPct` percent of MAX health per second at exactly the start, x (how hot / start) ^ power. 0 for the percent = off |
| HeatIgniteF / HeatIgniteLinger / HeatIgniteReach | 500 / 1 / 0 (15.3, your numbers) | a lazer at or over this F ignites players whose collision box touches it; the fire is put out this many seconds after the last touch; the lazer's box is grown by this many units |
| HeatIgniteInterval / HeatIgniteLife / HeatIgniteClasses | 0.1 s / 5 s / { acf_lazer = true } (15.3) | seconds between the checks; the length of one light (renewed while touching); the classes that ignite |

| HeatOutputInterval | **0.1** s (15.5; radiators too since 15.6; **OPTIONAL since 15.7**: 0.1 s when the line is missing) | seconds between refreshes of a lazer's heat readout (the wire outputs `HeatKJ` / `HeatF` / `HeatPct` and its hover text) and of a radiator's (the three wire outputs; one timer, never less than 0.02 s) while it holds heat; a refresh only; no cooling number depends on it |
| LazerOverlayDelay | **0.25** s (15.5; **OPTIONAL since 15.7**: 0.25 s when the line is missing) | the lazer's hover-text buffer (`ENT.OverlayDelay`); ACF's default is 1 s, its piledriver uses 0.1 |

| CapEnergyScale | **1** (13.1; was 2/3) | extra factor on capacitor capacity |
| CapMassPerVolume | **0.03** (13.4, your server; was 0.009, 0.004) | capacitor mass per cubic unit (60 unit = 20,358 kg) |
| LazerStartSeconds | **1** (13.4, your server; was 3, 0.5) | start energy = this many seconds of the lazer's draw (the 90% full rule still overrides) |
| MuzzleVolumeSmall / MuzzleVolumeBig | **1.4 / 2.4** (16.1) | the lazer hum's LOUDNESS at the smallest (20 mm) / biggest (300 mm) lazer. **Since 21 the engine's volume is the loudness / the BIGGER of the two** (the biggest lazer at 1, the smallest at 0.58); 20 and before: `min(loudness, 1)` = 1 at every size. Equal numbers = the same volume at every size (V70) |
| MuzzlePitchSmall / MuzzlePitchBig | **255** (21; 16.1 to 20: 315) **/ 180** | the lazer hum's pitch at the smallest / biggest lazer, a straight line between; held to 1 .. 255 (the engine's ceiling is 255: 315 made every lazer up to 144 mm sound the same) |
| MuzzleSoundLevel / MuzzleDbPerDoubling | **75 / 6** [GUESS] | the lazer hum's range in dB: 75 plus 6 per doubling of the loudness above 1 (20 mm 77.9 dB, 300 mm 82.6 dB); held to 20 .. 140. 0 per doubling = always 75 |
| MassMin / MassMax | **60 / 9000** (13.4, your server; was 40 / 2300) | lazer mass at 20 mm and at 300 mm; `MassExponent` 2 bends the curve between |
| (removed) | - | GenOverloadTolerance, GenOverloadSeconds, GenOverloadRepeat, GenOverloadBreakOrder |

Set it equal to `PPCCaliberMin` and the Small knobs describe the smallest PPC again (then re-tune them) |

See THE CATCH in 16.6 (damage falls with the square of the armour); use the Heat Checker and `lz_toy blast` to tune it |

A 50 mm PPC is 355 kg (222 in 16.7, 163 in 16.6); 100 mm 969 (845); 300 mm 9,000. Its heat limit and its shot heat follow the mass. About 225 would double the 16.6 50 mm PPC exactly (V28) |

ACF's blast radius grows with the filler ^ 0.33, so 0.1224 is HALF the radius (0.1224 ^ 0.33 = 0.5) and 1 gives the old blast back; 0.25 is 0.63 of it. The shot's energy cost does not change |

It is applied after ACF chose the targets, so it never changes the radius; the radius knob is `PPCBlastScalar`. 640 = 8 x 20 x 4: the x4 makes up for the weaker blast of the smaller yield (the damage at the same distance falls to about a quarter). The heat follows it unless `PPCHeatPerDamage` is moved the other way (it is) **(20: used for PEOPLE and when `PPCOwnDamage` is off; props take the own model)** |

Raise both to make every part take more; the ratio of the two ends sets how much harder big PPCs hit (V66) |

Numbers at a glance (healthy hub): 3500 hp = 18,200 kJ/s (60 unit capacitor 203,575 kJ in 11.19 s); 350 hp = 1,820 kJ/s (1 min 51 s); 50 hp = 260 kJ/s (13 min 3 s). At 0% health halve each.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------
## 6. Decisions on record (do not re-ask)

- K2: kept. K3: plain numbers for energy eating (now shaped by LazerDrawCurve). K4: yes, drained capacitors accept dumps; capacitors cannot dump with no room. **Shelved idea:** dump with no room could be allowed if it makes a lot of heat (when heat exists).
- Dump input is a kJ/s speed (not tied to charge rate). AllowCharge is the only capacitor switch. Opacity/alpha channel gone from lazers.
- Generator: slider is rated hp; scale by hp via knots; engines link directly (gearbox style); ropes tied to ACF's `acf_mobilityropelinks`; any tool links any pair; second generator type (throttle engines from contraption speed/acceleration, for mechs) comes LAST.
- **13:** the rating is a limit, not a breaking point (G4, the break order, is retired). Damage scales energy only, not the load on the engine.
- **13.1:** generation is doubled because heat, not the capacitors, should be what limits how often you can fire. Your server config values are the defaults.
- **13.4:** the five values in your uploaded server config are the defaults (the file is the source of truth for the server; I diff it each time you upload). `LazerDrawCurve` 0.75 settles G11.
- **13.5:** the biggest capacitor is 60 units across (G15 settled). `PenetrationPerMM` 0.3: ACF's penetration should hover around a third of the caliber, like the pass line. The stuff behind a burned-through plate takes the first plate's multiplier times 1/4 (not its own farther distance's). "damagemult = 1" settles G16.
- **13.2:** capacitor density 0.3 and the biggest lazer at 40000 kJ/s are your own server tuning; they are now the defaults too.
- **15 (your Oct 24 03:43 spec and 04:27 answers):** (1) every entity can hold heat, heat capacity = mass, most things sit at room temperature and heat changes only when told; (2) lazer heat = 1x its draw; capacitor heat = 1/3 of the kJ moved, each direction; generator heat = energy made x scalar (1); lazer hits add heat = the lazer's heat rate x a hit multiplier (small/big knobs); (3) a beam passes a plate only when the plate is HOT (>= 100% of the HOT value) AND the old pass rules allow it; cold thin plates block until hot (accepted); destroyed plates still pass; (4) capacitor critical heat = half the HOT excess (knob), damage quadratic in how far over; lazer damage over its cap replaces my old 95% idea, also quadratic, no "damage type" knob; "I want damage to scale along side heat"; (5) death by heat: while firing, a coin flip (0.5) makes an explosion of 2 x draw kJ via `ACF.HEPower` (not literal kg), else the entity just dies; (6) lock-out: marked at 90% (one var), the beam keeps going while Fire is held, cooling mode ("unavailable") when firing stops until 25% (small and big vars), stopping under 90% = refire whenever the power is stored, restart by itself if Fire is still held; (7) natural cooling: all props the same, faster when hotter, lazers and other ACF entities have their own multiplier; (8) outputs: kJ, Fahrenheit and the percent; popup shows Fahrenheit only; a C/F client switch is deferred; (9) radiators (16): own tool, scalable, `models/lasers/heatsink.mdl` (30 tall, 42 x 42), small 12 wide / 15 kg, large 60 wide / 650 kg (scale = width / 42), radius 300 - 800 growing with size, a sap-rate knob, an overclock input, linked radiators share a pool, members from the CFW contraption + distance, rebuilt on CFW events + a 15 s refresh, the radius shown with the tool out; (10) the "cancel firing" idea stays shelved until testing; (11) "we ALWAYS go for the core issue".
- **15.1 / 15.2 (your Oct 24 05:49 message and the Docs page "Heat System: Everything We Have So Far"):** (1) lazer heat is x5 "across the board" = both ends of the lazer scalar (`HeatLazerSmall / Big` 5); capacitors and generators unchanged. (2) The radiator numbers are the Docs page's (width 12 to 60, 15 to 650 kg, radius 300 to 800, sap 300 to 25,000 kJ/s, efficiency 100% up to 50% of HOT then a line to 40%, damage 4% per second at 200%, overclock up to 6); they were not multiplied by 5. (3) Radiators only cool entities of their own contraption within the radius; linked radiators pool. (4) The temperature of plain props is shown by the radiator tool, with a Celsius checkbox that changes that readout only.
- **15.3 (your Oct 26 01:51 message):** (1) the lock-out is deleted, not switched off; safeguards are the player's own, built on `HeatPct` / `HeatF` / `HeatKJ`. (2) "Over 120% should be turbo obliteration": the old quadratic law stays and a steep extra term starts at 120% of each thing's own limit (defaults 20 %/s at 120%, power 6; 130% = dead in about 3 s); for lazers, capacitors and radiators. (3) Natural cooling x2 = `HeatCoolTau` 150 (it also halves the radiator's own settle temperature: J1). (4) Ignite at 500 F, linger 1 s, "hitboxes intersecting" = the player's collision box touches the lazer's box; lazers only by default. (5) Coolant: tank = 1/8 of the bounding box volume in litres (cubic inches), flush = half a full tank in 5 s at a constant rate, steam in the picked colour with a hissing sound, wire input `Flush`, restock exactly like a fuel tank from an ACF supply crate. (6) The coolant effects (25% strength when empty, 2260 kJ per litre vented) are my own numbers, flagged [GUESS] and one knob each.
- **15.4 (your Oct 26 05:22 message):** (1) crew = `Player:InVehicle()`, a knob (`HeatIgniteSeated`), the veto hook is not asked about seated players; "hitbox" = the ignite box, not the beam. (2) The overclock is deleted (input, knobs, state, readout, `RadDamage`); the eaten heat is `RadSapSmall / Big` x5. (3) A radiator takes no heat damage at all (turbo included); waste heat and the efficiency curve stay as knobs. (4) "1.5x" did not exist (15.3 was 2x): `HeatCoolSpeed` 1.25 on the 15.2 speed (tau back to 300). (5) "5x on both" = the sap rate x5 and the radiator's own cooling x5 (`HeatCoolMultRad`). (6) The flush takes 125,000 kJ per litre, solved from "three 12-wide, 2 s, 100 mm lazer 115% -> 25%"; a wide radiator is far stronger, on purpose. (2) The fix gives the heat readout its own clock instead of speeding up the energy read, so the energy read keeps its 0.25 s / 1 s rates and a cold lazer still costs nothing. (3) `UpdateWireOutputs` no longer writes the heat outputs (one owner, found by mutation testing: the call was redundant). (4) The lazer's hover buffer is a knob (`LazerOverlayDelay` 0.25), because ACF's 1 s buffer alone would keep the hover text a second behind. (5) The delivery is the lazer file plus two config lines, NOT my config file, because your config holds values mine does not (the five you changed; `RadFlushKJPerLiter` unknown). (6) The capacitor, generator and radiator wire outputs were not changed (they are not gated by Fire).

(2) Only the three heat OUTPUTS of a radiator are on the clock; its hover text is not (M2). (3) The flush is not recalibrated: `RadFlushKJPerLiter` stays at the shipped 125,000 (yours too). (4) The readout flag starts as "not yet shown" (lazer and radiator): the first readout writes the cold state. - **16.1 (your Oct 30 01:48 and 01:58 messages):** (1) The lazers focus themselves: toward the first hit, at `LazerFocusSpeed` 1000 units a second, inside the focal limits, holding when nothing is hit; the slider is the start. (2) The crash was the PPC passing an entity where ACF wants the crate's number; fixed in the PPC, tested through ACF's real effect code. (3) PPCs respect safezones completely: a PPC in one does no damage and no heat, protected things take no damage and no heat, the lazer's rules are the example and are now shared; people who are protected get nothing from a PPC. (4) The PPC explodes on the first thing it touches; no penetration of anything for now; LATER: pass light props and explode on the heaviest. (5) The blast does not damage the shooter's contraption but HEATS it (and everything else ACF-ish in the heat zone); later the heat is to become the way lazers and PPCs deal damage (YOU will do that by hand). (6) The ball is `sphere_3`, sized in units. (7) The capacitor says "lazer_ents". (8) The toys are a client file that prints Lua in colours and runs small visual bits; changing a number rewrites only `local key = number` lines. - **16 (your Oct 28 05:46 message):** (1) The PPC's shot is a real ACF bullet with zero acceleration and no drag; the end of its flight is one HE explosion (ACF's own), nothing else; no penetration, no kinetic damage. (2) The shot's energy is the lazer's draw doubled, ONE second of it, on its own knobs. (3) The HE filler follows the energy (`PPCBlastScalar`); the damage scalar (`PPCPropDamageScalar`) multiplies what ACF works out for each prop (so it is relative to the blast the prop received); fragments are off (`PPCFragmentScalar` 0). (4) The 20% loss at 7 s is a straight line in the flight time from 1.0 to `PPCDamageAtEnd`, over the same `PPCLifetime` that deletes the projectile; it scales damage, not radius. (5) The model scales at caliber / `PPCCaliberBase` (100, the lazer's rate, its own knob); the projectile starts at the front of the model. (6) Fire is a press (0 to above 0) and holding repeats every `PPCRefireSeconds` (1): my reading of "fires one projectile when it bites", question S1. (7) A PPC is linked like a lazer and sits in the capacitor's `Lazers` set. (8) The effects wait until the damage is confirmed (your words). (9) A new knob never stops a file loading (the 15.7 rule): all 27 have in-code defaults. (10) The PPC copies the lazer's pool code instead of sharing it, so the installed lazer is not touched: a small bandaid, on the list to fix.

(6) A full config is sent only when you ask for one (you did, Oct 27 23:53): it carries your local values, is built from the shipped file by exact line replacement, and your own file on disk is never modified.

Spin-up 8 s (smallest) to 15 s (largest), the PPC wait 1 to 3 s / 1 to 5 s, the PPC range 300 units, heat a quarter of the energy stored, critical heat about 1500 F, the charge is the blast, no energy or heat without a parent, no explosion in a safezone, the PPC cut comes from "the ppc logic": all yours. The numbers marked [GUESS] in section 5 are mine until you say.

- **17:** the Active input is a THROTTLE (0 to 1 = the intended operating percent); the engine steers to the lower of the throttle and its ceiling. At 1 it is the 16.9 engine.
- **17:** model scale and mass follow a size curve (inverse exponential, blended with the straight line by `FusionSizeCurve`, sharpened by `FusionSizeCurveRate`); the spin-up, PPC and the other pairs stay straight.
- **17:** the hum is a server-side loop that follows the operating percent and the size; the file, volume, pitch and level are knobs.
- **17:** a damaged engine makes more heat per kJ (`FusionDamagedHeatMult`, 2 at 0% health). Before 17 it made less. Whether "twice as bad" means per kJ (2) or per second (4) is open (V52).
- **17:** your five config values are carried into the delivered config only; the tree keeps the shipped numbers.
- **18:** the PPC blast has half the radius (`PPCBlastScalar` 0.1224) and hits 8 times as hard as 17's near the blast at that smaller size (`PPCPropDamageScalar` 640 = 8 x 20 x 4); the heat each hit gives stays 17's (`PPCHeatPerDamage` 3750). Whether the heat should follow the damage, and how the damage should be matched across the radius (it is matched next to the blast), are open (V56, V57).
- **19:** the PPC ball is `models/alyx/emptool_glow`; the colour is per PPC, picked in the PPC tool, cyan by default, ALWAYS alpha 255 (no opacity anywhere); the size is the 18 ball times a straight line from 0.5 at 50 mm to 3 at 300 mm (read as multiples of the old ball, V60). The explosion picture is not tinted (V61).
- **20:** the PPC blast does its own damage to props (area / 264.7 x S x F x A in multiples of the part's own base health; S 2.2 at 50 mm to 4.4 at 300 mm; F the inverse exponential, rate 3; A the armour factor, influence 0.25 = armour moves it by 25% at most); people keep the 19 model. V64, V65, V66, V67.
- **20:** the PPC's self-damage is 0.25 of the old law (the turbo part too); it is the old speed for 5 s after a shot released while over 100% (restarted by each such shot). V68.
- **20:** the fusion engine's mass is a straight line over the rated power (`FusionMassSmall` .. `FusionMassBig`); the model scale keeps the size curve at blend 0.75 (rate 8). V69.
- **21:** the generator hums with `ambient/grinder/grinderloop_01.wav`, sized by the model's size curve over its rating and scaled by its load (silent when nothing is taken). V73, V74.
- **21:** the PPC's burst is `beams/beamstart5.wav` (the crackle stays); its volume scales with the caliber (0.6 at 20 mm to 1 at 300 mm), its pitch already did. V71, V72.
- **21:** the fusion engine's hum is `ambient/machines/refrigerator.wav` (file only).
- **21:** the lazer's hum starts with `PlayEx(volume, pitch)` and `SetSoundLevel` from `GetMuzzleSound`; `MuzzlePitchSmall` 255; the volume is normalised by the bigger end so the biggest lazer is at 1 and the smaller ones are quieter. V70.
- **22:** the flamethrower's damage is the sphere's, not the ray's; the ray only ends the flight (on a part, the ground or the world) and passes through people. V77, V79 - V82.
- **22:** a flamethrower burns napalm only, from tanks that are ACF's own fuel tanks (so the tank, its model and its capacity maths are ACF's). The tool makes and updates only napalm tanks. V76, V86.
- **22:** the fusion engine's heat is static (from its charge); the old heat per kJ stored is a switch, off. `FusionDamagedHeatMult` 5. V89 - V92.
- **22.1:** the flame drag is 40 x a shell's; the fire sprites are as wide as the hit sphere, growing with it. V93, V94.
- **22.2:** the chair is never set on fire by us and takes the heat / lazer / flame / PPC damage alone; a person in a chair is spared until the chair is gone (the chair takes his place); two optional switches `SeatNoIgnite` and `SeatSparePlayer` (default ON). Your trimmed config is the baseline; the next config starts from it with a short header. V95 - V98.

- **Mutation testing of the 16.9 code (`sim/mutate169.py`):** the breakage list was generated from the new lines (every comparison flipped, every and / or swapped, every + - * / swapped, every number moved by one or set to 0, every true / false flipped, every if negated or forced, every statement line deleted; in the fusion files everything, in the config the fusion helper block and the changed PPC lines, in the PPC ammo type the new hook lines). **The first complete run: 1,489 breakages, 6 did not compile, 1,326 caught, 163 survived** (logs `sim/mut169_0.log`, `mut169_1.log`). I read the survivors, found the real gaps (the capacitor line of the hover text, the critical flag clearing again, the guards against exploding twice, the edges of the charge and efficiency helpers, the exact status and bar text, a PPC hit with no clock given, a hit exactly when the wait ends) and wrote F22. **The second complete run: 1,489 breakages, 1,361 caught, 128 survived** (`mut169b_0.log`, `mut169b_1.log`); three more of those (init.lua lines 364, 432, 455: the capacitor line not clearing, a tiny energy flow shown as a plain label, an invalid capacitor counted in the sum) were then caught by three more checks, each verified by applying the mutant by hand. **125 are left: 12 in the config, 52 in the fusion entity, 1 in shared.lua, 60 in the tool.** None of them is a behaviour a player can see; they are of these kinds: (1) the tool's looks (colours, font sizes and weights, pixel spacing: 60); (2) starting values that are overwritten before anything reads them (`Pool`, `PoolVersion`, `Refusing`, `Status`, `IsOn`, `NextOutput`, `OverlaySig`, `Charge or 0`, the area default) and bookkeeping nothing can observe (`Engines[...] = nil` in `OnRemove` and in the hook loop, `Engines[self] = true`); (3) `> 0` against `>= 0` where the 0 case adds 0 (`Heat.Add`, `AddCharge`, the stored kJ) and `parented and operating > 0` whose other half is already forced by the parent check; (4) float floors under numbers that are thousands of times bigger (`1e-6`), and clamps another clamp already absorbs (a health of 2 becomes 1 again, an operating percent of 2 becomes the full store); (5) equal edges where both sides give the same number (`heat <= start`, `operating > ceiling`, `<` against `<=` on a float edge, the 1e-6 status thresholds); (6) the overlay's change signature and the explosion position's random jitter (cosmetic); (7) `local ACF = ACF`, `Disrupted` searched as plain text (no pattern characters in it), and `if name == "Active"` (the only input). **Not re-run this time:** the older code (16.7 and before) is byte-identical except the PPC energy knob values and the ball helper, which were mutated and caught.

F1 to F13 the entity (both sizes: model, scale, mass, name, wire, network value, ACF health and armour; junk sizes; a size change keeps the links and the health share; links in both directions, refusal of non-capacitors, `UnlinkAll`, removal with and without links; energy exactly hp x 5.2 x dt in time, the `dt` cap, `dt` 0; never more than the room, a full capacitor makes nothing, `AllowCharge` off says so; water-fill over the whole group and a capacitor linked later; exploding and removed capacitors; Draw 0 / 50 / 100 / junk / wired / unwired; health 100% to 0%; heat none / 1 / 0.25 / negative / junk; the status text for every state, the hover lines, the wire outputs, the refresh gates and their granularity; dupes of a lone engine and one with capacitors; the limit; a missing model; the load guards); F14 the config helpers (the numbers, the sizes list with unusable lines, the built-in defaults of all eleven numbers, a config with no fusion knob at all); F15 the tool on the server (left-click spawns / updates / refuses by prop protection, frozen, upright and turned toward the player on a floor and on a wall with the edge cases, no global left behind, the link kit works with it); 100 mm 969 kg, 221,842, 170,818). t_heat 690 (16.7: 822): **H21 (the 16.7 limits script, 132 checks) is removed with the script**; H14 still pins that the comment-only limits file creates no variable and that the lazer file owns `sbox_maxacf_lazers`, and H22 (the scan's sort) stays. - **Mutation testing of the 16.8 code (`sim/mutate168.py`):** **1,089 deliberate breakages** of the new lines (every comparison flipped, every and / or swapped, every + - * / swapped, every number moved by one or set to 0, every true / false flipped, every if negated or forced, every statement line deleted; in the fusion files everything, in the config the fusion knobs and helper block and the changed lines), 3 did not compile. **The first pass caught 991 and left 97** (logs `sim/mut168_0.log`, `mut168_1.log`); the survivors told me what to test (the shipped knob values, the overlay refresh in `Run`, the temperature line, the granularity of the refresh gates, the built-in defaults, fill times that land on both sides of a minute / hour boundary, the exact edge of the wall rule, hp 0.11, a config with no fusion knobs) and the tests were added; a complete second run (1,089 breakages, logs `sim/mut168c_0.log`, `mut168c_1.log`) after those tests left 85. **Final: 1,001 caught, 85 survived, and none of the 85 is a gap in the tests:** every one is cosmetic, an equivalent change or doubled safety (the build stops if one does not match a category): (1) 48: the looks of the tool: colours, font sizes and weights, and the pixel spacing of the readout (a colour or a spacing of one step more or less is not a behaviour; the tests assert the fonts exist and nest big > mid > small, that every text has a colour, that every text starts on the screen and that the last line ends on it). (2) 10: the tool's own formatters on inputs they never get: the comma formatter is fed numbers only (never nil, never negative: a made-up size needs hp above 0), `string.sub(x, 0, n)` is `string.sub(x, 1, n)`, and `<` against `<=` at exactly 100 s or 3600 s needs a fill time that is that number to the last bit (the tests pin 100.5 s, 3600.5 s and 3780.4 s on both sides). (3) 1: `IsValid(info)` in the info-text refresh: the panel is made one line above and is never removed while the callback can run. (4) 9: starting values that are overwritten before anything can read them (the type is set by `ApplyData` right after `Initialize`, the pool is rebuilt on the first pass because its version is stale, `Refusing` and the hover signature are set by the first pass, and the wire outputs are refreshed by the first pass and by `ApplyData`). (5) 5: doubled safety: `GetFusionType` clamps the type again itself (so `VerifyData` is a second line of defence, and a test asserts junk becomes the default through it anyway), `GetSurfaceArea() or 0` for a physics object that gives no number, `local ACF = ACF` (a global lookup either way), and the single input name test (the entity has one input). (6) 8: float floors under numbers that are hundreds of times bigger (`1e-6` against `1e-7`), and `> 0` against `>= 0` where the 0 case does nothing either way (`Heat.Add(self, 0)` returns at once, `AddCharge` is never asked of a capacitor with no room because the room list leaves full ones out, a 0-kJ pass stores nothing), and the arguments 2 in `GetFusionRate(id, 2, 1)` that the function clamps to 1. (7) 4: the arguments of the clamp in `GetFusionRate` (a draw of 2 or a health of 2 is clamped to 1 again inside: the tests pin 0, 0.5, 1 and over 1).
- **Mutation testing of ALL the 16.7 and older code again, against the 16.8 tests and tree (`sim/mutate168f.py`, 1,342 breakages, logs `sim/mut168f_0.log`, `mut168f_1.log`):** it caught 1,297 and left 43, **the same 43 as 16.7** (2 skipped as they did not compile), and none of the 43 is a gap in the tests: every one is an equivalent or defensive change (the build stops if one does not match a category): (1) 5: the blast hook's guards: `Blast.Hits` is always a table while the hook is switched on (it returns at once when no blast is active), `Compute().Damage` is always a number of 0 or more (a 0 adds a 0-kJ entry that the heat pass skips), and a 0 or 1 kJ edge of `KJ > 0` cannot be reached with a positive damage (half a kJ is tested). (2) 8: float floors that no real number reaches (`1e-6` under a limit that is hundreds of kJ), `damage <= 0` against `< 0` (a damage of 0 gives 0 kJ either way), a list index clamped to 1 afterwards, the scan list size when it is fractional or negative (the loop does the same), and `>` against `>=` in a maximum or in the sort comparator behind an `~=` guard. (3) 15: the same kind in the heat file: `1e-6` floors under limits of hundreds of kelvin, `kJ >= 0` where the heat is 0 either way (the store forgets a part that reaches 0: a test asserts it), the scan's own `kJ > 0` test (so no 0-kJ part reaches it), a row that has heat always has a limit above 0, and `string.sub(x, 0, n)` is `string.sub(x, 1, n)` in Lua. (4) 4: the toy's table can only ask for 2% to 99% of the radius, so the floor under the sphere's area (distance 0), the cap on the distance (beyond 99%) and the +0.5 that rounds 2 / 5 / 10 / 25 / 50 / 75 / 99 are never in play. (5) 7: the radiator's note: `NoteTaken` is only called from steps that run when the pool took heat, and dt is above 0 there (RunGroup's own `dt > 0` guards sit in front of it twice), so its own `dt <= 0` guard, the `or 0` fallbacks and the `dt > 0 and ... or 0` guards are defence in depth, and a part always has a note value when its note time is the sweep's. (6) 4: the tool: its own comma formatter is only fed numbers from the net (never nil) and `string.sub(x, 0, n)` is `string.sub(x, 1, n)`; `Reading.time` is only nil when `Reading.ent` is nil too, and that case is decided by the first half of the test.
