- **Mutation testing of the 16.2 code:** **1,219 deliberate breakages of the 16.2 code** (`sim/mutate162.py`: every comparison flipped, every and / or swapped, every + - * / swapped, every number moved by one or set to 0, every true / false flipped, every if negated or forced (one-line `if x then return end` too), every statement line deleted; in the config only the PPC helper block and the `HeatIgniteClasses` line, in the PPC init every line, in the PPC tool only its new heat sentence). **The first pass caught 1,165 and left 54.** The survivors led to new tests (t_ppc Q9: odd sound picks and an empty layer that must never ask `math.random` for a number between 1 and 0, the pitch held to 20 .. 255, the burst's volume held to 1, `AddShotHeat` on its own, half a kJ of heat being heat, the readout clock knob `HeatOutputInterval` and its 0.1 s default, the firing window's exact edge, the hover text's exact percent, the dice exactly at the chance, the explosion's place, the constraints being removed, the dead PPC's last Think; the tool test with half of the heat numbers missing; letting go of Fire never fires (a guard at the top of `TryShoot` that 16.1's run had also left open); and a non-round charge in F1 so the energy bar's rounding is pinned); a re-run of the survivors caught 27 more and **left 27. All 27 are equivalent changes or defensive code, in two groups: (1) 13 are the same equivalents as in 16.1** (the `local ACF = ACF` alias, the starting values `Pool`, `PoolVersion` and `ReadyShown` that are nil or overwritten anyway, `amount <= 0` in `CanAfford`, the Shots output's `cost > 0`, the 1e-6 floor in the hover text's shots line, `if SERVER`, `v == true` in `PPCFlag`, and the edge where the heat zone's falloff is 0 either way; the energy knobs are floored at 1e-6, so a shot's cost is never exactly 0 and `cost > 0` / `amount <= 0` cannot differ); **(2) 14 are new in 16.2 and equivalent or defensive:** starting values that are overwritten before they are read or are equal to the first thing the code does (`HeatDying` false or nil, `NextHeatCheck` and `NextHeatRead` 0 or 1 because the game clock is past 1 s when a PPC is placed, `NextHeatRead = 0` or 1 in `AddShotHeat` for the same reason), boundaries where both sides give the same answer (`now < NextHeatRead` and `now < NextHeatCheck` at exact equality, `over > 0` because the damage law gives 0 at exactly 0, `MaxHealth > 0` because 0 health gives 0 damage and `> 1` because no PPC has a health of 1 or less, `heatPct > 1` for the "Overheated" text at exactly 100%), and three guards that cannot be reached: `if not WireLib` (the entity has wire outputs, so Wire is there), `math.max(limitKJ, 1e-6)` in the readout (a PPC's limit is its capacity, 29 kJ or more, times a rise of at least 1 K) and the `or 0` of the sound pick (it is clamped to 1 anyway).

`v == true` in `AutoFocusOn` and `PPCFlag`, which falls through to the same answer; the heat pass's `Zone <= 0` guard, because `GetPPCHeat` gives 0 for it anyway; `KJ > 0`, because a zero heat added changes nothing; `Life < 45`), and in the toys: the plain flag of one `string.find`, the loop-end markers `i = n + 1`, the `j <= n` boundary, the hook ids' counter, `CurTime() > untilTime` at exact equality, a `string.find` start of 0 (the same as 1), and `PrintCode` from line 0 (the same as 1); ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
## 8. Open questions

New in 13 / 13.1 / 13.2 (G10, one answered, one to confirm, G13, G14):
- **G10** On a hub smaller than its engine, `Draw` only steers the first few percent (a Turbine-Large on a 350 hp hub asks for 229 hp at Draw 5 and is at the 350 hp rating by about Draw 8). Keep Draw as the engine's load, or make Draw a share of the hub's rating so 50 always means half of the rated hp?
- **G11** (answered, 13.4): the ends are 100 and 40000 kJ/s and your server's `LazerDrawCurve` is 0.75, now the default.
- **G12** "A scalar for energy per HP": I read that as the existing `GenKJPerHP` (now 5.2), visible on the tool, plus the new damage multiplier. If you meant a second multiplier somewhere else, tell me where.

- **G13** (13.1) With your numbers every beam is cut by `BeamRangeCap` 50000 while it still does damage (FalloffAtRange 3/4 would reach 0 at 60,000 to 120,000). That is fine if the cap is what you want; if you want beams to fade out fully before the cap, either raise `BeamRangeCap` to 120000, or lower `FalloffAtRange` / `EffectiveRangeBig` so the line reaches 0 sooner.

- **G14** (13.2) With a 300 mm lazer at 40000 kJ/s and the biggest hub at 18,200 kJ/s, one 300 mm lazer can never run on generation alone (it needs 2.2 biggest hubs); only stored energy carries it (a full 60 unit capacitor: 5.1 s). Mechs with 3 or 4 lazers: is the intent that capacitor size sets the burst length and hubs only refill between fights, with heat as the real limit later? If you want sustained fire from one hub, raise `GenKJPerHP` or lower `LazerDrawBig`.

- **G15** (answered, 13.5): `CapDiameterMax` is 60.
- **G16** (answered, 13.5): "1x" = `DamageMult` 1.
- **G17** (13.5) ACF's `pen` number (caliber x `PenetrationPerMM`) does not follow the damage multiplier; only the damage area does. Far away a beam still hits with 18 mm of ACF penetration even though its pass line has shrunk. Should `pen` follow the multiplier too?
- **G18** (13.5.1) Halo and popup missing on EVERY ACF entity in his singleplayer because ACF's look-at timer is dead (see the 13.5.1 block). Needs: the fresh-session printout of v2.3 (the 'CAUGHT' section and the addon audit). Question for him: does it also happen on the server? Did a workshop addon (Portal, ULib, Wiremod, PAC3) update today, or was one added?
- **G19** (13.5) Capacitors already on the server above 60 units get squeezed to 60 the next time they are updated or pasted. If that is a problem for existing contraptions, tell me and I will change it (for example, leave existing ones alone).

New in 15 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **H1** HOT = 1,100 F with 0% = room temperature (59 F), for plates and lazers alike (`HeatHotF`, `HeatLazerCapF`).
- **H2** A capacitor's critical heat = half the HOT excess (about 580 F). Generators do nothing when hot yet. Should a hub that sits near 1,880 F (T196) lose output, or take damage, or need radiators to keep up (16)?
- **H3** (built in 15.2) Plain props show their temperature through the console command `acf_lazers_heat_info` and, since 15.2, next to the crosshair while the radiator tool is out.
- **H4** The hit multiplier is 1.0 for every lazer size (`HeatHitSmall / Big`). A 20 mm lazer cannot open a 400 kg plate (about 1,130 s). Fine, or should small lazers heat plates harder (like their 2x prop-damage boost)?
- **H5** (built in 15.2, plus: not other radiators) Radiator members = every entity with a physics object in the same contraption within the radius, except players, NPCs and radiators.
- **H6** Heat damage is credited to the lazer's owner, so inside a safe zone a lazer may never die of its own heat. Is that right (consistent with hits) or should heat always hurt?
- **H7** (OBSOLETE in 15.3: no lock-out) A lazer pushed over 90% while idle (by a beam from another lazer) goes straight to cooling mode. OK?
- **H8** (OBSOLETE in 15.3: no lock-out, cooling twice as fast, heat x5) Calibration: a 20 mm lazer levels off at 91% of its limit with natural cooling alone, so it touches the lock-out and a long burst leaves it unavailable for about 4 minutes at the defaults. Intended?

New in 22.3 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V99** If you still burn in a seat with 22.3: WHICH weapon (lazer, flamethrower, hot lazer, something else), whether the console printed a `put out <name> (burning in a vehicle)` line and what it says after "the last fire of ours:". No line while you burn means the fire is not from a fire of ours that is alive (another addon, or a fire that began after ours burned out): then tell me what else is on the server (fire addons such as vFire).
- **V100** The safety net puts out any fire on a seated person while a fire of ours is alive, also one from another addon (it cannot tell them apart). Default: yes. `SeatPutOut = false,` keeps only the rule (nothing of ours lights a seated person) and drops the net; `SeatFireGrace` (10 s) is how long after our last fire the net keeps working. Do you want it narrower or wider?
- **V101** `HeatIgniteSeated = true` used to mean "people in a vehicle burn from a hot lazer". Now it only works when `SeatNoIgnite = false,` too (your config has `HeatIgniteSeated = false`, so nothing changes for you). Default: yes, because you said "dont set players in chairs on fire".

New in 22.2 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V95** (from the 21:21 reply) Should your four flame numbers (`FlameLifetime` 1.5, `FlameSpeedSmall` 50, `FlameSpeedBig` 100, `FlameDragMult` 200) become the SHIPPED defaults in my tree? Default until you say: no, the tree keeps 3 / 80 / 150 / 40 and your numbers live only in your config.
- **V96** Which seat is it: a plain ACF seat (pod) on a prop contraption, or a seat of an LVS / simfphys / WAC / Glide vehicle? ACF types those cars as "squishy" (not as a vehicle) and their seats are separate entities; 22.2 treats any seat that ACF can damage as a chair, but I did not look at those addons. Also tell me if a person in YOUR chair is not spared (then `Player:GetVehicle()` or the ACF type is not what I assume).
- **V97** The PPC blast, the capacitor explosion, the fusion explosion and other addons' damage reach a seated person himself when he is inside the blast sphere. Default: not changed (I only stopped the chair's own hit from being handed on to him, for the PPC blast). Do you want a seated person spared from those too (the chair takes the hit in his place)?
- **V98** A chair that is protected by a safezone, or one ACF cannot damage, spares nobody (a person in it is hit as a person). Default: yes, that way a protected chair cannot be used to make a person untouchable.

New in 22.1 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V93** "10x more drag" I read as ten times the 22 value: 4 -> 40. A shot that leaves the gun level at a normal height lands in about 0.6 s whatever the drag (gravity), so it only goes from 66 m to 61 m; the drag shows on shots from higher up and on the whole 3 s reach. What distance do you want a level shot at your usual gun height to reach? I can set the drag (x400 = 35 m) and/or the muzzle speeds to hit a number.
- **V94** The fire sprites are now exactly as wide as the sphere (x1) at both ends. How it looks depends on the HL2 sprite textures and on whether GMod takes a particle's size as a width or a half width, which I cannot see from here: tell me if the visible fire is wider or narrower than the wireframe and by how much, and I change `FlameTrailSizeStart` / `FlameTrailSizeEnd`.

New in 22 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V76** The fuel rate: 0.5 L/s at 75 mm and 2 L/s at 150 mm, on a straight line, is 3.5 L/s at 225 mm (what I built, `FlameFuelBig` 3.5). If the 2 was meant for the BIGGEST one too, set `FlameFuelBig` 2 (then 150 mm would be 1.25).
- **V77** (22; answered by your 20:35 message "10x more drag", see V93) "4 x more drag than normal ACF projectiles, slowing it down till it falls to the ground": I made the drag coefficient 4 x that of a steel shell 4 calibers long (`FlameDragMult`, `FlameDragRefLength`); 22.1 made it 40 x. The 22 text claiming that drag took only 5% of the speed was wrong (it was the length of the velocity, which counts the fall): see the correction in the 22.1 block.
- **V78** Heat per hit: your "some initial value of heat that scales with the caliber" I made PERCENT OF HOT (HeatHotF 1100 F = 100%): 6% at 75 mm to 20% at 225 mm. "170% heat" is read as 170% of HOT, the heat of the touch is added BEFORE the test, and each projectile touches a part once, so at 150 mm (13%) a part needs 14 touches, about 2 s of fire at 7 a second, to be over the line (4 s at 75 mm, 1.3 s at 225 mm); then it loses 3 hp a touch, about 21 hp a second. Is that the pace you want?
- **V79** The sphere starts 16 units across at EVERY size (3 x at the end), as you said; a bigger flamethrower does not get a bigger sphere. Should it?
- **V80** A projectile touches a given thing once (not once per step): a person hit by one flame takes ONE hit; the next projectile hits again (7 a second).
- **V81** The sphere is tested against each entity's BOUNDING BOX (in its own turned space), not its exact shape. A big hollow prop or a long thin one is touched as soon as the sphere reaches its box.
- **V82** "People" are what ACF calls Squishy: players and NPCs. The projectile's ray passes through them. Vehicles from other addons (LVS, Glide) are not special: they are parts if ACF knows them, otherwise not touched.
- **V83** The flames start at (51.3, -2.33, 3.75) from the entity's ORIGIN (the model's origin; I take "center of the prop" as that), times the scale. If the model's origin is not at its middle, the muzzle point is where the knobs put it, not where the barrel is: tell me the offset and I move it (`FlameMuzzleX / Y / Z`).
- **V84** Mass 50 / 250 / 450 kg (50 to 450, a straight line), napalm 0.9 kg per litre, armour half the caliber's (like the lazer and PPC), health from the surface area: all my numbers.
- **V85** Every sound and sprite is a guess I never heard: the gun's loop `ambient/fire/fire_med_loop1.wav` (pitch 130 at 75 mm to 80 at 225 mm), the start `ambient/fire/ignite.wav`, the projectile loop `ambient/fire/fire_small_loop1.wav` (every 3rd), the trail `effects/fire_cloud1/2` (orange). Tell me what is wrong, or give me the files.
- **V86** The tool updates only NAPALM tanks (a tank of any other fuel is refused). The link controls of ALL tools of the addon now treat fuel tanks and flamethrowers as linkable (that is how the unlink-all of the heat tool reaches them).
- **V87** The tank model is assumed centred on its origin, so a new tank is placed half its height (plus 1) above the spot you aim at.
- **V88** Damage to a part goes through ACF's damage call with the heat store's usual attacker for that part (`Heat.GetAttacker`), as the heat that kills a part by itself does, so a part killed by flames is credited as the heat store credits it, not necessarily to the shooter. Do you want the shooter named instead?
- **V89** The fusion engine's static heat REPLACES the old heat (a share of the kJ it puts into the capacitors): with both on, a healthy 6500 hp engine on your 2.5 would also make 845 kJ per 0.1 s. `FusionEnergyHeatMult` 1 puts the old one back on top.
- **V90** `FusionDamagedHeatMult` is 5 now (it was 2): "5x at lowest health, scaling" as a straight line from 1 at full health. It now only multiplies the static heat.
- **V91** "Standard heat generation from max theoretical charging output" is read as `FusionHeatScalar` x the engine's FULL output (hp x GenKJPerHP kJ/s). On YOUR 2.5 that standard is 84,500 kJ/s at 6500 hp and the static heat at full charge 12,675 kJ/s; with the shipped 0.25 it is 1,268. Which standard did you mean? (It settles at 736 F on your 2.5 for a healthy 6500 hp engine; 161 F on 0.25.)
- **V92** "The charge percentage" is the engine's OWN charge (its store, `FusionChargeSeconds` 10 s of full output), not the capacitors' fill. A wrecked engine runs at no more than 50% (`FusionDamagedEfficiency`), so its charge settles at half the store and the real damaged heat is 0.375 x standard; the x5 is on the multiplier only. Is that right, or should the damaged heat be 5 x the FULL-charge heat?

New in 21 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V70** The lazer: the diagnosis is from the code (pitch 315 held to 255 up to 144 mm; volume `min(loudness, 1)` = 1 at every size), not heard. To make the volume scale it has to go DOWN for the small ones: the smallest lazer is now at 0.58 of the biggest. Right amount? (`MuzzleVolumeSmall` / `MuzzleVolumeBig`: equal numbers = the same volume at every size.) Does the pitch now slope the way you expect?
- **V71** The PPC: I read "this needs to be a sound for the ppc firing" as the BURST layer (replacing `whiteflash`), with the zap crackle still on top. If you want beamstart5 alone: `PPCFireSound2` `""`.
- **V72** The PPC's volume scales 0.6 at 20 mm up to 1 at 300 mm (50 mm 0.64, 100 mm 0.71); the pitch was already 125 down to 75; the range (100 dB) is the same for all. Do you want the range to scale too, or a bigger spread of volume (`PPCFireVolumeSmallMult`)?
- **V73** "Scaling with its HP rating" for the generator: I used the MODEL's size curve (50 hp is the small end, 350 hp is a third of the way, 3500 hp the big end). A straight line in hp would put 350 hp at 9% of the way (almost the small end). Which?
- **V74** The generator hum also follows the LOAD (hp taken / rated): silent when nothing is taken, 0.4 of the volume and 0.85 of the pitch at a trickle. If you want it to hum at its rated level whenever an engine is linked and running, say so (`GenHumLoadFloor` 1 and `GenHumPitchIdle` 1, and `GenHumMinLoad` 0 keeps it on).
- **V75** I assume the three files exist in your game and that the two hums LOOP by themselves (`grinderloop_01` and `refrigerator`; a patch plays a file once unless the file has loop points). If one stops after a few seconds or gaps, tell me which: the fix is to restart the patch when it ends.

New in 20 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V64** "Worry less about how much armour a prop has": I read it as armour counting for a quarter at most (`PPCArmourInfluence` 0.25: a part with far more armour than the blast's penetration takes 0.75 of what a bare part of the same area takes). 0 = armour ignored; 1 = ACF's law back. Which?
- **V65** "Inversely exponential" falloff: F(x) = (e^(-3x) - e^(-3)) / (1 - e^(-3)): 1 at the centre, 0 at the radius, 0.73 at 10%, 0.44 at 25%, 0.18 at half the radius, 0.06 at 75%. `PPCFalloffRate` is how sharp (6 puts F(25%) at 0.22), `PPCFalloffBlend` 0 is the straight line. Right shape, or sharper / flatter?
- **V66** The LEVEL: the damage at the centre is 2.2 times the part's base health at 50 mm to 4.4 times at 300 mm (100 mm: 2.64). I matched 19 for a 20 mm part of 3000 cm2 at 10% of the radius (about 16 points from a 100 mm shot). Away from that, thin parts take less at range and thick parts much more than in 19; a part is destroyed out to about a third of the radius. The heat is still damage x 3750, so it follows the new damage. Too hard, too soft, at which range? (`PPCBlastHealthSmall` and `PPCBlastHealthBig` are the two knobs.)
- **V67** People (ACF "Squishy") keep the 19 damage and falloff: the new model measures a PART'S OWN health. Do you want the new falloff for people too (it would need its own scale in points)?
- **V68** Self-damage: I applied the 4 times slower (`PPCHeatDamageMult` 0.25) to the WHOLE law, the turbo part included (a PPC at 130% now dies in 12 seconds, not 3). The fast rate is exactly 19's speed and lasts 5 seconds after a shot fired while the PPC's heat was already above 100%, restarted by each such shot. Is the window right (`PPCHeatOverfireSeconds`), and should "very fast" be faster than 19 (`PPCHeatOverfireMult` above 1)?
- **V69** Fusion: the mass is a straight line over the rated power (YOUR 600 kg at 100 hp to 10,000 kg at 6500 hp); the "3/4 as exponential" I read as the BLEND 0.75 on the model size (rate unchanged at 8). If you meant the rate (a softer bend), say so: `FusionSizeCurveRate` 6 and `FusionSizeCurve` 1. Which?

New in 19 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):

New in 18 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V56** The heat each blast hit gives a part is what it was in 17 at the same distance (`PPCHeatPerDamage` 3750 against the 8 times damage). If you meant the heat to follow the damage (8 times as hot, capped at `PPCHeatMaxPct` 300% of the part's hot point), put `PPCHeatPerDamage` back to 30000. Which?

A higher `PPCPropDamageScalar` matches farther out and makes the part next to the blast take that much MORE than the 8 times blast: about 730 matches at a quarter of the radius (next to the blast 1.14 times), about 960 at half the radius (1.5 times next to it), about 1900 at 80% (3 times). A very thin prop at the centre takes more than the 8 times blast at any of these. Which level: close (640), a quarter (730), half (960) or range (1900)?

New in 17 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V49** The Active input is a throttle: the number IS the intended operating percent (0.4 = 40%), over 1 counts as 1, under 0 and NaN as 0, nothing wired = `FusionActiveDefault` (1). The engine steers to the lower of the throttle and its ceiling and falls to a lowered throttle at the spin-up speed (`FusionSpinDownMult` 1). Right? Should a lowered throttle fall faster (so a player can cool an engine quicker)?
- **V50** The size curve: blend 1 (full inverse exponential), rate 8, on BOTH the model scale and the mass. At rate 8 the engine is 98% of its size by 3300 hp, so everything above 2000 hp looks nearly alike; rate 6 makes your two steps equal, rate 4 is mild. Which rate, and should the mass follow the same curve as the scale or stay on the line (mass drives heat capacity)?
- **V51** The hum: file `ambient/machines/power_transformer_loop_1.wav` [GUESS that it exists and loops], volume 0.3 to 1, pitch 125 down to 70, sound level 60 to 80 dB, silent under 1% operating. Tell me what is wrong (too quiet, too high, wrong file, should it be heard farther, or should the pitch not change with the operating percent).
- **V52** Damaged heat: the default 2 means "twice the heat per kJ", which comes out as THE SAME heat per second as a healthy engine (a wrecked engine makes half the energy). If you meant "the engine produces twice as much heat as it would healthy", it is 4 (x2.00 per second at 0% health). Which? Also: should a HOT engine (its heat efficiency below 1) make extra heat per kJ too? I did not build that: it feeds on itself (hot -> more heat -> hotter).
- **V53** With your `FusionHeatScalar` 2.5 an engine of 6500 hp explodes in about two minutes at full throttle with no cooling (it survives at 46% throttle or less), 3300 hp settles at 1,258 F (83% of critical) and the smaller ones are fine. Is that the balance you want (the big engine NEEDS cooling or a throttle), or should the scalar differ by size?
- **V54** The link kit's help lines (V34, V48) still do not name the fusion engine; they work with it. Update them (one more file)?
- **V55** PPC penetration: still nothing built, waiting for your decision after the test (V47).
- **ANSWERED by your messages:** FusionHPMin 100, FusionMaxPerPlayer 8, FusionMassSmall 600, FusionHeatScalar 2.5, FusionScaleSmall 0.3 (carried); the hum (built); the size curve and its knob (built); "does a less efficient engine make more heat" (no before 17; now yes per kJ); the throttle (built).

New in 16.9 (defaults used where you have not answered; tell me if any is wrong):
- **V36** One slider (rated hp 250 to 6500) and the mass, the model scale, the spin-up (8 s to 15 s), the PPC scalar (1 to 0.5) and the longest PPC wait (3 s to 5 s) on straight lines over it ("scale linearly unless I decide" - I did not decide anything else). Right?

- **V40** THE HEAT FINDING (see the 16.9 block): with a quarter of the stored energy as heat an engine settles by itself at 224 F (250 hp) to 562 F (6500 hp) and never gets near 1500 F, nor to ~780 F where the heat efficiency starts to fall. Engines only go critical from outside heat. Do you want that, or: more heat (`FusionHeatScalar` 0.5 / 1 / 2: the 6500 hp settles at 858 / 1,148 / 1,444 F), a lower `FusionCriticalF`, or the engine to need radiators? - **I2** (CHANGED in 15.3: with natural cooling twice as fast the 60-wide settles at 63% at x5 and 71% at x6, so nothing destroys itself with the overclock any more at the default waste: see J1) A 60-wide radiator at overclock 5 settled at 101% of HOT (negligible damage, it only dies after minutes at about x6); that is the Docs page's table. OK?
- **I3** "Constrained" = the same CFW contraption (any chain of constraints), not "directly constrained". OK?
- **I4** Radiators never cool other radiators. OK?
- **I5** Capacitor and generator heat scalars are still 1/3 and 1 (you said "for lazers"). Should they go x5 too?
- **I6** The look-at readout shows a plain part's temperature only with the RADIATOR tool out. Do you also want it with every tool (or a console variable)?
- **I7** The radiator is not spawnable from the Q menu (tool only). OK?

## 10. Assumptions and risks ([GUESS] list)

- Model front is +X and up is +Z (spawn code and the input point use this).
- Mass 400 kg and armour 40 mm at scale 1 are guesses.
- The `Draw` input detection (whether it is wired) uses the wire input's source field; the fallback treats a touched input as wired. [GUESS] on real Wiremod.
- ACF engines without a normal driveshaft (`Engine.Out`) are refused with a message.
- The load model is tuned in a simulation of ACF's engine code, not in the game. [GUESS] that a big engine held back by the cap behaves in game like it does in the simulation (revs to the limiter, steady 350 hp).
- 13: the tool panel uses `DPanel` with a custom `Paint`, `surface.CreateFont` with "Arial" and `TOOL:DrawToolScreen`. These are standard [KNOW] calls, but font sizes and text widths are [GUESS] until seen in game.
- 13: damage health fraction = `ACF.Health / ACF.MaxHealth` of the hub. [SEEN] ACF's damage code writes `Entity.ACF.Health` on every hit (`acf/damage/damage_sv.lua:244`) and the capacitor already reads the same pair, so the hub's efficiency follows hits live. Not yet seen in game.
- 13.4: your five hand edits come from the config file you uploaded on Oct 19 (second upload). [GUESS] that it is the file actually loaded on the server. Your file was my 13.1 config plus your edits, so I do not know whether the 13.2 comment changes or the 13.3 `cl_init.lua` are installed there.
- 13.5: my working copy of the lazer `init.lua` was identical to your handoff-10 upload; [GUESS] that your installed one is too. The penetration change is tested against the real file and ACF's real damage maths in my harness, never in game.
- 13.5: [KNOW] ACF's damage formula as read in `damage_result.lua` (`Meta:Compute`); [GUESS] that your older ACF-3 branch has the same one.
- 13.5: the halo cause is not known; the diagnostic is built from reading ACF's code, and it was run only against a stand-in client. [GUESS] that its printout names the failing condition.
- 13.4: [KNOW] the 50,000 kg physics-object mass limit (not tested here; the 13.5 limit of 60 units keeps capacitors well under it).
- 13.4: "1x focus multiplier" read as DamageMult = 1 (see G16).
- 13.3: my working copy of the lazer `cl_init.lua` is the one from your handoff-11 upload (identical to `w_orig11`; its old header mentioned 6.1 and 9.7). [GUESS] that your installed file is the same; if you changed it since, send it and I will merge the fix into YOUR version.
- 13.3: [KNOW] the engine counts alpha under 255 or a non-Normal render mode as translucent; [GUESS] the exact behaviour of the solid pass with a translucent model (it matches your symptom, but I cannot run GMod). `RENDERMODE_NORMAL` and `Entity:GetRenderMode` are standard [KNOW].
- 13.2: CapEnergyPerVolume 0.3 and LazerDrawBig 40000 come from your chat message, not from an uploaded file. [GUESS] that nothing else on the server changed; if it did, upload the config again and I will diff it.

- 14: [GUESS] that the missing `CONTENTS_HITBOX` in `MASK_SOLID` is why ACF's bullets hit the fresh parented props and the lazer did not. [SEEN] the masks differ and what each contains; NOT seen in game. [KNOW] `bit.band`, `MASK_SOLID`, `MASK_SHOT` exist in GMod.

## 11. Reference facts for later (user, Oct 19 22:08; in the delivered copy since 15)
- Gauss rifle model at 1x scale = a 44 mm gauss rifle. Its muzzle/emitter point is 38 units forward of the model origin at 1x.
- PPC model at 0.88 scale = a 75 mm PPC (so 1x would be about 85 mm; [GUESS] straight proportion). Its emitter point is 63 units forward of the model origin at 1x. **Built in 16:** the muzzle is the front of the model (`OBBMaxs().x`, 63.1 units at 1x, scaling with the model), the scale is caliber / `PPCCaliberBase` (100; 85 would match this note exactly: question S3).
